# Functions

This document lists all functions available in `meson.build` files.
See the [root manual document]() for
an overview of all features.

<p style="padding: 7.5px; margin: 0px;"></p>

## add_global_arguments()

Adds global arguments to the compiler command line.

<p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- A bit of space because we remove the top margin below -->
<div style="display: flex;">
  <h3 style="margin-top: 0px;">Signature</h3>
  <div style="flex-grow: 1;"></div>
  <div class="d-flex flex-column align-items-center gap-1">

</div>
</div>

<pre><code class="language-meson"># Adds global arguments to the compiler command line
[[#@void]] add_global_arguments(
  [[#@str]]                                  <b>Compiler argument</b>...,  # The compiler arguments to add

  # Keyword arguments:
  <a href="#add_global_arguments_language"><b>language</b></a> : [[#@array]][[[#@str]]]                                   <i>[required]</i>  # Specifies the language(s) that the arguments should be
  <a href="#add_global_arguments_native"><b>native</b></a>   : [[#@bool]]                                                     # A boolean specifying whether the arguments should be
)</code></pre>


```{note}
Usually you should use [[add_project_arguments]] instead, because that works even when you project is used as a subproject.
```
```{note}
You must pass always arguments individually `arg1, arg2, ...` rather than as a string `'arg1 arg2', ...`
```


  <p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- A bit more space -->

  ### Arguments



  The
  function accepts between `0` and `infinity` variadic
  arguments (`Compiler argument...`) of type <code><span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span></code>.

  The compiler arguments to add

  
  <p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- Extra space -->

  The function `add_global_arguments()`
  accepts the following keyword arguments:

  <table class="table table-bordered table-sm">
    <thead>
      <tr>
        <th class="text-center text-nowrap">Name</th>
        <th class="text-center text-nowrap">Type</th>
        <th>Description</th>
      </tr>
    </thead>
    <tbody>
      <tr id="add_global_arguments_language">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">language</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Specifies the language(s) that the arguments should be
applied to. If an array of languages is given, the arguments are added
to each of the corresponding compiler command lines. Note that there
is no way to remove an argument set in this way. If you have an
argument that is only used in a subset of targets, you have to specify
it in per-target flags.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="add_global_arguments_native">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">native</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`0.48.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@bool]]</span>
  <br><code class="language-meson text-nowrap">default = false</code>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  A boolean specifying whether the arguments should be
applied to the native or cross compilation. If `true` the arguments
will only be used for native compilations. If `false` the arguments
will only be used in cross compilations. If omitted, the flags are
added to native compilations if compiling natively and cross
compilations (only) when cross compiling.

  </td>
      </tr>
    </tbody>
  </table>

<p style="padding: 7.5px; margin: 0px;"></p>

---
<p style="padding: 7.5px; margin: 0px;"></p>

## add_global_link_arguments()

Adds global arguments to the linker command line.

Like [[add_global_arguments]] but the arguments are passed to the linker.

<p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- A bit of space because we remove the top margin below -->
<div style="display: flex;">
  <h3 style="margin-top: 0px;">Signature</h3>
  <div style="flex-grow: 1;"></div>
  <div class="d-flex flex-column align-items-center gap-1">

</div>
</div>

<pre><code class="language-meson"># Adds global arguments to the linker command line
[[#@void]] add_global_link_arguments(
  [[#@str]]                                  <b>Linker argument</b>...,  # The linker arguments to add

  # Keyword arguments:
  <a href="#add_global_link_arguments_language"><b>language</b></a> : [[#@array]][[[#@str]]]                                   <i>[required]</i>  # Specifies the language(s) that the arguments should be
  <a href="#add_global_link_arguments_native"><b>native</b></a>   : [[#@bool]]                                                     # A boolean specifying whether the arguments should be
)</code></pre>


```{note}
Usually you should use [[add_project_link_arguments]] instead, because that works even when you project is used as a subproject.
```
```{note}
You must pass always arguments individually `arg1, arg2, ...` rather than as a string `'arg1 arg2', ...`
```


  <p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- A bit more space -->

  ### Arguments



  The
  function accepts between `0` and `infinity` variadic
  arguments (`Linker argument...`) of type <code><span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span></code>.

  The linker arguments to add

  
  <p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- Extra space -->

  The function `add_global_link_arguments()`
  accepts the following keyword arguments:

  <table class="table table-bordered table-sm">
    <thead>
      <tr>
        <th class="text-center text-nowrap">Name</th>
        <th class="text-center text-nowrap">Type</th>
        <th>Description</th>
      </tr>
    </thead>
    <tbody>
      <tr id="add_global_link_arguments_language">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">language</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Specifies the language(s) that the arguments should be
applied to. If an array of languages is given, the arguments are added
to each of the corresponding compiler command lines. Note that there
is no way to remove an argument set in this way. If you have an
argument that is only used in a subset of targets, you have to specify
it in per-target flags.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="add_global_link_arguments_native">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">native</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`0.48.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@bool]]</span>
  <br><code class="language-meson text-nowrap">default = false</code>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  A boolean specifying whether the arguments should be
applied to the native or cross compilation. If `true` the arguments
will only be used for native compilations. If `false` the arguments
will only be used in cross compilations. If omitted, the flags are
added to native compilations if compiling natively and cross
compilations (only) when cross compiling.

  </td>
      </tr>
    </tbody>
  </table>

## add_languages()

Add programming languages used by the project.

This is equivalent to having
them in the `project` declaration. This function is usually used to
add languages that are only used under some conditions.

Returns `true` if all languages specified were found and `false` otherwise.

If `native` is omitted, the languages may be used for either build or host
machine, but are never required for the build machine.  (i.e. it is equivalent
to `add_languages(*langs*, native: false, required: *required*) and
add_languages(*langs*, native: true, required: false)`. This default behaviour
may change to `native: false` in a future Meson version.

<p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- A bit of space because we remove the top margin below -->
<div style="display: flex;">
  <h3 style="margin-top: 0px;">Signature</h3>
  <div style="flex-grow: 1;"></div>
  <div class="d-flex flex-column align-items-center gap-1">

</div>
</div>

<pre><code class="language-meson"># Add programming languages used by the project
[[#@bool]] add_languages(
  [[#@str]]                                  <b>Language</b>...,  # The languages to add

  # Keyword arguments:
  <a href="#add_languages_native"><b>native</b></a>   : [[#@bool]]                                                                              # If set to `true`, the language will be used to compile for the build
  <a href="#add_languages_required"><b>required</b></a> : [[#@bool]] | [[#@feature]]                                                                    # If set to `true`, Meson will halt if any of the languages
)</code></pre>

  <p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- A bit more space -->

  ### Example

```meson
project('foobar', 'c')

if compiling_for_osx
  add_languages('objc')
endif
if add_languages('cpp', required : false)
  executable('cpp-app', 'main.cpp')
endif

# More code...
```




  <p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- A bit more space -->

  ### Arguments



  The
  function accepts between `0` and `infinity` variadic
  arguments (`Language...`) of type <code><span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span></code>.

  The languages to add

  
  <p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- Extra space -->

  The function `add_languages()`
  accepts the following keyword arguments:

  <table class="table table-bordered table-sm">
    <thead>
      <tr>
        <th class="text-center text-nowrap">Name</th>
        <th class="text-center text-nowrap">Type</th>
        <th>Description</th>
      </tr>
    </thead>
    <tbody>
      <tr id="add_languages_native">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">native</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`0.54.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@bool]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  If set to `true`, the language will be used to compile for the build
machine, if `false`, for the host machine.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="add_languages_required">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">required</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@bool]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@feature]]</span>
  <br><code class="language-meson text-nowrap">default = true</code>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  If set to `true`, Meson will halt if any of the languages
specified are not found. *(since 0.47.0)* The value of a
[`feature`](Build-options.md#features) option can also be passed.

  </td>
      </tr>
    </tbody>
  </table>

<p style="padding: 7.5px; margin: 0px;"></p>

---
<p style="padding: 7.5px; margin: 0px;"></p>

## add_project_arguments()

Adds project specific arguments to the compiler command line.

This function behaves in the same way as [[add_global_arguments]] except
that the arguments are only used for the current project, they won't
be used in any other subproject.

<p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- A bit of space because we remove the top margin below -->
<div style="display: flex;">
  <h3 style="margin-top: 0px;">Signature</h3>
  <div style="flex-grow: 1;"></div>
  <div class="d-flex flex-column align-items-center gap-1">

</div>
</div>

<pre><code class="language-meson"># Adds project specific arguments to the compiler command line
[[#@void]] add_project_arguments(
  [[#@str]]                                  <b>Compiler argument</b>...,  # The compiler arguments to add

  # Keyword arguments:
  <a href="#add_project_arguments_language"><b>language</b></a> : [[#@array]][[[#@str]]]                                   <i>[required]</i>  # Specifies the language(s) that the arguments should be
  <a href="#add_project_arguments_native"><b>native</b></a>   : [[#@bool]]                                                     # A boolean specifying whether the arguments should be
)</code></pre>


```{note}
You must pass always arguments individually `arg1, arg2, ...` rather than as a string `'arg1 arg2', ...`
```


  <p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- A bit more space -->

  ### Arguments



  The
  function accepts between `0` and `infinity` variadic
  arguments (`Compiler argument...`) of type <code><span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span></code>.

  The compiler arguments to add

  
  <p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- Extra space -->

  The function `add_project_arguments()`
  accepts the following keyword arguments:

  <table class="table table-bordered table-sm">
    <thead>
      <tr>
        <th class="text-center text-nowrap">Name</th>
        <th class="text-center text-nowrap">Type</th>
        <th>Description</th>
      </tr>
    </thead>
    <tbody>
      <tr id="add_project_arguments_language">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">language</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Specifies the language(s) that the arguments should be
applied to. If an array of languages is given, the arguments are added
to each of the corresponding compiler command lines. Note that there
is no way to remove an argument set in this way. If you have an
argument that is only used in a subset of targets, you have to specify
it in per-target flags.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="add_project_arguments_native">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">native</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`0.48.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@bool]]</span>
  <br><code class="language-meson text-nowrap">default = false</code>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  A boolean specifying whether the arguments should be
applied to the native or cross compilation. If `true` the arguments
will only be used for native compilations. If `false` the arguments
will only be used in cross compilations. If omitted, the flags are
added to native compilations if compiling natively and cross
compilations (only) when cross compiling.

  </td>
      </tr>
    </tbody>
  </table>

<p style="padding: 7.5px; margin: 0px;"></p>

## add_project_dependencies()

Adds arguments to the compiler and linker command line, so that the
given set of dependencies is included in all build products for this
project.

<p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- A bit of space because we remove the top margin below -->
<div style="display: flex;">
  <h3 style="margin-top: 0px;">Signature</h3>
  <div style="flex-grow: 1;"></div>
  <div class="d-flex flex-column align-items-center gap-1">

{meson:since}`0.63.0`
</div>
</div>

<pre><code class="language-meson"># Adds arguments to the compiler and linker command line, so that the
[[#@void]] add_project_dependencies(
  [[#@dep]]                                  <b>dependencies</b>...,  # The dependencies to add; if internal dependencies are included, they must not include any built object

  # Keyword arguments:
  <a href="#add_project_dependencies_language"><b>language</b></a> : [[#@array]][[[#@str]]]                                   <i>[required]</i>  # Specifies the language(s) that the arguments should be
  <a href="#add_project_dependencies_native"><b>native</b></a>   : [[#@bool]]                                                     # A boolean specifying whether the arguments should be
)</code></pre>




  <p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- A bit more space -->

  ### Arguments



  The
  function accepts between `0` and `infinity` variadic
  arguments (`dependencies...`) of type <code><span class="text-nowrap">[[@dep]]</span></code>.

  The dependencies to add; if internal dependencies are included, they must not include any built object.

  
  <p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- Extra space -->

  The function `add_project_dependencies()`
  accepts the following keyword arguments:

  <table class="table table-bordered table-sm">
    <thead>
      <tr>
        <th class="text-center text-nowrap">Name</th>
        <th class="text-center text-nowrap">Type</th>
        <th>Description</th>
      </tr>
    </thead>
    <tbody>
      <tr id="add_project_dependencies_language">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">language</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Specifies the language(s) that the arguments should be
applied to. If an array of languages is given, the arguments are added
to each of the corresponding compiler command lines. Note that there
is no way to remove an argument set in this way. If you have an
argument that is only used in a subset of targets, you have to specify
it in per-target flags.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="add_project_dependencies_native">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">native</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`0.48.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@bool]]</span>
  <br><code class="language-meson text-nowrap">default = false</code>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  A boolean specifying whether the arguments should be
applied to the native or cross compilation. If `true` the arguments
will only be used for native compilations. If `false` the arguments
will only be used in cross compilations. If omitted, the flags are
added to native compilations if compiling natively and cross
compilations (only) when cross compiling.

  </td>
      </tr>
    </tbody>
  </table>

<p style="padding: 7.5px; margin: 0px;"></p>

## add_test_setup()

Add a custom test setup. This setup can be used to run the tests with a
custom setup, for example under Valgrind.

To use the test setup, run `meson test --setup=*name*` inside the
build dir.

Note that all these options are also available while running the
`meson test` script for running tests instead of `ninja test` or
`msbuild RUN_TESTS.vcxproj`, etc depending on the backend.

<p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- A bit of space because we remove the top margin below -->
<div style="display: flex;">
  <h3 style="margin-top: 0px;">Signature</h3>
  <div style="flex-grow: 1;"></div>
  <div class="d-flex flex-column align-items-center gap-1">

</div>
</div>

<pre><code class="language-meson"># Add a custom test setup
[[#@void]] add_test_setup(
  [[#@str]]                                  <a href="#add_test_setup_name"><b>name</b></a>,     # The name of the test setup

  # Keyword arguments:
  <a href="#add_test_setup_env"><b>env</b></a>                : [[#@env]] | [[#@array]][[[#@str]]] | [[#@dict]][[[#@str]]]                                                                                                     # environment variables to set
  <a href="#add_test_setup_exclude_suites"><b>exclude_suites</b></a>     : [[#@array]][[[#@str]]]                                                                                                                       # An array of test suites that should be excluded when using this setup
  <a href="#add_test_setup_exe_wrapper"><b>exe_wrapper</b></a>        : [[#@array]][[[#@str]] | [[#@program]]]                                                                                                             # The command or script followed by the arguments to it
  <a href="#add_test_setup_gdb"><b>gdb</b></a>                : [[#@bool]]                                                                                                                             # If `true`, the tests are also run under `gdb`
  <a href="#add_test_setup_is_default"><b>is_default</b></a>         : [[#@bool]]                                                                                                                             # Set whether this is the default test setup
  <a href="#add_test_setup_timeout_multiplier"><b>timeout_multiplier</b></a> : [[#@int]]                                                                                                                              # A number to multiply the test timeout with
)</code></pre>




  <p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- A bit more space -->

  ### Arguments


  The function `add_test_setup()` accepts the following positional arguments:

  <table class="table table-bordered table-sm">
    <thead>
      <tr>
        <th class="text-center text-nowrap">Name</th>
        <th class="text-center text-nowrap">Type</th>
        <th>Description</th>
      </tr>
    </thead>
    <tbody>
      <tr id="add_test_setup_name">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">name</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  The name of the test setup
  </td>
      </tr>
    </tbody>
  </table>

  <p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- Extra space -->


  Finally, `add_test_setup()`
  accepts the following keyword arguments:

  <table class="table table-bordered table-sm">
    <thead>
      <tr>
        <th class="text-center text-nowrap">Name</th>
        <th class="text-center text-nowrap">Type</th>
        <th>Description</th>
      </tr>
    </thead>
    <tbody>
      <tr id="add_test_setup_env">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">env</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@env]]</span> | [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>] | [[@dict]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  environment variables to set
, such as `['NAME1=value1', 'NAME2=value2']`,
or an [[@env]] object which allows more sophisticated
environment juggling. *(Since 0.52.0)* A dictionary is also accepted.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="add_test_setup_exclude_suites">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">exclude_suites</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`0.57.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  An array of test suites that should be excluded when using this setup. Suites specified in the `--suite` option to `meson test` will always run, overriding `add_test_setup` if necessary.
  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="add_test_setup_exe_wrapper">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">exe_wrapper</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@program]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  The command or script followed by the arguments to it
  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="add_test_setup_gdb">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">gdb</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@bool]]</span>
  <br><code class="language-meson text-nowrap">default = false</code>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  If `true`, the tests are also run under `gdb`
  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="add_test_setup_is_default">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">is_default</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`0.49.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@bool]]</span>
  <br><code class="language-meson text-nowrap">default = false</code>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Set whether this is the default test setup.
If `true`, the setup will be used whenever `meson test` is run
without the `--setup` option.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="add_test_setup_timeout_multiplier">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">timeout_multiplier</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@int]]</span>
  <br><code class="language-meson text-nowrap">default = 1</code>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  A number to multiply the test timeout with.
*Since 0.57* if timeout_multiplier is `<= 0` the test has infinite duration,
in previous versions of Meson the test would fail with a timeout immediately.

  </td>
      </tr>
    </tbody>
  </table>

## alias_target()

This function creates a new top-level target. Like all top-level
targets, this integrates with the selected backend. For instance, with
you can run it as `meson compile target_name`. This is a dummy target
that does not execute any command, but ensures that all dependencies
are built. Dependencies can be any build target. Since 0.60.0, this includes
[[@run_tgt]].

*Since 1.6.0* passing a [[@both_libs]] object builds both shared and
static libraries.

<p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- A bit of space because we remove the top margin below -->
<div style="display: flex;">
  <h3 style="margin-top: 0px;">Signature</h3>
  <div style="flex-grow: 1;"></div>
  <div class="d-flex flex-column align-items-center gap-1">

{meson:since}`0.52.0`
</div>
</div>

<pre><code class="language-meson"># This function creates a new top-level target
[[#@alias_tgt]] alias_target(
  [[#@str]]                                  <a href="#alias_target_target_name"><b>target_name</b></a>,     # The name of the alias target
  [[#@tgt]]                                  <b>Dep</b>...,          # The targets to depend on
)</code></pre>




  <p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- A bit more space -->

  ### Arguments


  The function `alias_target()` accepts the following positional arguments:

  <table class="table table-bordered table-sm">
    <thead>

        <th class="text-center text-nowrap">Type</th>

      </tr>
    </thead>

      <tr id="alias_target_target_name">

  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  </td>

  The name of the alias target
  </td>
      </tr>

  Additionally, the
  function accepts between `1` and `infinity` variadic
  arguments (`Dep...`) of type <code><span class="text-nowrap">[[@tgt]]</span></code>.

  The targets to depend on

## assert()

Abort with an error message if `condition` evaluates to `false`.

<p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- A bit of space because we remove the top margin below -->
<div style="display: flex;">
  <h3 style="margin-top: 0px;">Signature</h3>
  <div style="flex-grow: 1;"></div>
  <div class="d-flex flex-column align-items-center gap-1">

</div>
</div>

<pre><code class="language-meson"># Abort with an error message if `condition` evaluates to `false`
[[#@void]] assert(
  [[#@bool]]                                  <a href="#assert_condition"><b>condition</b></a>,     # Abort if this evaluates to `false`
  [[#@str]]                                   [<a href="#assert_message"><b>message</b></a>],     # The error message to print
)</code></pre>


```{note}
The `message` argument is optional since 0.53.0 and defaults to print the condition statement.
```


  <p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- A bit more space -->

  ### Arguments


  The function `assert()` accepts the following positional arguments:

  <table class="table table-bordered table-sm">
    <thead>
      <tr>
        <th class="text-center text-nowrap">Name</th>
        <th class="text-center text-nowrap">Type</th>
        <th>Description</th>
      </tr>
    </thead>
    <tbody>
      <tr id="assert_condition">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">condition</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@bool]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Abort if this evaluates to `false`
  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="assert_message">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">message</code>
  <br>{meson:optional}` `
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  The error message to print.
  </td>
      </tr>
    </tbody>
  </table>

## benchmark()

Creates a benchmark item that will be run when the benchmark target is
run. The behavior of this function is identical to [[test]]
except for:

* benchmark() has no `is_parallel` keyword because benchmarks are not run in parallel
* benchmark() does not automatically add the `MALLOC_PERTURB_` environment variable

Defined benchmarks can be run in a backend-agnostic way by calling
`meson test --benchmark` inside the build dir, or by using backend-specific
commands, such as `ninja benchmark` or `msbuild RUN_TESTS.vcxproj`.

<p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- A bit of space because we remove the top margin below -->
<div style="display: flex;">
  <h3 style="margin-top: 0px;">Signature</h3>
  <div style="flex-grow: 1;"></div>
  <div class="d-flex flex-column align-items-center gap-1">

</div>
</div>

<pre><code class="language-meson"># Creates a benchmark item that will be run when the benchmark target is
[[#@void]] benchmark(
  [[#@str]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        <a href="#benchmark_name"><b>name</b></a>,           # The *unique* test id
  [[#@exe]] | [[#@jar]] | [[#@program]] | [[#@file]] | [[#@custom_tgt]] | [[#@custom_idx]]                                                                                                                                                                                                       <a href="#benchmark_executable"><b>executable</b></a>,     # The program to execute

  # Keyword arguments:
  <a href="#benchmark_args"><b>args</b></a>              : [[#@array]][[[#@str]] | [[#@file]] | [[#@tgt]] | [[#@program]]]                                                                                                                                                         # Arguments to pass to the executable
  <a href="#benchmark_depends"><b>depends</b></a>           : [[#@array]][[[#@build_tgt]] | [[#@custom_tgt]] | [[#@custom_idx]] | [[#@program]]]                                                                                                                                      # specifies that this test depends on the specified
  <a href="#benchmark_env"><b>env</b></a>               : [[#@env]] | [[#@array]][[[#@str]]] | [[#@dict]][[[#@str]]]                                                                                                                                                              # environment variables to set, such as `['NAME1=value1',
  <a href="#benchmark_expected_exitcode"><b>expected_exitcode</b></a> : [[#@int]]                                                                                                                                                                                       # the test is considered passed if the
  <a href="#benchmark_expected_fail"><b>expected_fail</b></a>     : [[#@bool]]                                                                                                                                                                                      # when true the test is considered passed if the
  <a href="#benchmark_priority"><b>priority</b></a>          : [[#@int]]                                                                                                                                                                                       # specifies the priority of a test
  <a href="#benchmark_protocol"><b>protocol</b></a>          : [[#@str]]                                                                                                                                                                                       # specifies how the test results are parsed and can
  <a href="#benchmark_should_fail"><b>should_fail</b></a>       : [[#@bool]]                                                                                                                                                                                      # when true the test is considered passed if the
  <a href="#benchmark_suite"><b>suite</b></a>             : [[#@str]] | [[#@array]][[[#@str]]]                                                                                                                                                                          # `'label'` (or array of labels `['label1', 'label2']`)
  <a href="#benchmark_timeout"><b>timeout</b></a>           : [[#@int]]                                                                                                                                                                                       # the amount of seconds the test is allowed to run, a test
  <a href="#benchmark_verbose"><b>verbose</b></a>           : [[#@bool]]                                                                                                                                                                                      # if true, forces the test results to be logged as if `--verbose` was passed
  <a href="#benchmark_workdir"><b>workdir</b></a>           : [[#@str]]                                                                                                                                                                                       # absolute path that will be used as the working directory
)</code></pre>


```{note}
Prior to 0.52.0 benchmark would warn that `depends` and `priority` were unsupported, this is incorrect.
```


  <p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- A bit more space -->

  ### Arguments


  The function `benchmark()` accepts the following positional arguments:

  <table class="table table-bordered table-sm">

  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  </td>

  The *unique* test id
  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="benchmark_executable">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">executable</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@exe]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@jar]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@program]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@file]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@custom_tgt]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@custom_idx]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  The program to execute. *(Since 1.4.0)* A CustomTarget is also accepted.

  </td>
      </tr>
    </tbody>

  <p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- Extra space -->


  Finally, `benchmark()`
  accepts the following keyword arguments:

  <table class="table table-bordered table-sm">
    <thead>
      <tr>
        <th class="text-center text-nowrap">Name</th>
        <th class="text-center text-nowrap">Type</th>
        <th>Description</th>
      </tr>
    </thead>
    <tbody>
      <tr id="benchmark_args">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">args</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@file]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@tgt]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@program]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Arguments to pass to the executable
  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="benchmark_depends">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">depends</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`0.46.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@build_tgt]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@custom_tgt]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@custom_idx]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@program]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  specifies that this test depends on the specified
target(s), even though it does not take any of them as a command
line argument. This is meant for cases where test finds those
targets internally, e.g. plugins or globbing. Those targets are built
before test is executed even if they have `build_by_default : false`.
`program` is supported *since 1.11.2*.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="benchmark_env">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">env</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@env]]</span> | [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>] | [[@dict]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  environment variables to set, such as `['NAME1=value1',
'NAME2=value2']`, or an [[@env]] object which allows more sophisticated
environment juggling. *(Since 0.52.0)* A dictionary is also accepted.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="benchmark_expected_exitcode">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">expected_exitcode</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`1.11.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@int]]</span>
  <br><code class="language-meson text-nowrap">default = 0</code>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  the test is considered passed if the
executable returns the specified returncode,
only has effect when protocol is set to exitcode

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="benchmark_expected_fail">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">expected_fail</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`1.11.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@bool]]</span>
  <br><code class="language-meson text-nowrap">default = false</code>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  when true the test is considered passed if the
executable returns a non-zero return value (i.e. reports an error)

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="benchmark_priority">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">priority</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`0.52.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@int]]</span>
  <br><code class="language-meson text-nowrap">default = 0</code>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  specifies the priority of a test. Tests with a
higher priority are *started* before tests with a lower priority.
The starting order of tests with identical priorities is
implementation-defined. The default priority is 0, negative numbers are
permitted.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="benchmark_protocol">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">protocol</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`0.50.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  <br><code class="language-meson text-nowrap">default = 'exitcode'</code>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  specifies how the test results are parsed and can
be one of `exitcode`, `tap`, or `gtest`. For more information about test
harness protocol read [Unit Tests](Unit-tests.md). The following values are
accepted:

- `exitcode`: the executable's exit code is used by the test harness
  to record the outcome of the test).
- `tap`: [Test Anything Protocol](https://www.testanything.org/).
- `gtest` *(since 0.55.0)*: for Google Tests.
- `rust` *(since 0.56.0)*: for native rust tests

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="benchmark_should_fail">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">should_fail</code>
  <br>{meson:deprecated}`1.11.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@bool]]</span>
  <br><code class="language-meson text-nowrap">default = false</code>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  when true the test is considered passed if the
executable returns a non-zero return value (i.e. reports an error)

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="benchmark_suite">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">suite</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  `'label'` (or array of labels `['label1', 'label2']`)
attached to this test. The suite name is qualified by a (sub)project
name resulting in `(sub)project_name:label`. In the case of an array
of strings, the suite names will be `(sub)project_name:label1`,
`(sub)project_name:label2`, etc.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="benchmark_timeout">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">timeout</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@int]]</span>
  <br><code class="language-meson text-nowrap">default = 30</code>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  the amount of seconds the test is allowed to run, a test
that exceeds its time limit is always considered failed, defaults to
30 seconds. *Since 0.57* if timeout is `<= 0` the test has infinite duration,
in previous versions of Meson the test would fail with a timeout immediately.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="benchmark_verbose">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">verbose</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`0.62.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@bool]]</span>
  <br><code class="language-meson text-nowrap">default = false</code>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  if true, forces the test results to be logged as if `--verbose` was passed
to `meson test`.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="benchmark_workdir">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">workdir</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  absolute path that will be used as the working directory
for the test

  </td>
      </tr>
    </tbody>
  </table>

## both_libraries()

Builds both a static and shared library with the given sources.
Positional and keyword arguments are otherwise the same as for
[[library]]. Source files will be compiled only once and
object files will be reused to build both shared and static libraries,
unless `b_staticpic` user option or `pic` argument are set to false in
which case sources will be compiled twice.

<p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- A bit of space because we remove the top margin below -->
<div style="display: flex;">
  <h3 style="margin-top: 0px;">Signature</h3>
  <div style="flex-grow: 1;"></div>
  <div class="d-flex flex-column align-items-center gap-1">

{meson:since}`0.46.0`
</div>
</div>

<pre><code class="language-meson"># Builds both a static and shared library with the given sources
[[#@both_libs]] both_libraries(
  [[#@str]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                        <a href="#both_libraries_target_name"><b>target_name</b></a>,     # The *unique* name of the build target
  [[#@str]] | [[#@file]] | [[#@custom_tgt]] | [[#@custom_idx]] | [[#@generated_list]]                                                                                                                                                                      <b>source</b>...,       # Input source to compile

  # Keyword arguments:
  <a href="#both_libraries__lang__args"><b>&lt;lang&gt;_args</b></a>                  : [[#@array]][[[#@str]] | [[#@file]]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             # compiler flags to use for the given language; eg: `cpp_args` for C++
  <a href="#both_libraries__lang__pch"><b>&lt;lang&gt;_pch</b></a>                   : [[#@str]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           # precompiled header file to use for the given language
  <a href="#both_libraries__lang__shared_args"><b>&lt;lang&gt;_shared_args</b></a>           : [[#@array]][[[#@str]]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    # Arguments that are only passed to a shared library
  <a href="#both_libraries__lang__static_args"><b>&lt;lang&gt;_static_args</b></a>           : [[#@array]][[[#@str]]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    # Arguments that are only passed to a static library
  <a href="#both_libraries_build_by_default"><b>build_by_default</b></a>             : [[#@bool]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          # Causes, when set to `true`, to have this target be built by default
  <a href="#both_libraries_build_rpath"><b>build_rpath</b></a>                  : [[#@str]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           # A string to add to target's rpath definition in the build dir,
  <a href="#both_libraries_build_subdir"><b>build_subdir</b></a>                 : [[#@str]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           # Places the build results in a subdirectory of the given name rather than directly into the build directory
  <a href="#both_libraries_d_debug"><b>d_debug</b></a>                      : [[#@array]][[[#@str]]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    # The [D version identifiers](https://dlang
  <a href="#both_libraries_d_import_dirs"><b>d_import_dirs</b></a>                : [[#@array]][[[#@inc]] | [[#@str]]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              # the directories to add to the string search path (i
  <a href="#both_libraries_d_module_versions"><b>d_module_versions</b></a>            : [[#@array]][[[#@str]] | [[#@int]]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              # List of module version identifiers set when compiling D sources
  <a href="#both_libraries_d_unittest"><b>d_unittest</b></a>                   : [[#@bool]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          # When set to true, the D modules are compiled in debug mode
  <a href="#both_libraries_darwin_versions"><b>darwin_versions</b></a>              : [[#@str]] | [[#@int]] | [[#@array]][[[#@str]]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        # Defines the `compatibility version` and `current version` for the dylib on macOS
  <a href="#both_libraries_dependencies"><b>dependencies</b></a>                 : [[#@array]][[[#@dep]]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    # one or more dependency objects
  <a href="#both_libraries_extra_files"><b>extra_files</b></a>                  : [[#@str]] | [[#@file]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    # Not used for the build itself but are shown as source files in IDEs
  <a href="#both_libraries_gnu_symbol_visibility"><b>gnu_symbol_visibility</b></a>        : [[#@str]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           # Specifies how symbols should be exported, see
  <a href="#both_libraries_gui_app"><b>gui_app</b></a>                      : [[#@bool]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          # When set to true flags this target as a GUI application
  <a href="#both_libraries_implicit_include_directories"><b>implicit_include_directories</b></a> : [[#@bool]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          # Controls whether Meson adds the current source and build directories to the include path
  <a href="#both_libraries_include_directories"><b>include_directories</b></a>          : [[#@array]][[[#@inc]] | [[#@str]]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              # one or more objects created with the [[include_directories]] function, or
  <a href="#both_libraries_install"><b>install</b></a>                      : [[#@bool]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          # When set to true, all outputs of this build_target will be installed
  <a href="#both_libraries_install_dir"><b>install_dir</b></a>                  : [[#@array]][[[#@str]] | [[#@bool]]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             # override install directory for outputs of this build target
  <a href="#both_libraries_install_mode"><b>install_mode</b></a>                 : [[#@array]][[[#@str]] | [[#@int]]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              # Specify the file mode in symbolic format
  <a href="#both_libraries_install_rpath"><b>install_rpath</b></a>                : [[#@str]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           # A string to set the target's rpath to after install
  <a href="#both_libraries_install_tag"><b>install_tag</b></a>                  : [[#@str]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           # A string used by the `meson install --tags` command
  <a href="#both_libraries_install_vala_gir"><b>install_vala_gir</b></a>             : [[#@str]] | [[#@bool]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    # If set to a string value, the vala generated gir library will be
  <a href="#both_libraries_install_vala_header"><b>install_vala_header</b></a>          : [[#@str]] | [[#@bool]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    # If set to a string value, the vala generated header will be installed to
  <a href="#both_libraries_install_vala_vapi"><b>install_vala_vapi</b></a>            : [[#@str]] | [[#@bool]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    # If set to a string value, the vala generated vapi library will be
  <a href="#both_libraries_link_args"><b>link_args</b></a>                    : [[#@array]][[[#@str]]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    # Flags to use during linking
  <a href="#both_libraries_link_depends"><b>link_depends</b></a>                 : [[#@str]] | [[#@file]] | [[#@custom_tgt]] | [[#@custom_idx]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          # Strings, files, or custom targets the link step depends on
  <a href="#both_libraries_link_early_args"><b>link_early_args</b></a>              : [[#@array]][[[#@str]]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    # Flags to use during linking
  <a href="#both_libraries_link_language"><b>link_language</b></a>                : [[#@str]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           # Makes the linker for this target be for the specified language
  <a href="#both_libraries_link_whole"><b>link_whole</b></a>                   : [[#@array]][[[#@lib]] | [[#@custom_tgt]] | [[#@custom_idx]]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          # Links all contents of the given static libraries whether they are used or
  <a href="#both_libraries_link_with"><b>link_with</b></a>                    : [[#@array]][[[#@lib]] | [[#@custom_tgt]] | [[#@custom_idx]]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          # One or more shared or static libraries
  <a href="#both_libraries_name_prefix"><b>name_prefix</b></a>                  : [[#@str]] | [[#@array]][[[#@void]]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             # The string that will be used as the prefix for the
  <a href="#both_libraries_name_suffix"><b>name_suffix</b></a>                  : [[#@str]] | [[#@array]][[[#@void]]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             # The string that will be used as the extension for the
  <a href="#both_libraries_native"><b>native</b></a>                       : [[#@bool]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          # Controls whether the target is compiled for the build or host machines
  <a href="#both_libraries_objects"><b>objects</b></a>                      : [[#@array]][[[#@extracted_obj]] | [[#@file]] | [[#@str]]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             # List of object files that should be linked in this target
  <a href="#both_libraries_override_options"><b>override_options</b></a>             : [[#@array]][[[#@str]]] | [[#@dict]][[[#@str]] | [[#@bool]] | [[#@int]] | [[#@array]][[[#@str]]]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                # takes an array of strings in the same format as `project`'s `default_options`
  <a href="#both_libraries_pic"><b>pic</b></a>                          : [[#@bool]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          # Builds the library as positional independent code
  <a href="#both_libraries_prelink"><b>prelink</b></a>                      : [[#@bool]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          # If `true` the object files in the target will be prelinked,
  <a href="#both_libraries_rust_abi"><b>rust_abi</b></a>                     : [[#@str]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           # Set the specific ABI to compile (when compiling rust)
  <a href="#both_libraries_rust_crate_type"><b>rust_crate_type</b></a>              : [[#@str]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           # Set the specific type of rust crate to compile (when compiling rust)
  <a href="#both_libraries_rust_dependency_map"><b>rust_dependency_map</b></a>          : [[#@dict]][[[#@str]]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     # On rust targets this allows giving custom names to the crates that are
  <a href="#both_libraries_shortname"><b>shortname</b></a>                    : [[#@str]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           # A string specifying a DLL name fitting to 8
  <a href="#both_libraries_sources"><b>sources</b></a>                      : [[#@str]] | [[#@file]] | [[#@custom_tgt]] | [[#@custom_idx]] | [[#@generated_list]] | [[#@structured_src]]                                                                                                                                                                                                        # Additional source files
  <a href="#both_libraries_soversion"><b>soversion</b></a>                    : [[#@str]] | [[#@int]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     # A string or integer specifying the soversion of this shared library,
  <a href="#both_libraries_vala_gir"><b>vala_gir</b></a>                     : [[#@str]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           # If set, generates a GIR file with the given name
  <a href="#both_libraries_vala_header"><b>vala_header</b></a>                  : [[#@str]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           # On Vala targets, this provides a way to override the name of the generated
  <a href="#both_libraries_vala_shared_args"><b>vala_shared_args</b></a>             : [[#@array]][[[#@str]] | [[#@file]]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             # Arguments that are only passed to a shared library
  <a href="#both_libraries_vala_static_args"><b>vala_static_args</b></a>             : [[#@array]][[[#@str]] | [[#@file]]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             # Arguments that are only passed to a static library
  <a href="#both_libraries_vala_vapi"><b>vala_vapi</b></a>                    : [[#@str]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           # On Vala targets, this provides a way to override the name of the generated
  <a href="#both_libraries_version"><b>version</b></a>                      : [[#@str]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           # A string specifying the version of this shared library,
  <a href="#both_libraries_vs_module_defs"><b>vs_module_defs</b></a>               : [[#@str]] | [[#@file]] | [[#@custom_tgt]] | [[#@custom_idx]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          # Specify a Microsoft module definition file for controlling symbol exports,
  <a href="#both_libraries_win_subsystem"><b>win_subsystem</b></a>                : [[#@str]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           # Specifies the subsystem type to use
)</code></pre>




  <p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- A bit more space -->

  ### Arguments


  The function `both_libraries()` accepts the following positional arguments:

  <table class="table table-bordered table-sm">
    <thead>
      <tr>
        <th class="text-center text-nowrap">Name</th>
        <th class="text-center text-nowrap">Type</th>
        <th>Description</th>
      </tr>
    </thead>
    <tbody>
      <tr id="both_libraries_target_name">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">target_name</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  The *unique* name of the build target
  </td>
      </tr>
    </tbody>
  </table>

  <p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- Extra space -->

  Additionally, the
  function accepts between `0` and `infinity` variadic
  arguments (`source...`) of type <code><span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@file]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@custom_tgt]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@custom_idx]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@generated_list]]</span></code>.

  Input source to compile. The following types are supported:

- Strings relative to the current source directory
- [[@file]] objects defined in any preceding build file
- The return value of configure-time generators such as [[configure_file]]
- The return value of build-time generators such as
  [[custom_target]] or [[generator.process]]

These input files can be sources, objects, libraries, or any other
file. Meson will automatically categorize them based on the extension
and use them accordingly. For instance, sources (`.c`, `.cpp`,
`.vala`, `.rs`, etc) will be compiled and objects (`.o`, `.obj`) and
libraries (`.so`, `.dll`, etc) will be linked.

With the Ninja backend, Meson will create a build-time [order-only
dependency](https://ninja-build.org/manual.html#ref_dependencies) on
all generated input files, including unknown files. This is needed to
bootstrap the generation of the real dependencies in the
[depfile](https://ninja-build.org/manual.html#ref_headers) generated
by your compiler to determine when to rebuild sources. Ninja relies on
this dependency file for all input files, generated and non-generated.
The behavior is similar for other backends.


  
  <p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- Extra space -->

  Finally, `both_libraries()`
  accepts the following keyword arguments:

  <table class="table table-bordered table-sm">
    <thead>
      <tr>
        <th class="text-center text-nowrap">Name</th>
        <th class="text-center text-nowrap">Type</th>

  [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@file]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  compiler flags to use for the given language; eg: `cpp_args` for C++

  </td>
      </tr>

  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Arguments that are only passed to a static library

        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">build_by_default</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`0.38.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@bool]]</span>
  <br><code class="language-meson text-nowrap">default = true</code>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Causes, when set to `true`, to have this target be built by default.
This means it will be built when `meson compile` is called without any
arguments. The default value is `true` for all built target types.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="both_libraries_build_rpath">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">build_rpath</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`0.42.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  A string to add to target's rpath definition in the build dir,
but which will be removed on install

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="both_libraries_build_subdir">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">build_subdir</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`1.10.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Places the build results in a subdirectory of the given name rather than directly into the build directory. This does not affect the install directory, which uses install_dir.
This allows inserting a directory name into the build path, either when needed to use the build result while building other targets or as a way to support multiple targets with the same basename by using unique build_subdir values for each one.
To prevent collisions within the build directory, build_subdir is not allowed to match a file or directory in the source directory, nor contain '..' to refer to the parent of the build directory.
  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="both_libraries_d_debug">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">d_debug</code>
  </td>

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="both_libraries_d_import_dirs">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">d_import_dirs</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`0.62.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@inc]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  the directories to add to the string search path (i.e. `-J` switch for DMD).
Must be objects created with the [[include_directories]] function or plain
strings, which will be implicitly converted into [[@inc]] objects.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="both_libraries_d_module_versions">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">d_module_versions</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@int]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  List of module version identifiers set when compiling D sources.
  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="both_libraries_d_unittest">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">d_unittest</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@bool]]</span>
  <br><code class="language-meson text-nowrap">default = false</code>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  When set to true, the D modules are compiled in debug mode.
  </td>

      <tr id="both_libraries_darwin_versions">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">darwin_versions</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`0.48.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@int]]</span> | [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Defines the `compatibility version` and `current version` for the dylib on macOS.
If an array is specified, it must be
either zero, one, or two elements. If only one element is specified
or if it's not an array the specified value will be used for setting
both compatibility version and current version. If unspecified, the
`soversion` will be used as per the aforementioned rules.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="both_libraries_dependencies">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">dependencies</code>
  </td>

  </td>

        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">extra_files</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@file]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Not used for the build itself but are shown as source files in IDEs

These may only be static sources.

      <tr id="both_libraries_gnu_symbol_visibility">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">gnu_symbol_visibility</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`0.48.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Specifies how symbols should be exported, see
e.g [the GCC Wiki](https://gcc.gnu.org/wiki/Visibility) for more
information. This value can either be an empty string or one of
`default`, `internal`, `hidden`, `protected` or `inlineshidden`, which
is the same as `hidden` but also includes things like C++ implicit
constructors as specified in the GCC manual. Ignored on compilers that
do not support GNU visibility arguments. See also
[`snippets.symbol_visibility_header()`](Snippets-module.md#symbol_visibility_header)
method to help with defining public API.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="both_libraries_gui_app">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">gui_app</code>
  <br>{meson:deprecated}`0.56.0`
  </td>
