  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@bool]]</span>
  <br><code class="language-meson text-nowrap">default = false</code>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  When set to true flags this target as a GUI application
on platforms where this makes a difference, **deprecated** since
0.56.0, use `win_subsystem` instead.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="both_libraries_implicit_include_directories">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">implicit_include_directories</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`0.42.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@bool]]</span>
  <br><code class="language-meson text-nowrap">default = true</code>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Controls whether Meson adds the current source and build directories to the include path
  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="both_libraries_include_directories">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">include_directories</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@inc]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  one or more objects created with the [[include_directories]] function, or
*(since 0.50.0)* strings, which will be implicitly convert to [[@inc]]
objects

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="both_libraries_install">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">install</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@bool]]</span>
  <br><code class="language-meson text-nowrap">default = false</code>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  When set to true, all outputs of this build_target will be installed.
  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="both_libraries_install_dir">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">install_dir</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@bool]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  override install directory for outputs of this build target. If the value is a
relative path, it will be considered relative the `prefix` option.
For example, if you want to install plugins into a subdir, you'd use
something like this: `install_dir : get_option('libdir') / 'projectname-1.0'`.

This can be set to an array of values to control the the installation path
of build targets with multiple outputs. Currently, that means Vala. Setting
them to `true` means "use the default", and `false` means, don't install.
By default the first element is true, and all other elements are false.

*Since 1.11.0* Passing an array is deprecated, and install_vala_header,
install_vala_vapi, and install_vala_gir keyword arguments should be used
instead.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="both_libraries_install_mode">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">install_mode</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`0.47.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@int]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Specify the file mode in symbolic format
and optionally the owner/uid and group/gid for the installed files.

See the `install_mode` kwarg of [[install_data]] for more information.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="both_libraries_install_rpath">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">install_rpath</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  A string to set the target's rpath to after install
(but *not* before that). On Windows, this argument has no effect.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="both_libraries_install_tag">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">install_tag</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`0.60.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  A string used by the `meson install --tags` command
to install only a subset of the files. By default all build targets have the
tag `runtime` except for static libraries that have the `devel` tag.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="both_libraries_install_vala_gir">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">install_vala_gir</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`1.11.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@bool]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  If set to a string value, the vala generated gir library will be
installed to that path, if set to `true`, the default gir path will be
used, if `false` the gir library will not be installed.

It is an error for this to be either `true` or a string and `vala_gir` to
be unset.

The default value is `false`.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="both_libraries_install_vala_header">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">install_vala_header</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`1.11.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@bool]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  If set to a string value, the vala generated header will be installed to
that path, if set to `true`, the default include path will be used, if
`false` the header will not be installed.

The default value is `false`.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="both_libraries_install_vala_vapi">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">install_vala_vapi</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`1.11.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@bool]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  If set to a string value, the vala generated vapi library will be
installed to that path, if set to `true`, the default vapi path will be
used, if `false` the vapi library will not be installed.

The default value is `false`.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="both_libraries_link_args">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">link_args</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Flags to use during linking. You can use UNIX-style
flags here for all platforms.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="both_libraries_link_depends">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">link_depends</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@file]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@custom_tgt]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@custom_idx]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Strings, files, or custom targets the link step depends on
such as a symbol visibility map. The purpose is to
automatically trigger a re-link (but not a re-compile) of the target
when this file changes.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="both_libraries_link_early_args">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">link_early_args</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Flags to use during linking. These are just like link_args,
except that they are inserted into the linker command line
before any object files or libraries. You can use UNIX-style
flags here for all platforms. This is only supported when using
the Ninja backend.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="both_libraries_link_language">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">link_language</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`0.51.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Makes the linker for this target be for the specified language.
It is generally unnecessary to set
this, as Meson will detect the right linker to use in most cases. There are
only two cases where this is needed. One, your main function in an
executable is not in the language Meson picked, or second you want to force
a library to use only one ABI.

*(broken until 0.55.0)*

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="both_libraries_link_whole">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">link_whole</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`0.40.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@lib]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@custom_tgt]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@custom_idx]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Links all contents of the given static libraries whether they are used or
not, equivalent to the `-Wl,--whole-archive` argument flag of GCC, or the
'/WHOLEARCHIVE' MSVC linker option. This allows the linked target to
re-export symbols from all objects in the static libraries.

*(since 0.41.0)* If passed an array that array will be flattened.

*(since 0.51.0)* This argument also accepts outputs produced by
custom targets. The user must ensure that the output is a library in
the correct format.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="both_libraries_link_with">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">link_with</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@lib]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@custom_tgt]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@custom_idx]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  One or more shared or static libraries
(built by this project) that this target should be linked with. *(since 0.41.0)* If passed an
array that array will be flattened. *(since 0.51.0)* The arguments can also be custom targets.
In this case Meson will assume that merely adding the output file in the linker command
line is sufficient to make linking work. If this is not sufficient,
then the build system writer must write all other steps manually.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="both_libraries_name_prefix">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">name_prefix</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@void]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  The string that will be used as the prefix for the
target output filename by overriding the default (only used for
libraries). By default this is `lib` on all platforms and compilers,
except for MSVC shared libraries where it is omitted to follow
convention, and Cygwin shared libraries where it is `cyg`.

Set this to `[]`, or omit the keyword argument for the default behaviour.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="both_libraries_name_suffix">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">name_suffix</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@void]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  The string that will be used as the extension for the
target by overriding the default. By default on Windows this is
`exe` for executables and on other platforms it is omitted.

For shared libraries, the default value
is `dylib` on macOS, `dll` on Windows, and `so` everywhere else.
For static libraries, it is `a` everywhere. By convention MSVC
static libraries use the `lib` suffix, but we use `a` to avoid a
potential name clash with shared libraries which also generate
import libraries with a `lib` suffix.

Set this to `[]`, or omit the keyword argument for the default behaviour.

  </td>
      </tr>

- 'rust' (default): Create a "rlib" or "dylib" crate depending on the library

- 'c': Create a "cdylib" or "staticlib" crate depending on the library
  type being build.

      <tr id="both_libraries_rust_crate_type">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">rust_crate_type</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`0.42.0`
  <br>{meson:deprecated}`1.3.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Set the specific type of rust crate to compile (when compiling rust).

If the target is an [[executable]] this defaults to "bin", the only
allowed value.

If it is a [[static_library]] it defaults to "lib", and may be "lib",
"staticlib", or "rlib". If "lib" then Rustc will pick a default, "staticlib"
means a C ABI library, "rlib" means a Rust ABI.

If it is a [[shared_library]] it defaults to "lib", and may be "lib",
"dylib", "cdylib", or "proc-macro". If "lib" then Rustc will pick a
default, "cdylib" means a C ABI library, "dylib" means a Rust ABI, and
"proc-macro" is a special rust procedural macro crate.

"proc-macro" is new in 0.62.0.

*Since 1.3.0* this is deprecated and replaced by "rust_abi" keyword argument.
`proc_macro` crates are now handled by the [`rust.proc_macro()`](Rust-module.md#proc_macro)
method.

  </td>

  </td>
        <td class="text-center">

  </td>
        <td>
  
  On rust targets this allows giving custom names to the crates that are
linked into the target.  For example, passing a dependency map of
`{ 'gtk4': 'gtk' }` has the same effect of writing `gtk.package = "gtk4"`
in the `dependencies` section of a Cargo.toml file, or
`extern crate gtk4 as gtk` inside Rust code.

*Since 1.10.0*, the keys can be either crate names or target names.

  </td>

  </td>

  A string or integer specifying the soversion of this shared library,
such as `0`. On Linux and Windows this is used to set the
soversion (or equivalent) in the filename. For example, if
`soversion` is `4`, a Windows DLL will be called `foo-4.dll` and one
of the aliases of the Linux shared library would be
`libfoo.so.4`. If this is not specified, the first part of `version`
is used instead (see below). For example, if `version` is `3.6.0` and
`soversion` is not defined, it is set to `3`.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="both_libraries_vala_gir">

<p style="padding: 7.5px; margin: 0px;"></p>

---
<p style="padding: 7.5px; margin: 0px;"></p>

## build_target()

Creates a build target whose type can be set dynamically with the
`target_type` keyword argument.

`target_type` may be set to one of:

- `executable` (see [[executable]])
- `shared_library` (see [[shared_library]])
- `shared_module` (see [[shared_module]])
- `static_library` (see [[static_library]])
- `both_libraries` (see [[both_libraries]])
- `library` (see [[library]])
- `jar` (see [[jar]])*

This declaration:

```meson
executable(<arguments and keyword arguments>)
```

is equivalent to this:

```meson
build_target(<arguments and keyword arguments>, target_type : 'executable')
```

The arrays for the kwargs (such as `sources`, `objects`, and `dependencies`) are
always flattened, which means you can freely nest and add arrays while
creating the final array.

The returned object also has methods that are documented in [[@build_tgt]].

*"jar" is deprecated because it is fundamentally a different thing than the
other build_target types.

<p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- A bit of space because we remove the top margin below -->
<div style="display: flex;">
  <h3 style="margin-top: 0px;">Signature</h3>
  <div style="flex-grow: 1;"></div>
  <div class="d-flex flex-column align-items-center gap-1">

</div>
</div>

<pre><code class="language-meson"># Creates a build target whose type can be set dynamically with the
[[#@build_tgt]] build_target(
  [[#@str]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                        <a href="#build_target_target_name"><b>target_name</b></a>,     # The *unique* name of the build target
  [[#@str]] | [[#@file]] | [[#@custom_tgt]] | [[#@custom_idx]] | [[#@generated_list]]                                                                                                                                                                      <b>source</b>...,       # Input source to compile

  # Keyword arguments:
  <a href="#build_target__lang__args"><b>&lt;lang&gt;_args</b></a>                  : [[#@array]][[[#@str]] | [[#@file]]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             # compiler flags to use for the given language; eg: `cpp_args` for C++
  <a href="#build_target__lang__pch"><b>&lt;lang&gt;_pch</b></a>                   : [[#@str]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           # precompiled header file to use for the given language
  <a href="#build_target__lang__shared_args"><b>&lt;lang&gt;_shared_args</b></a>           : [[#@array]][[[#@str]]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    # Arguments that are only passed to a shared library
  <a href="#build_target__lang__static_args"><b>&lt;lang&gt;_static_args</b></a>           : [[#@array]][[[#@str]]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    # Arguments that are only passed to a static library
  <a href="#build_target_android_exe_type"><b>android_exe_type</b></a>             : [[#@str]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           # Specifies the intended target of the executable
  <a href="#build_target_build_by_default"><b>build_by_default</b></a>             : [[#@bool]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          # Causes, when set to `true`, to have this target be built by default
  <a href="#build_target_build_rpath"><b>build_rpath</b></a>                  : [[#@str]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           # A string to add to target's rpath definition in the build dir,
  <a href="#build_target_build_subdir"><b>build_subdir</b></a>                 : [[#@str]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           # Places the build results in a subdirectory of the given name rather than directly into the build directory
  <a href="#build_target_d_debug"><b>d_debug</b></a>                      : [[#@array]][[[#@str]]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    # The [D version identifiers](https://dlang
  <a href="#build_target_d_import_dirs"><b>d_import_dirs</b></a>                : [[#@array]][[[#@inc]] | [[#@str]]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              # the directories to add to the string search path (i
  <a href="#build_target_d_module_versions"><b>d_module_versions</b></a>            : [[#@array]][[[#@str]] | [[#@int]]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              # List of module version identifiers set when compiling D sources
  <a href="#build_target_d_unittest"><b>d_unittest</b></a>                   : [[#@bool]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          # When set to true, the D modules are compiled in debug mode
  <a href="#build_target_darwin_versions"><b>darwin_versions</b></a>              : [[#@str]] | [[#@int]] | [[#@array]][[[#@str]]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        # Defines the `compatibility version` and `current version` for the dylib on macOS
  <a href="#build_target_dependencies"><b>dependencies</b></a>                 : [[#@array]][[[#@dep]]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    # one or more dependency objects
  <a href="#build_target_export_dynamic"><b>export_dynamic</b></a>               : [[#@bool]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          # when set to true causes the target's symbols to be
  <a href="#build_target_extra_files"><b>extra_files</b></a>                  : [[#@str]] | [[#@file]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    # Not used for the build itself but are shown as source files in IDEs
  <a href="#build_target_gnu_symbol_visibility"><b>gnu_symbol_visibility</b></a>        : [[#@str]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           # Specifies how symbols should be exported, see
  <a href="#build_target_gui_app"><b>gui_app</b></a>                      : [[#@bool]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          # When set to true flags this target as a GUI application
  <a href="#build_target_implib"><b>implib</b></a>                       : [[#@bool]] | [[#@str]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    # When set to a string, that will be the name of a generated import library
  <a href="#build_target_implicit_include_directories"><b>implicit_include_directories</b></a> : [[#@bool]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          # Controls whether Meson adds the current source and build directories to the include path
  <a href="#build_target_include_directories"><b>include_directories</b></a>          : [[#@array]][[[#@inc]] | [[#@str]]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              # one or more objects created with the [[include_directories]] function, or
  <a href="#build_target_install"><b>install</b></a>                      : [[#@bool]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          # When set to true, all outputs of this build_target will be installed
  <a href="#build_target_install_dir"><b>install_dir</b></a>                  : [[#@array]][[[#@str]] | [[#@bool]]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             # override install directory for outputs of this build target
  <a href="#build_target_install_mode"><b>install_mode</b></a>                 : [[#@array]][[[#@str]] | [[#@int]]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              # Specify the file mode in symbolic format
  <a href="#build_target_install_rpath"><b>install_rpath</b></a>                : [[#@str]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           # A string to set the target's rpath to after install
  <a href="#build_target_install_tag"><b>install_tag</b></a>                  : [[#@str]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           # A string used by the `meson install --tags` command
  <a href="#build_target_install_vala_gir"><b>install_vala_gir</b></a>             : [[#@str]] | [[#@bool]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    # If set to a string value, the vala generated gir library will be
  <a href="#build_target_install_vala_header"><b>install_vala_header</b></a>          : [[#@str]] | [[#@bool]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    # If set to a string value, the vala generated header will be installed to
  <a href="#build_target_install_vala_vapi"><b>install_vala_vapi</b></a>            : [[#@str]] | [[#@bool]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    # If set to a string value, the vala generated vapi library will be
  <a href="#build_target_java_resources"><b>java_resources</b></a>               : [[#@structured_src]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                # Resources to be added to the jar
  <a href="#build_target_link_args"><b>link_args</b></a>                    : [[#@array]][[[#@str]]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    # Flags to use during linking
  <a href="#build_target_link_depends"><b>link_depends</b></a>                 : [[#@str]] | [[#@file]] | [[#@custom_tgt]] | [[#@custom_idx]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          # Strings, files, or custom targets the link step depends on
  <a href="#build_target_link_early_args"><b>link_early_args</b></a>              : [[#@array]][[[#@str]]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    # Flags to use during linking
  <a href="#build_target_link_language"><b>link_language</b></a>                : [[#@str]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           # Makes the linker for this target be for the specified language
  <a href="#build_target_link_whole"><b>link_whole</b></a>                   : [[#@array]][[[#@lib]] | [[#@custom_tgt]] | [[#@custom_idx]]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          # Links all contents of the given static libraries whether they are used or
  <a href="#build_target_link_with"><b>link_with</b></a>                    : [[#@array]][[[#@lib]] | [[#@custom_tgt]] | [[#@custom_idx]]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          # One or more shared or static libraries
  <a href="#build_target_main_class"><b>main_class</b></a>                   : [[#@str]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           # Main class for running the built jar
  <a href="#build_target_name_prefix"><b>name_prefix</b></a>                  : [[#@str]] | [[#@array]][[[#@void]]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             # The string that will be used as the prefix for the
  <a href="#build_target_name_suffix"><b>name_suffix</b></a>                  : [[#@str]] | [[#@array]][[[#@void]]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             # The string that will be used as the extension for the
  <a href="#build_target_native"><b>native</b></a>                       : [[#@bool]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          # Controls whether the target is compiled for the build or host machines
  <a href="#build_target_objects"><b>objects</b></a>                      : [[#@array]][[[#@extracted_obj]] | [[#@file]] | [[#@str]]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             # List of object files that should be linked in this target
  <a href="#build_target_override_options"><b>override_options</b></a>             : [[#@array]][[[#@str]]] | [[#@dict]][[[#@str]] | [[#@bool]] | [[#@int]] | [[#@array]][[[#@str]]]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                # takes an array of strings in the same format as `project`'s `default_options`
  <a href="#build_target_pic"><b>pic</b></a>                          : [[#@bool]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          # Builds the library as positional independent code
  <a href="#build_target_pie"><b>pie</b></a>                          : [[#@bool]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          # Build a position-independent executable
  <a href="#build_target_prelink"><b>prelink</b></a>                      : [[#@bool]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          # If `true` the object files in the target will be prelinked,
  <a href="#build_target_rust_abi"><b>rust_abi</b></a>                     : [[#@str]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           # Set the specific ABI to compile (when compiling rust)
  <a href="#build_target_rust_crate_type"><b>rust_crate_type</b></a>              : [[#@str]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           # Set the specific type of rust crate to compile (when compiling rust)
  <a href="#build_target_rust_dependency_map"><b>rust_dependency_map</b></a>          : [[#@dict]][[[#@str]]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     # On rust targets this allows giving custom names to the crates that are
  <a href="#build_target_shortname"><b>shortname</b></a>                    : [[#@str]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           # A string specifying a DLL name fitting to 8
  <a href="#build_target_sources"><b>sources</b></a>                      : [[#@str]] | [[#@file]] | [[#@custom_tgt]] | [[#@custom_idx]] | [[#@generated_list]] | [[#@structured_src]]                                                                                                                                                                                                        # Additional source files
  <a href="#build_target_soversion"><b>soversion</b></a>                    : [[#@str]] | [[#@int]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     # A string or integer specifying the soversion of this shared library,
  <a href="#build_target_target_type"><b>target_type</b></a>                  : [[#@str]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           # The actual target type to build
  <a href="#build_target_vala_gir"><b>vala_gir</b></a>                     : [[#@str]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           # If set, generates a GIR file with the given name
  <a href="#build_target_vala_header"><b>vala_header</b></a>                  : [[#@str]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           # On Vala targets, this provides a way to override the name of the generated
  <a href="#build_target_vala_shared_args"><b>vala_shared_args</b></a>             : [[#@array]][[[#@str]] | [[#@file]]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             # Arguments that are only passed to a shared library
  <a href="#build_target_vala_static_args"><b>vala_static_args</b></a>             : [[#@array]][[[#@str]] | [[#@file]]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             # Arguments that are only passed to a static library
  <a href="#build_target_vala_vapi"><b>vala_vapi</b></a>                    : [[#@str]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           # On Vala targets, this provides a way to override the name of the generated
  <a href="#build_target_version"><b>version</b></a>                      : [[#@str]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           # A string specifying the version of this shared library,
  <a href="#build_target_vs_module_defs"><b>vs_module_defs</b></a>               : [[#@str]] | [[#@file]] | [[#@custom_tgt]] | [[#@custom_idx]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          # Specify a Microsoft module definition file for controlling symbol exports,
  <a href="#build_target_win_subsystem"><b>win_subsystem</b></a>                : [[#@str]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           # Specifies the subsystem type to use
)</code></pre>




  <p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- A bit more space -->

  ### Arguments


  The function `build_target()` accepts the following positional arguments:

  <table class="table table-bordered table-sm">
    <thead>
      <tr>
        <th class="text-center text-nowrap">Name</th>
        <th class="text-center text-nowrap">Type</th>
        <th>Description</th>
      </tr>
    </thead>
    <tbody>
      <tr id="build_target_target_name">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">target_name</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  The *unique* name of the build target
  </td>
      </tr>
    </tbody>
  </table>

  <p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- Extra space -->

  Additionally, the
  function accepts between `0` and `infinity` variadic
  arguments (`source...`) of type <code><span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@file]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@custom_tgt]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@custom_idx]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@generated_list]]</span></code>.

  Input source to compile. The following types are supported:

- Strings relative to the current source directory
- [[@file]] objects defined in any preceding build file
- The return value of configure-time generators such as [[configure_file]]
- The return value of build-time generators such as
  [[custom_target]] or [[generator.process]]

These input files can be sources, objects, libraries, or any other
file. Meson will automatically categorize them based on the extension
and use them accordingly. For instance, sources (`.c`, `.cpp`,
`.vala`, `.rs`, etc) will be compiled and objects (`.o`, `.obj`) and
libraries (`.so`, `.dll`, etc) will be linked.

With the Ninja backend, Meson will create a build-time [order-only
dependency](https://ninja-build.org/manual.html#ref_dependencies) on
all generated input files, including unknown files. This is needed to
bootstrap the generation of the real dependencies in the
[depfile](https://ninja-build.org/manual.html#ref_headers) generated
by your compiler to determine when to rebuild sources. Ninja relies on
this dependency file for all input files, generated and non-generated.
The behavior is similar for other backends.


  
  <p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- Extra space -->

  Finally, `build_target()`
  accepts the following keyword arguments:

  <table class="table table-bordered table-sm">
    <thead>
      <tr>
        <th class="text-center text-nowrap">Name</th>
        <th class="text-center text-nowrap">Type</th>
        <th>Description</th>
      </tr>
    </thead>
    <tbody>
      <tr id="build_target__lang__args">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">&lt;lang&gt;_args</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@file]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  compiler flags to use for the given language; eg: `cpp_args` for C++

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="build_target__lang__pch">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">&lt;lang&gt;_pch</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  precompiled header file to use for the given language
  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="build_target__lang__shared_args">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">&lt;lang&gt;_shared_args</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`1.3.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Arguments that are only passed to a shared library
  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="build_target__lang__static_args">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">&lt;lang&gt;_static_args</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`1.3.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Arguments that are only passed to a static library
  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="build_target_android_exe_type">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">android_exe_type</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`1.8.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  <br><code class="language-meson text-nowrap">default = 'executable'</code>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Specifies the intended target of the executable. This can either be
`executable`, if the intended usecase is to run the executable using
fork + exec, or `application` if the executable is supposed to be
loaded as shared object by the android runtime.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="build_target_build_by_default">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">build_by_default</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`0.38.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@bool]]</span>
  <br><code class="language-meson text-nowrap">default = true</code>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Causes, when set to `true`, to have this target be built by default.
This means it will be built when `meson compile` is called without any
arguments. The default value is `true` for all built target types.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="build_target_build_rpath">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">build_rpath</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`0.42.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  A string to add to target's rpath definition in the build dir,
but which will be removed on install

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="build_target_build_subdir">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">build_subdir</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`1.10.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Places the build results in a subdirectory of the given name rather than directly into the build directory. This does not affect the install directory, which uses install_dir.
This allows inserting a directory name into the build path, either when needed to use the build result while building other targets or as a way to support multiple targets with the same basename by using unique build_subdir values for each one.
To prevent collisions within the build directory, build_subdir is not allowed to match a file or directory in the source directory, nor contain '..' to refer to the parent of the build directory.
  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="build_target_d_debug">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">d_debug</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  The [D version identifiers](https://dlang.org/spec/version.html#version) to add
during the compilation of D source files.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="build_target_d_import_dirs">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">d_import_dirs</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`0.62.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@inc]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  the directories to add to the string search path (i.e. `-J` switch for DMD).
Must be objects created with the [[include_directories]] function or plain
strings, which will be implicitly converted into [[@inc]] objects.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="build_target_d_module_versions">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">d_module_versions</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@int]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  List of module version identifiers set when compiling D sources.
  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="build_target_d_unittest">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">d_unittest</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@bool]]</span>
  <br><code class="language-meson text-nowrap">default = false</code>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  When set to true, the D modules are compiled in debug mode.
  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="build_target_darwin_versions">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">darwin_versions</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`0.48.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@int]]</span> | [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Defines the `compatibility version` and `current version` for the dylib on macOS.
If an array is specified, it must be
either zero, one, or two elements. If only one element is specified
or if it's not an array the specified value will be used for setting
both compatibility version and current version. If unspecified, the
`soversion` will be used as per the aforementioned rules.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="build_target_dependencies">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">dependencies</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@dep]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  one or more dependency objects
created with
[[dependency]] or [[compiler.find_library]]
(for external deps) or [[declare_dependency]]
(for deps built by the project)

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="build_target_export_dynamic">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">export_dynamic</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`0.45.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@bool]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  when set to true causes the target's symbols to be
dynamically exported, allowing modules built using the
[[shared_module]] function to refer to functions,
variables and other symbols defined in the executable itself.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="build_target_extra_files">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">extra_files</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@file]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Not used for the build itself but are shown as source files in IDEs
that group files by targets (such as Visual Studio).

These may only be static sources.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="build_target_gnu_symbol_visibility">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">gnu_symbol_visibility</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`0.48.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Specifies how symbols should be exported, see
e.g [the GCC Wiki](https://gcc.gnu.org/wiki/Visibility) for more
information. This value can either be an empty string or one of
`default`, `internal`, `hidden`, `protected` or `inlineshidden`, which
is the same as `hidden` but also includes things like C++ implicit
constructors as specified in the GCC manual. Ignored on compilers that
do not support GNU visibility arguments. See also
[`snippets.symbol_visibility_header()`](Snippets-module.md#symbol_visibility_header)
method to help with defining public API.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="build_target_gui_app">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">gui_app</code>
  <br>{meson:deprecated}`0.56.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@bool]]</span>
  <br><code class="language-meson text-nowrap">default = false</code>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  When set to true flags this target as a GUI application
on platforms where this makes a difference, **deprecated** since
0.56.0, use `win_subsystem` instead.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="build_target_implib">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">implib</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`0.42.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@bool]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  When set to a string, that will be the name of a generated import library.

*Since 1.10.0* passing a boolean value is deprecated.

When set to true, an import library is generated for the
executable (the name of the import library is based on *exe_name*).
Alternatively, when set to a string, that gives the base name for
the import library.  The import library is used when the returned
build target object appears in `link_with:` elsewhere.  Only has any
effect on platforms where that is meaningful (e.g. Windows). Implies
the `export_dynamic` argument.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="build_target_implicit_include_directories">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">implicit_include_directories</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`0.42.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@bool]]</span>
  <br><code class="language-meson text-nowrap">default = true</code>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Controls whether Meson adds the current source and build directories to the include path
  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="build_target_include_directories">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">include_directories</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@inc]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  one or more objects created with the [[include_directories]] function, or
*(since 0.50.0)* strings, which will be implicitly convert to [[@inc]]

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="build_target_install">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">install</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@bool]]</span>
  <br><code class="language-meson text-nowrap">default = false</code>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  When set to true, all outputs of this build_target will be installed.
  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="build_target_install_dir">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">install_dir</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@bool]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  override install directory for outputs of this build target. If the value is a
relative path, it will be considered relative the `prefix` option.
For example, if you want to install plugins into a subdir, you'd use
something like this: `install_dir : get_option('libdir') / 'projectname-1.0'`.

This can be set to an array of values to control the the installation path
of build targets with multiple outputs. Currently, that means Vala. Setting
them to `true` means "use the default", and `false` means, don't install.
By default the first element is true, and all other elements are false.

*Since 1.11.0* Passing an array is deprecated, and install_vala_header,
install_vala_vapi, and install_vala_gir keyword arguments should be used
instead.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="build_target_install_mode">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">install_mode</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`0.47.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@int]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Specify the file mode in symbolic format
and optionally the owner/uid and group/gid for the installed files.

See the `install_mode` kwarg of [[install_data]] for more information.

  </td>
      </tr>

      <tr id="build_target_link_args">

  <code class="language-meson">link_args</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>]

  </td>

      <tr id="build_target_link_depends">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">link_depends</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@file]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@custom_tgt]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@custom_idx]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>

such as a symbol visibility map. The purpose is to

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="build_target_link_early_args">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">link_early_args</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Flags to use during linking. These are just like link_args,
except that they are inserted into the linker command line
before any object files or libraries. You can use UNIX-style
flags here for all platforms. This is only supported when using
the Ninja backend.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="build_target_link_language">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">link_language</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`0.51.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Makes the linker for this target be for the specified language.
It is generally unnecessary to set
this, as Meson will detect the right linker to use in most cases. There are
only two cases where this is needed. One, your main function in an
executable is not in the language Meson picked, or second you want to force
a library to use only one ABI.

*(broken until 0.55.0)*

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="build_target_link_whole">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">link_whole</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`0.40.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@lib]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@custom_tgt]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@custom_idx]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Links all contents of the given static libraries whether they are used or
not, equivalent to the `-Wl,--whole-archive` argument flag of GCC, or the
'/WHOLEARCHIVE' MSVC linker option. This allows the linked target to
re-export symbols from all objects in the static libraries.

*(since 0.41.0)* If passed an array that array will be flattened.

*(since 0.51.0)* This argument also accepts outputs produced by
custom targets. The user must ensure that the output is a library in
the correct format.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="build_target_link_with">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">link_with</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@lib]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@custom_tgt]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@custom_idx]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  One or more shared or static libraries
(built by this project) that this target should be linked with. *(since 0.41.0)* If passed an
array that array will be flattened. *(since 0.51.0)* The arguments can also be custom targets.
In this case Meson will assume that merely adding the output file in the linker command
line is sufficient to make linking work. If this is not sufficient,
then the build system writer must write all other steps manually.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="build_target_main_class">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">main_class</code>

      <tr id="build_target_name_prefix">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">name_prefix</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@void]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  The string that will be used as the prefix for the
target output filename by overriding the default (only used for
libraries). By default this is `lib` on all platforms and compilers,
except for MSVC shared libraries where it is omitted to follow
convention, and Cygwin shared libraries where it is `cyg`.

Set this to `[]`, or omit the keyword argument for the default behaviour.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="build_target_name_suffix">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">name_suffix</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@void]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  The string that will be used as the extension for the
target by overriding the default. By default on Windows this is
`exe` for executables and on other platforms it is omitted.

For shared libraries, the default value
is `dylib` on macOS, `dll` on Windows, and `so` everywhere else.
For static libraries, it is `a` everywhere. By convention MSVC
static libraries use the `lib` suffix, but we use `a` to avoid a
potential name clash with shared libraries which also generate
import libraries with a `lib` suffix.

Set this to `[]`, or omit the keyword argument for the default behaviour.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="build_target_native">

      <tr id="build_target_rust_abi">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">rust_abi</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`1.3.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Set the specific ABI to compile (when compiling rust).
- 'rust' (default): Create a "rlib" or "dylib" crate depending on the library
  type being build.
- 'c': Create a "cdylib" or "staticlib" crate depending on the library
  type being build.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="build_target_rust_crate_type">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">rust_crate_type</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`0.42.0`
  <br>{meson:deprecated}`1.3.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Set the specific type of rust crate to compile (when compiling rust).

If the target is an [[executable]] this defaults to "bin", the only
allowed value.

If it is a [[static_library]] it defaults to "lib", and may be "lib",
"staticlib", or "rlib". If "lib" then Rustc will pick a default, "staticlib"
means a C ABI library, "rlib" means a Rust ABI.

If it is a [[shared_library]] it defaults to "lib", and may be "lib",
"dylib", "cdylib", or "proc-macro". If "lib" then Rustc will pick a
default, "cdylib" means a C ABI library, "dylib" means a Rust ABI, and
"proc-macro" is a special rust procedural macro crate.

"proc-macro" is new in 0.62.0.

*Since 1.3.0* this is deprecated and replaced by "rust_abi" keyword argument.
`proc_macro` crates are now handled by the [`rust.proc_macro()`](Rust-module.md#proc_macro)
method.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="build_target_rust_dependency_map">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">rust_dependency_map</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`1.2.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  [[@dict]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  On rust targets this allows giving custom names to the crates that are
linked into the target.  For example, passing a dependency map of
`{ 'gtk4': 'gtk' }` has the same effect of writing `gtk.package = "gtk4"`
in the `dependencies` section of a Cargo.toml file, or
`extern crate gtk4 as gtk` inside Rust code.

*Since 1.10.0*, the keys can be either crate names or target names.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="build_target_shortname">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">shortname</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`1.10.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>

  </td>

      <tr id="build_target_sources">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">sources</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@file]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@custom_tgt]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@custom_idx]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@generated_list]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@structured_src]]</span>

        <td>

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="build_target_soversion">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">soversion</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@int]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  A string or integer specifying the soversion of this shared library,
such as `0`. On Linux and Windows this is used to set the
soversion (or equivalent) in the filename. For example, if
`soversion` is `4`, a Windows DLL will be called `foo-4.dll` and one
of the aliases of the Linux shared library would be
`libfoo.so.4`. If this is not specified, the first part of `version`
is used instead (see below). For example, if `version` is `3.6.0` and
`soversion` is not defined, it is set to `3`.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="build_target_target_type">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">

      <tr id="build_target_version">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">version</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  A string specifying the version of this shared library,
such as `1.1.0`. On Linux and OS X, this is used to set the shared

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="build_target_vs_module_defs">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">vs_module_defs</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`1.3.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@file]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@custom_tgt]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@custom_idx]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Specify a Microsoft module definition file for controlling symbol exports,
etc., on platforms where that is possible (e.g. Windows).

This can be used to expose which functions a shared_module loaded by an
executable will be allowed to use.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="build_target_win_subsystem">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">win_subsystem</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`0.56.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  <br><code class="language-meson text-nowrap">default = 'console'</code>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Specifies the subsystem type to use
on the Windows platform. Typical values include `console` for text
mode programs and `windows` for gui apps. The value can also contain
version specification such as `windows,6.0`. See [MSDN
documentation](https://docs.microsoft.com/en-us/cpp/build/reference/subsystem-specify-subsystem)
for the full list.

  </td>
      </tr>
    </tbody>
  </table>

## configuration_data()

finally use it in a call to [[configure_file]].

## configure_file()

This function can run in three modes depending on the keyword arguments
passed to it.

When a [[@cfg_data]] object is passed
to the `configuration:` keyword argument, it takes a template file as
the `input:` (optional) and produces the `output:` (required) by
substituting values from the configuration data as detailed in [the
configuration file documentation](Configuration.md). *(since 0.49.0)*
A dictionary can be passed instead of a
[[@cfg_data]] object.

When an array of strings is passed to the `command:` keyword argument,
it takes any source or configured file as the `input:` and assumes
that the `output:` is produced when the specified command is run.

You can install the outputted file with the `install_dir:` kwarg, see below.

*(since 0.47.0)* When the `copy:` keyword argument is set to `true`,
this function will copy the file provided in `input:` to a file in the
current build directory with the name provided in `output:`.
The copy happens at configuration.  If the input file is subsequently
modified, Meson will regenerate the build files.
To perform the copy at build time (with the usual dependency on the source
file) use `copyfile()` from the FS (filesystem) module.

<p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- A bit of space because we remove the top margin below -->
<div style="display: flex;">
  <h3 style="margin-top: 0px;">Signature</h3>
  <div style="flex-grow: 1;"></div>
  <div class="d-flex flex-column align-items-center gap-1">

</div>
</div>

<pre><code class="language-meson"># This function can run in three modes depending on the keyword arguments
[[#@file]] configure_file(
  <a href="#configure_file_build_subdir"><b>build_subdir</b></a>  : [[#@str]]                                                                                                                                                                    # Places the build results in a subdirectory of the given name rather than directly into the build directory
  <a href="#configure_file_capture"><b>capture</b></a>       : [[#@bool]]                                                                                                                                                                   # When this argument is set to true,
  <a href="#configure_file_command"><b>command</b></a>       : [[#@array]][[[#@str]] | [[#@file]]]                                                                                                                                                      # As explained above, if specified, Meson does not create
  <a href="#configure_file_configuration"><b>configuration</b></a> : [[#@cfg_data]] | [[#@dict]][[[#@str]] | [[#@int]] | [[#@bool]]]                                                                                                                                      # As explained above, when passed this will provide the replacement
  <a href="#configure_file_copy"><b>copy</b></a>          : [[#@bool]]                                                                                                                                                                   # As explained above, if specified Meson only
  <a href="#configure_file_depfile"><b>depfile</b></a>       : [[#@str]]                                                                                                                                                                    # A dependency file that the command can write listing
  <a href="#configure_file_encoding"><b>encoding</b></a>      : [[#@str]]                                                                                                                                                                    # Set the file encoding for the input and output file

)</code></pre>
