## custom_target()

Create a custom top level build target. The only positional argument
is the name of this target and cannot contain path separators (`/` or `\`).
The name of custom target might not be used by every backends, for instance with
the Ninja backend, `subdir/meson.build` containing the example below,
`ninja -C builddir foo` or `ninja -C builddir subdir/foo` won't work,
it is instead `ninja -C builddir subdir/file.txt`. However, `meson compile subdir/foo`
is accepted.
```meson
custom_target('foo', output: 'file.txt', ...)
```

The files passed to `output:` cannot contain path separators. See the 
[manual on custom build targets](Custom-build-targets.md#details-on-command-invocation) for
an explanation on where output files should be placed.

You can install the outputted files with the `install_dir:` kwarg, see below.

*Since 0.60.0* the name argument is optional and defaults to the basename of the first
output (`file.txt` in the example above).

The array of strings passed to the `command` keyword argument accept
the following special string substitutions:

- `@INPUT@`: the full path to the input passed to `input`. If more than
  one input is specified, all of them will be substituted as separate
  arguments only if the command uses `'@INPUT@'` as a
  standalone-argument. For instance, this would not work: `command :
  ['cp', './@INPUT@']`, but this would: `command : ['cp', '@INPUT@']`.
- `@OUTPUT@`: the full path to the output passed to `output`. If more
  than one outputs are specified, the behavior is the same as
  `@INPUT@`.
- `@INPUT0@` `@INPUT1@` `...`: the full path to the input with the specified array index in `input`
- `@OUTPUT0@` `@OUTPUT1@` `...`: the full path to the output with the specified array index in `output`
- `@OUTDIR@`: the full path to the directory where the output(s) must be written
- `@DEPFILE@`: the full path to the dependency file passed to `depfile`
- `@PLAINNAME@`: the input filename, without a path
- `@PLAINNAME0@` `@PLAINNAME1@` `...` *(since 1.5.0)*: the input filename without a path, with the specified array index in `input`
- `@BASENAME@`: the input filename, with extension removed
- `@BASENAME0@` `@BASENAME1@` `...` *(since 1.5.0)*: the input filename with extension removed, with the specified array index in `input`
- `@PRIVATE_DIR@` *(since 0.50.1)*: path to a directory where the custom target must store all its intermediate files.
- `@SOURCE_ROOT@`: the path to the root of the source tree. Depending on the backend,
  this may be an absolute or a relative to current workdir path.
- `@BUILD_ROOT@`: the path to the root of the build tree. Depending on the backend,
  this may be an absolute or a relative to current workdir path.
- `@CURRENT_SOURCE_DIR@`: this is the directory where the currently
  processed meson.build is located in.  Depending on the backend,
  this may be an absolute or a relative to current workdir path.

*(since 0.47.0)* The `depfile` keyword argument also accepts the
`@BASENAME@` and `@PLAINNAME@` substitutions.

The returned object also has methods that are documented in [[@custom_tgt]].

<p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- A bit of space because we remove the top margin below -->
<div style="display: flex;">
  <h3 style="margin-top: 0px;">Signature</h3>
  <div style="flex-grow: 1;"></div>
  <div class="d-flex flex-column align-items-center gap-1">

</div>
</div>

<pre><code class="language-meson"># Create a custom top level build target
[[#@custom_tgt]] custom_target(
  [[#@str]]                                  [<a href="#custom_target_name"><b>name</b></a>],   # The *unique* id of the custom target

  # Keyword arguments:
  <a href="#custom_target_build_always"><b>build_always</b></a>       : [[#@bool]]                                                                                                                                                                                      # If `true` this target is always considered out of
  <a href="#custom_target_build_always_stale"><b>build_always_stale</b></a> : [[#@bool]]                                                                                                                                                                                      # If `true` the target is always considered out of date
  <a href="#custom_target_build_by_default"><b>build_by_default</b></a>   : [[#@bool]]                                                                                                                                                                                      # Causes, when set to true, to
  <a href="#custom_target_build_subdir"><b>build_subdir</b></a>       : [[#@str]]                                                                                                                                                                                       # Places the build results in a subdirectory of the given name rather than directly into the build directory
  <a href="#custom_target_capture"><b>capture</b></a>            : [[#@bool]]                                                                                                                                                                                      # There are some compilers that can't be told to write
  <a href="#custom_target_command"><b>command</b></a>            : [[#@array]][[[#@str]] | [[#@file]] | [[#@exe]] | [[#@program]]]                                                                                                                                                         # Command to run to create outputs from inputs
  <a href="#custom_target_console"><b>console</b></a>            : [[#@bool]]                                                                                                                                                                                      # Keyword argument conflicts with `capture`, and is meant
  <a href="#custom_target_depend_files"><b>depend_files</b></a>       : [[#@array]][[[#@str]] | [[#@file]]]                                                                                                                                                                         # files ([[@str]],
  <a href="#custom_target_depends"><b>depends</b></a>            : [[#@array]][[[#@build_tgt]] | [[#@custom_tgt]] | [[#@custom_idx]] | [[#@program]]]                                                                                                                                      # Specifies that this target depends on the specified
  <a href="#custom_target_depfile"><b>depfile</b></a>            : [[#@str]]                                                                                                                                                                                       # A dependency file that the command can write listing
  <a href="#custom_target_env"><b>env</b></a>                : [[#@env]] | [[#@array]][[[#@str]]] | [[#@dict]][[[#@str]]]                                                                                                                                                              # environment variables to set, such as
  <a href="#custom_target_feed"><b>feed</b></a>               : [[#@bool]]                                                                                                                                                                                      # There are some compilers that can't be told to read
  <a href="#custom_target_input"><b>input</b></a>              : [[#@array]][[[#@str]] | [[#@file]] | [[#@program]]]                                                                                                                                                               # Array of source files
  <a href="#custom_target_install"><b>install</b></a>            : [[#@bool]]                                                                                                                                                                                      # When true, one or more files of this target are installed during the install step (see `install_dir` for details)
  <a href="#custom_target_install_dir"><b>install_dir</b></a>        : [[#@str]] | [[#@array]][[[#@str]] | [[#@bool]]]                                                                                                                                                                   # If only one install_dir is provided, all outputs are installed there
  <a href="#custom_target_install_mode"><b>install_mode</b></a>       : [[#@array]][[[#@str]] | [[#@int]]]                                                                                                                                                                          # The file mode and optionally the owner/uid and group/gid
  <a href="#custom_target_install_tag"><b>install_tag</b></a>        : [[#@array]][[[#@str]]]                                                                                                                                                                                # An array of strings, one per output, used by the `meson install --tags` command
  <a href="#custom_target_output"><b>output</b></a>             : [[#@array]][[[#@str]]]                                                                                                                                                                                # Array of output files
)</code></pre>


```{note}
Assuming that `command:` is executed by a POSIX `sh` shell
is not portable, notably to Windows. Instead, consider using a
`native: true` [[executable]], or a python script.

```

```{warning}
the `install_mode` kwarg ignored integer values between 0.60.0 -- 1.1.0.
```

  <p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- A bit more space -->

  ### Arguments


  The function `custom_target()` accepts the following positional arguments:

  <table class="table table-bordered table-sm">
    <thead>
      <tr>
        <th class="text-center text-nowrap">Name</th>
        <th class="text-center text-nowrap">Type</th>
        <th>Description</th>
      </tr>
    </thead>
    <tbody>
      <tr id="custom_target_name">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">name</code>
  <br>{meson:optional}` `
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  The *unique* id of the custom target

This posarg is optional *since 0.60.0*. It defaults to the basename
of the first output.

  </td>
      </tr>
    </tbody>
  </table>

  <p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- Extra space -->


  Finally, `custom_target()`
  accepts the following keyword arguments:

  <table class="table table-bordered table-sm">
    <thead>
      <tr>
        <th class="text-center text-nowrap">Name</th>
        <th class="text-center text-nowrap">Type</th>
        <th>Description</th>
      </tr>
    </thead>
    <tbody>
      <tr id="custom_target_build_always">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">build_always</code>
  <br>{meson:deprecated}`0.47.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@bool]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  If `true` this target is always considered out of
date and is rebuilt every time.  Equivalent to setting both
`build_always_stale` and `build_by_default` to true.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="custom_target_build_always_stale">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">build_always_stale</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`0.47.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@bool]]</span>
  <br><code class="language-meson text-nowrap">default = false</code>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  If `true` the target is always considered out of date.
Useful for things such as build timestamps or revision control tags.
The associated command is run even if the outputs are up to date.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="custom_target_build_by_default">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">build_by_default</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`0.38.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@bool]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Causes, when set to true, to
have this target be built by default. This means it will be built when
`meson compile` is called without any arguments. The default value is `false`.

*(since 0.50.0)* If `build_by_default` is explicitly set to false, `install`
will no longer override it. If `build_by_default` is not set, `install` will
still determine its default.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="custom_target_build_subdir">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">build_subdir</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`1.10.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Places the build results in a subdirectory of the given name rather than directly into the build directory. This does not affect the install directory, which uses install_dir.
This allows inserting a directory name into the build path, either when needed to use the build result while building other targets or as a way to support multiple targets with the same basename by using unique build_subdir values for each one.
To prevent collisions within the build directory, build_subdir is not allowed to match a file or directory in the source directory, nor contain '..' to refer to the parent of the build directory.
  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="custom_target_capture">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">capture</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@bool]]</span>
  <br><code class="language-meson text-nowrap">default = false</code>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  There are some compilers that can't be told to write
their output to a file but instead write it to standard output. When
this argument is set to true, Meson captures `stdout` and writes it
to the target file. Note that your command argument array may not
contain `@OUTPUT@` when capture mode is active.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="custom_target_command">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">command</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@file]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@exe]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@program]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Command to run to create outputs from inputs. The command
may be strings or the return value of functions that return file-like
objects such as [[find_program]],
[[executable]], [[configure_file]],
[[files]], [[custom_target]], etc.
Meson will automatically insert the appropriate dependencies on
targets and files listed in this keyword argument.
Note: always specify commands in array form `['commandname',
'-arg1', '-arg2']` rather than as a string `'commandname -arg1
-arg2'` as the latter will *not* work.

*Note:* due to an [issue with path handling](https://github.com/mesonbuild/meson/issues/1564)
any backslash (`\`) characters are rewritten as slash (`/`).

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="custom_target_console">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">console</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`0.48.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@bool]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Keyword argument conflicts with `capture`, and is meant
for commands that are resource-intensive and take a long time to
finish. With the Ninja backend, setting this will add this target
to [Ninja's `console` pool](https://ninja-build.org/manual.html#_the_literal_console_literal_pool),
which has special properties such as not buffering stdout and
serializing all targets in this pool.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="custom_target_depend_files">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">depend_files</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@file]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  files ([[@str]],
[[@file]], or the return value of [[configure_file]] that
this target depends on but are not listed in the `command` keyword
argument. Useful for adding regen dependencies.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="custom_target_depends">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">depends</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@build_tgt]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@custom_tgt]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@custom_idx]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@program]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Specifies that this target depends on the specified
target(s), even though it does not take any of them as a command
line argument. This is meant for cases where you have a tool that
e.g. does globbing internally. Usually you should just put the
generated sources as inputs and Meson will set up all dependencies
automatically (custom_idx was unavailable as a type between 0.60
and 1.4.0).  `program` is supported *since 1.11.2*.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="custom_target_depfile">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">depfile</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  A dependency file that the command can write listing
all the additional files this target depends on, for example a C
compiler would list all the header files it included, and a change
in any one of these files triggers a recompilation.

*(since 0.47.0)* the `@BASENAME@` and `@PLAINNAME@` substitutions
are also accepted.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="custom_target_env">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">env</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`0.57.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@env]]</span> | [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>] | [[@dict]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  environment variables to set, such as
`{'NAME1': 'value1', 'NAME2': 'value2'}` or `['NAME1=value1', 'NAME2=value2']`,
or an [[@env]] object which allows more
sophisticated environment juggling.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="custom_target_feed">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">feed</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`0.59.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@bool]]</span>
  <br><code class="language-meson text-nowrap">default = false</code>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  There are some compilers that can't be told to read
their input from a file and instead read it from standard input. When this
argument is set to `true`, Meson feeds the input file to `stdin`. Note that
your argument array may not contain `@INPUT@` when feed mode is active.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="custom_target_input">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">input</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@file]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@program]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Array of source files. *(since 0.41.0)* the array is flattened.
`program` is supported *since 1.11.2*.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="custom_target_install">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">install</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@bool]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  When true, one or more files of this target are installed during the install step (see `install_dir` for details).
  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="custom_target_install_dir">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">install_dir</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@bool]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  If only one install_dir is provided, all outputs are installed there.
*Since 0.40.0* Allows you to specify the installation directory for each
corresponding output. For example:
```meson
custom_target('different-install-dirs',
  output : ['first.file', 'second.file'],
  install : true,
  install_dir : ['somedir', 'otherdir'])
```
This would install `first.file` to `somedir` and `second.file` to `otherdir`.

To only install some outputs, pass `false` for the outputs that you
don't want installed. For example:
```meson
    custom_target('only-install-second',
      output : ['first.file', 'second.file'],
      install : true,
      install_dir : [false, 'otherdir'])
```
This would install `second.file` to `otherdir` and not install `first.file`.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="custom_target_install_mode">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">install_mode</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`0.47.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@int]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  The file mode and optionally the owner/uid and group/gid.
See the `install_mode` kwarg of [[install_data]] for more information.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="custom_target_install_tag">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">install_tag</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`0.60.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  An array of strings, one per output, used by the `meson install --tags` command
to install only a subset of the files.

By default all outputs have no install tag which means they are not being
installed when `--tags` argument is specified. If only one tag is specified,
it is assumed that all outputs have the same tag. `false` can be used for
outputs that have no tag or are not installed.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="custom_target_output">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">output</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Array of output files. These cannot contain path separators.

  </td>
      </tr>
    </tbody>
  </table>

<p style="padding: 7.5px; margin: 0px;"></p>

## debug()

Write the argument string to the meson build log.

<p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- A bit of space because we remove the top margin below -->
<div style="display: flex;">
  <h3 style="margin-top: 0px;">Signature</h3>
  <div style="flex-grow: 1;"></div>
  <div class="d-flex flex-column align-items-center gap-1">

{meson:since}`0.63.0`
</div>
</div>

<pre><code class="language-meson"># Write the argument string to the meson build log
[[#@void]] debug(
  [[#@str]] | [[#@int]] | [[#@bool]] | [[#@array]][[[#@str]] | [[#@int]] | [[#@bool]]] | [[#@dict]][[[#@str]] | [[#@int]] | [[#@bool]]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          <a href="#debug_message"><b>message</b></a>,     # The message to print
  [[#@str]] | [[#@int]] | [[#@bool]] | [[#@array]][[[#@str]] | [[#@int]] | [[#@bool]]] | [[#@dict]][[[#@str]] | [[#@int]] | [[#@bool]]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          <b>msg</b>...,      # Additional parameters will be separated by spaces
)</code></pre>




  <p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- A bit more space -->

  ### Arguments


  The function `debug()` accepts the following positional arguments:

  <table class="table table-bordered table-sm">
    <thead>
      <tr>
        <th class="text-center text-nowrap">Name</th>
        <th class="text-center text-nowrap">Type</th>
        <th>Description</th>
      </tr>
    </thead>
    <tbody>
      <tr id="debug_message">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">message</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@int]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@bool]]</span> | [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@int]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@bool]]</span>] | [[@dict]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@int]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@bool]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  The message to print
  </td>
      </tr>
    </tbody>
  </table>

  <p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- Extra space -->

  Additionally, the
  function accepts between `0` and `infinity` variadic
  arguments (`msg...`) of type <code><span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@int]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@bool]]</span> | [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@int]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@bool]]</span>] | [[@dict]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@int]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@bool]]</span>]</code>.

  Additional parameters will be separated by spaces

  
  <p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- Extra space -->


<p style="padding: 7.5px; margin: 0px;"></p>

## declare_dependency()

This function returns a [[@dep]] object that
behaves like the return value of [[dependency]] but is
internal to the current build. One use case for this is in
subprojects. This allows a subproject to easily specify how it should
be used. This makes it interchangeable with the same dependency that
is provided externally by the system. Another common use case is to
declare project targets as dependencies so they may be used as
dependencies of other build targets.

<p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- A bit of space because we remove the top margin below -->
<div style="display: flex;">
  <h3 style="margin-top: 0px;">Signature</h3>
  <div style="flex-grow: 1;"></div>
  <div class="d-flex flex-column align-items-center gap-1">

</div>
</div>

<pre><code class="language-meson"># This function returns a [[@dep]] object that
[[#@dep]] declare_dependency(
  <a href="#declare_dependency_compile_args"><b>compile_args</b></a>        : [[#@array]][[[#@str]]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                         # Compile arguments to use
  <a href="#declare_dependency_d_import_dirs"><b>d_import_dirs</b></a>       : [[#@array]][[[#@inc]] | [[#@str]]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                   # the directories to add to the string search path (i
  <a href="#declare_dependency_d_module_versions"><b>d_module_versions</b></a>   : [[#@str]] | [[#@int]] | [[#@array]][[[#@str]] | [[#@int]]]                                                                                                                                                                                                       # The [D version identifiers](https://dlang
  <a href="#declare_dependency_dependencies"><b>dependencies</b></a>        : [[#@array]][[[#@dep]]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                         # Other dependencies needed to use this dependency
  <a href="#declare_dependency_extra_files"><b>extra_files</b></a>         : [[#@array]][[[#@str]] | [[#@file]]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                  # extra files to add to targets
  <a href="#declare_dependency_include_directories"><b>include_directories</b></a> : [[#@array]][[[#@inc]] | [[#@str]]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                   # the directories to add to header search path,
  <a href="#declare_dependency_link_args"><b>link_args</b></a>           : [[#@array]][[[#@str]]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                         # Link arguments to use
  <a href="#declare_dependency_link_whole"><b>link_whole</b></a>          : [[#@array]][[[#@lib]]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                         # Libraries to link fully, same as [[executable]].
  <a href="#declare_dependency_link_with"><b>link_with</b></a>           : [[#@array]][[[#@lib]]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                         # Libraries to link against
  <a href="#declare_dependency_objects"><b>objects</b></a>             : [[#@array]][[[#@extracted_obj]]]                                                                                                                                                                                                               # an array of object files, to be linked directly into the targets that use the
  <a href="#declare_dependency_sources"><b>sources</b></a>             : [[#@array]][[[#@str]] | [[#@file]] | [[#@custom_tgt]] | [[#@custom_idx]] | [[#@generated_list]]]                                                                                                                                                                       # sources to add to targets
  <a href="#declare_dependency_variables"><b>variables</b></a>           : [[#@dict]][[[#@str]]] | [[#@array]][[[#@str]]]                                                                                                                                                                                                             # a dictionary of arbitrary strings,
  <a href="#declare_dependency_version"><b>version</b></a>             : [[#@str]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                # the version of this dependency,
)</code></pre>




  <p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- A bit more space -->

  ### Arguments




  The function `declare_dependency()`
  accepts the following keyword arguments:

  <table class="table table-bordered table-sm">
    <thead>
      <tr>
        <th class="text-center text-nowrap">Name</th>
        <th class="text-center text-nowrap">Type</th>
        <th>Description</th>
      </tr>
    </thead>
    <tbody>
      <tr id="declare_dependency_compile_args">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">

  </td>

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="declare_dependency_d_import_dirs">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">

  <br>{meson:since}`0.62.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@inc]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  the directories to add to the string search path (i.e. `-J` switch for DMD).
Must be [[@inc]] objects or plain strings.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="declare_dependency_d_module_versions">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">d_module_versions</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`0.62.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@int]]</span> | [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@int]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>

      <tr id="declare_dependency_dependencies">

        <td class="text-center">

  </td>
        <td>
  
  Other dependencies needed to use this dependency.
  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="declare_dependency_extra_files">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">extra_files</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`1.2.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@file]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>

mostly used for IDE integration.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="declare_dependency_include_directories">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">include_directories</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">

  </td>
        <td>
  
  the directories to add to header search path,
must be [[@inc]] objects or *(since 0.50.0)* plain strings.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="declare_dependency_link_args">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">link_args</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Link arguments to use.
  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="declare_dependency_link_whole">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">link_whole</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`0.46.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">

## dependency()

Finds an external dependency (usually a library installed on your
system) with the given name with `pkg-config` and [with
CMake](Dependencies.md#cmake) if `pkg-config` fails. Additionally,
frameworks (OSX only) and [library-specific fallback detection
logic](Dependencies.md#dependencies-with-custom-lookup-functionality)
are also supported.

*Since 0.60.0* more than one name can be provided, they will be tried in order
and the first name to be found will be used. The fallback subproject will be
used only if none of the names are found on the system. Once one of the name has
been found, all other names are added into the cache so subsequent calls for any
of those name will return the same value. This is useful in case a dependency
could have different names, such as `png` and `libpng`.

* Since *0.64.0* a dependency fallback can be provided by WrapDB. Simply download
the database locally using `meson wrap update-db` command and Meson will
automatically fallback to subprojects provided by WrapDB if the dependency is
not found on the system and the project does not ship their own `.wrap` file.

Dependencies can also be resolved in two other ways:

* if the same name was used in a `meson.override_dependency` prior to
  the call to `dependency`, the overriding dependency will be returned
  unconditionally; that is, the overriding dependency will be used
  independent of whether an external dependency is installed in the system.
  Typically, `meson.override_dependency` will have been used by a
  subproject.

* by a fallback subproject which, if needed, will be brought into the current
  build specification as if [`subproject()`](#subproject) had been called.
  The subproject can be specified with the `fallback` argument.  Alternatively,
  if the `fallback` argument is absent, *since 0.55.0* Meson can
  automatically identify a subproject as a fallback if a wrap file
  [provides](Wrap-dependency-system-manual.md#provide-section) the
  dependency, or if a subproject has the same name as the dependency.
  In the latter case, the subproject must use `meson.override_dependency` to
  specify the replacement, or Meson will report a hard error.  See the
  [Wrap documentation](Wrap-dependency-system-manual.md#provide-section)
  for more details.  This automatic search can be controlled using the
  `allow_fallback` keyword argument.

If `dependency_name` is `''`, the dependency is always not found. So
with `required: false`, this always returns a dependency object for
which the `found()` method returns `false`, and which can be passed
like any other dependency to the `dependencies:` keyword argument of a
`build_target`. This can be used to implement a dependency which is
sometimes not required e.g. in some branches of a conditional, or with
a `fallback:` kwarg, can be used to declare an optional dependency
that only looks in the specified subproject, and only if that's
allowed by `--wrap-mode`.

The returned object [[@dep]] also has additional methods.

<p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- A bit of space because we remove the top margin below -->
<div style="display: flex;">
  <h3 style="margin-top: 0px;">Signature</h3>
  <div style="flex-grow: 1;"></div>
  <div class="d-flex flex-column align-items-center gap-1">

</div>
</div>

<pre><code class="language-meson"># Finds an external dependency (usually a library installed on your
[[#@dep]] dependency(
  [[#@str]]                                  <b>names</b>...,  # The names of the dependency to look up

  # Keyword arguments:
  <a href="#dependency_allow_fallback"><b>allow_fallback</b></a>    : [[#@bool]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                 # Specifies whether Meson should automatically pick a fallback subproject
  <a href="#dependency_default_options"><b>default_options</b></a>   : [[#@array]][[[#@str]]] | [[#@dict]][[[#@str]] | [[#@bool]] | [[#@int]] | [[#@array]][[[#@str]]]]                                                                                                                                                                       # An array of default option values
  <a href="#dependency_disabler"><b>disabler</b></a>          : [[#@bool]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                 # Returns a [[disabler]] object instead of a not-found dependency
  <a href="#dependency_fallback"><b>fallback</b></a>          : [[#@array]][[[#@str]]] | [[#@str]]                                                                                                                                                                                                     # Manually specifies a subproject fallback
  <a href="#dependency_include_type"><b>include_type</b></a>      : [[#@str]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                  # An enum flag, marking how the dependency
  <a href="#dependency_language"><b>language</b></a>          : [[#@str]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                  # Defines what language-specific dependency to find
  <a href="#dependency_method"><b>method</b></a>            : [[#@str]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                  # Defines the way the dependency is detected, the default is
  <a href="#dependency_native"><b>native</b></a>            : [[#@bool]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                 # If set to `true`, causes Meson to find the dependency on
  <a href="#dependency_not_found_message"><b>not_found_message</b></a> : [[#@str]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                  # An optional string that will be printed as a [[message]] if the dependency was not found.
  <a href="#dependency_required"><b>required</b></a>          : [[#@bool]] | [[#@feature]]                                                                                                                                                                                                       # When set to `false`, Meson will proceed with the build
  <a href="#dependency_static"><b>static</b></a>            : [[#@bool]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                 # Tells the dependency provider to try to get static
  <a href="#dependency_version"><b>version</b></a>           : [[#@array]][[[#@str]]] | [[#@str]]                                                                                                                                                                                                     # Specifies the required version,
)</code></pre>


```{note}
This function supports additional [library-specific](Dependencies.md#dependencies-with-custom-lookup-functionality) keyword arguments that may also be accepted (e.g. `modules` specifies submodules to use for dependencies such as Qt or Boost. `components` allows the user to manually add CMake `COMPONENTS` for the `find_package` lookup)
```


  <p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- A bit more space -->

  ### Arguments



  The
  function accepts between `1` and `infinity` variadic
  arguments (`names...`) of type <code><span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span></code>.

  The names of the dependency to look up. The dependencies are looked up in
the order they are provided here. The first found dependency will then be
used. The fallback subproject will be used only if none of the names are
found on the system. Once one of the name has been found, all other names
are added into the cache so subsequent calls for any of those name will
return the same value. This is useful in case a dependency could have
different names, such as `png` and `libpng`.

**NOTE:** Before *0.60.0* only a single dependency name was allowed.


  {meson:since}`0.60.0`

  <p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- Extra space -->

  The function `dependency()`
  accepts the following keyword arguments:

## error()

  </td>
        <td>
  
  The message to print
  </td>
      </tr>
    </tbody>
  </table>

  <p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- Extra space -->

  Additionally, the
  function accepts between `0` and `infinity` variadic
  arguments (`msg...`) of type <code><span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span></code>.

  Additional parameters will be separated by spaces

  {meson:since}`0.58.0`

  <p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- Extra space -->


<p style="padding: 7.5px; margin: 0px;"></p>

---
<p style="padding: 7.5px; margin: 0px;"></p>

## executable()

Creates a new executable. The first argument specifies its name and
the remaining positional arguments define the input files to use.

The arrays for the kwargs (such as `sources`, `objects`, and `dependencies`) are
always flattened, which means you can freely nest and add arrays while
creating the final array.

The returned object also has methods that are documented in [[@exe]].

*Since 1.3.0* executable names can be the same across multiple targets as
long as they each have a different `name_suffix`.

<p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- A bit of space because we remove the top margin below -->
<div style="display: flex;">
  <h3 style="margin-top: 0px;">Signature</h3>
  <div style="flex-grow: 1;"></div>
  <div class="d-flex flex-column align-items-center gap-1">

</div>
</div>

<pre><code class="language-meson"># Creates a new executable

  [[#@str]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                        <a href="#executable_target_name"><b>target_name</b></a>,     # The *unique* name of the build target
  [[#@str]] | [[#@file]] | [[#@custom_tgt]] | [[#@custom_idx]] | [[#@generated_list]]                                                                                                                                                                      <b>source</b>...,       # Input source to compile

  # Keyword arguments:

  <a href="#executable_export_dynamic"><b>export_dynamic</b></a>               : [[#@bool]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          # when set to true causes the target's symbols to be

  <a href="#executable_gnu_symbol_visibility"><b>gnu_symbol_visibility</b></a>        : [[#@str]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           # Specifies how symbols should be exported, see
  <a href="#executable_gui_app"><b>gui_app</b></a>                      : [[#@bool]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          # When set to true flags this target as a GUI application
  <a href="#executable_implib"><b>implib</b></a>                       : [[#@bool]] | [[#@str]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    # When set to a string, that will be the name of a generated import library
  <a href="#executable_implicit_include_directories"><b>implicit_include_directories</b></a> : [[#@bool]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          # Controls whether Meson adds the current source and build directories to the include path
  <a href="#executable_include_directories"><b>include_directories</b></a>          : [[#@array]][[[#@inc]] | [[#@str]]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              # one or more objects created with the [[include_directories]] function, or
  <a href="#executable_install"><b>install</b></a>                      : [[#@bool]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          # When set to true, all outputs of this build_target will be installed

  <a href="#executable_install_vala_gir"><b>install_vala_gir</b></a>             : [[#@str]] | [[#@bool]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    # If set to a string value, the vala generated gir library will be

  <a href="#executable_install_vala_vapi"><b>install_vala_vapi</b></a>            : [[#@str]] | [[#@bool]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    # If set to a string value, the vala generated vapi library will be

  <a href="#executable_sources"><b>sources</b></a>                      : [[#@str]] | [[#@file]] | [[#@custom_tgt]] | [[#@custom_idx]] | [[#@generated_list]] | [[#@structured_src]]                                                                                                                                                                                                        # Additional source files
  <a href="#executable_vala_gir"><b>vala_gir</b></a>                     : [[#@str]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           # If set, generates a GIR file with the given name
  <a href="#executable_vala_header"><b>vala_header</b></a>                  : [[#@str]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           # On Vala targets, this provides a way to override the name of the generated
  <a href="#executable_vala_vapi"><b>vala_vapi</b></a>                    : [[#@str]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           # On Vala targets, this provides a way to override the name of the generated
  <a href="#executable_vs_module_defs"><b>vs_module_defs</b></a>               : [[#@str]] | [[#@file]] | [[#@custom_tgt]] | [[#@custom_idx]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          # Specify a Microsoft module definition file for controlling symbol exports,

)</code></pre>

The `link_language` kwarg was broken until 0.55.0

  ### Arguments


  The function `executable()` accepts the following positional arguments:

  <table class="table table-bordered table-sm">
    <thead>
      <tr>
        <th class="text-center text-nowrap">Name</th>
        <th class="text-center text-nowrap">Type</th>
        <th>Description</th>
      </tr>
    </thead>
    <tbody>
      <tr id="executable_target_name">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">target_name</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  The *unique* name of the build target
  </td>
      </tr>
    </tbody>
  </table>

  <p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- Extra space -->

  Additionally, the
  function accepts between `0` and `infinity` variadic
  arguments (`source...`) of type <code><span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@file]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@custom_tgt]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@custom_idx]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@generated_list]]</span></code>.

  Input source to compile. The following types are supported:

- Strings relative to the current source directory
- [[@file]] objects defined in any preceding build file
- The return value of configure-time generators such as [[configure_file]]
- The return value of build-time generators such as
  [[custom_target]] or [[generator.process]]

These input files can be sources, objects, libraries, or any other
file. Meson will automatically categorize them based on the extension
and use them accordingly. For instance, sources (`.c`, `.cpp`,
`.vala`, `.rs`, etc) will be compiled and objects (`.o`, `.obj`) and
libraries (`.so`, `.dll`, etc) will be linked.

With the Ninja backend, Meson will create a build-time [order-only
dependency](https://ninja-build.org/manual.html#ref_dependencies) on
all generated input files, including unknown files. This is needed to
bootstrap the generation of the real dependencies in the
[depfile](https://ninja-build.org/manual.html#ref_headers) generated
by your compiler to determine when to rebuild sources. Ninja relies on
this dependency file for all input files, generated and non-generated.
The behavior is similar for other backends.


  
  <p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- Extra space -->

  Finally, `executable()`
  accepts the following keyword arguments:

  <table class="table table-bordered table-sm">
    <thead>
      <tr>
        <th class="text-center text-nowrap">Name</th>
        <th class="text-center text-nowrap">Type</th>
        <th>Description</th>
      </tr>
    </thead>
    <tbody>
      <tr id="executable__lang__args">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">&lt;lang&gt;_args</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@file]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  compiler flags to use for the given language; eg: `cpp_args` for C++

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="executable__lang__pch">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">&lt;lang&gt;_pch</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  precompiled header file to use for the given language
  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="executable_android_exe_type">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">android_exe_type</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`1.8.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  <br><code class="language-meson text-nowrap">default = 'executable'</code>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Specifies the intended target of the executable. This can either be
`executable`, if the intended usecase is to run the executable using
fork + exec, or `application` if the executable is supposed to be
loaded as shared object by the android runtime.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="executable_build_by_default">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">build_by_default</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`0.38.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@bool]]</span>
  <br><code class="language-meson text-nowrap">default = true</code>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Causes, when set to `true`, to have this target be built by default.
This means it will be built when `meson compile` is called without any
arguments. The default value is `true` for all built target types.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="executable_build_rpath">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">build_rpath</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`0.42.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  A string to add to target's rpath definition in the build dir,
but which will be removed on install

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="executable_build_subdir">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">build_subdir</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`1.10.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Places the build results in a subdirectory of the given name rather than directly into the build directory. This does not affect the install directory, which uses install_dir.
This allows inserting a directory name into the build path, either when needed to use the build result while building other targets or as a way to support multiple targets with the same basename by using unique build_subdir values for each one.
To prevent collisions within the build directory, build_subdir is not allowed to match a file or directory in the source directory, nor contain '..' to refer to the parent of the build directory.
  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="executable_d_debug">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">d_debug</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  The [D version identifiers](https://dlang.org/spec/version.html#version) to add
during the compilation of D source files.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="executable_d_import_dirs">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">d_import_dirs</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`0.62.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@inc]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  the directories to add to the string search path (i.e. `-J` switch for DMD).
Must be objects created with the [[include_directories]] function or plain
strings, which will be implicitly converted into [[@inc]] objects.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="executable_d_module_versions">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">d_module_versions</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@int]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  List of module version identifiers set when compiling D sources.
  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="executable_d_unittest">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">d_unittest</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@bool]]</span>
  <br><code class="language-meson text-nowrap">default = false</code>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  When set to true, the D modules are compiled in debug mode.
  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="executable_dependencies">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">dependencies</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@dep]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  one or more dependency objects
created with
[[dependency]] or [[compiler.find_library]]
(for external deps) or [[declare_dependency]]
(for deps built by the project)

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="executable_export_dynamic">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">export_dynamic</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`0.45.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@bool]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  when set to true causes the target's symbols to be
dynamically exported, allowing modules built using the
[[shared_module]] function to refer to functions,
variables and other symbols defined in the executable itself.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="executable_extra_files">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">extra_files</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@file]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Not used for the build itself but are shown as source files in IDEs
that group files by targets (such as Visual Studio).

These may only be static sources.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="executable_gnu_symbol_visibility">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">gnu_symbol_visibility</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`0.48.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Specifies how symbols should be exported, see
e.g [the GCC Wiki](https://gcc.gnu.org/wiki/Visibility) for more
information. This value can either be an empty string or one of
`default`, `internal`, `hidden`, `protected` or `inlineshidden`, which
is the same as `hidden` but also includes things like C++ implicit
constructors as specified in the GCC manual. Ignored on compilers that
do not support GNU visibility arguments. See also
[`snippets.symbol_visibility_header()`](Snippets-module.md#symbol_visibility_header)
method to help with defining public API.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="executable_gui_app">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">gui_app</code>
  <br>{meson:deprecated}`0.56.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@bool]]</span>
  <br><code class="language-meson text-nowrap">default = false</code>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  When set to true flags this target as a GUI application
on platforms where this makes a difference, **deprecated** since
0.56.0, use `win_subsystem` instead.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="executable_implib">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">implib</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`0.42.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@bool]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  When set to a string, that will be the name of a generated import library.

*Since 1.10.0* passing a boolean value is deprecated.

When set to true, an import library is generated for the
executable (the name of the import library is based on *exe_name*).
Alternatively, when set to a string, that gives the base name for
the import library.  The import library is used when the returned
build target object appears in `link_with:` elsewhere.  Only has any
effect on platforms where that is meaningful (e.g. Windows). Implies
the `export_dynamic` argument.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="executable_implicit_include_directories">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">implicit_include_directories</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`0.42.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@bool]]</span>
  <br><code class="language-meson text-nowrap">default = true</code>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Controls whether Meson adds the current source and build directories to the include path
  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="executable_include_directories">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">include_directories</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@inc]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  one or more objects created with the [[include_directories]] function, or
*(since 0.50.0)* strings, which will be implicitly convert to [[@inc]]
objects

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="executable_install">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">install</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@bool]]</span>
  <br><code class="language-meson text-nowrap">default = false</code>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  When set to true, all outputs of this build_target will be installed.
  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="executable_install_dir">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">install_dir</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@bool]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  override install directory for outputs of this build target. If the value is a
relative path, it will be considered relative the `prefix` option.
For example, if you want to install plugins into a subdir, you'd use
something like this: `install_dir : get_option('libdir') / 'projectname-1.0'`.

This can be set to an array of values to control the the installation path
of build targets with multiple outputs. Currently, that means Vala. Setting
them to `true` means "use the default", and `false` means, don't install.
By default the first element is true, and all other elements are false.

*Since 1.11.0* Passing an array is deprecated, and install_vala_header,
install_vala_vapi, and install_vala_gir keyword arguments should be used
instead.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="executable_install_mode">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">install_mode</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`0.47.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@int]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Specify the file mode in symbolic format
and optionally the owner/uid and group/gid for the installed files.

See the `install_mode` kwarg of [[install_data]] for more information.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="executable_install_rpath">

## files()

This command takes the strings given to it in arguments and returns
corresponding File objects that you can use as sources for build
targets. The difference is that file objects remember the subdirectory
they were defined in and can be used anywhere in the source tree.

<p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- A bit of space because we remove the top margin below -->
<div style="display: flex;">
  <h3 style="margin-top: 0px;">Signature</h3>
  <div style="flex-grow: 1;"></div>
  <div class="d-flex flex-column align-items-center gap-1">

</div>
</div>

<pre><code class="language-meson"># This command takes the strings given to it in arguments and returns
[[#@array]][[[#@file]]] files(
  [[#@str]]                                  <b>file</b>...,  # Path to the file
)</code></pre>

  <p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- A bit more space -->

  ### Example

As an example suppose you have source file `foo.cpp` in subdirectory
`bar1` and you would like to use it in a build target that is defined
in `bar2`. To make this happen you first create the object in `bar1`
like this:

```meson
    foofile = files('foo.cpp')
```

Then you can use it in `bar2` like this:

```meson
    executable('myprog', 'myprog.cpp', foofile, ...)
```

Meson will then do the right thing.




  <p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- A bit more space -->

  ### Arguments



  The
  function accepts between `0` and `infinity` variadic
  arguments (`file...`) of type <code><span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span></code>.

  Path to the file.

  
  <p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- Extra space -->


<p style="padding: 7.5px; margin: 0px;"></p>

---
<p style="padding: 7.5px; margin: 0px;"></p>

## find_program()

`program_name` here is a string that can be an executable or script
to be searched for in `PATH` or other places inside the project.
The search order is:

1. Program overrides set via [[meson.override_find_program]]
1. [`[provide]` sections](Wrap-dependency-system-manual.md#provide-section)
  in subproject wrap files, if [`wrap_mode`](Builtin-options.md#core-options) is
  set to `forcefallback`
1. [`[binaries]` section](Machine-files.md#binaries) in your machine files
1. Directories provided using the `dirs:` kwarg (see below)
1. Project's source tree relative to the current subdir
   - If you use the return value of [[configure_file]], the
     current subdir inside the build tree is used instead
1. `PATH` environment variable
1. [`[provide]` sections](Wrap-dependency-system-manual.md#provide-section) in
  subproject wrap files, if [`wrap_mode`](Builtin-options.md#core-options) is
  set to anything other than `nofallback`

Meson will also autodetect scripts with a shebang line and run them
with the executable/interpreter specified in it both on Windows
(because the command invocator will reject the command otherwise) and
Unixes (if the script file does not have the executable bit set).
Hence, you *must not* manually add the interpreter while using this
script as part of an array of commands. Since *0.50.0* if the "python3"
program is requested and it is not found in the system, Meson will return
its current interpreter.

If you need to check for a program in a non-standard location, you can
just pass an absolute path to `find_program`, e.g.

```meson
setcap = find_program('setcap', '/usr/sbin/setcap', '/sbin/setcap', required : false)
```

It is also possible to pass an array to `find_program` in case you
need to construct the set of paths to search on the fly:

```meson
setcap = find_program(['setcap', '/usr/sbin/setcap', '/sbin/setcap'], required : false)
```

*Since 1.2.0* `find_program('meson')` is automatically overridden to the Meson
command used to execute the build script.

The returned [[@program]] object also has documented methods.

<p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- A bit of space because we remove the top margin below -->
<div style="display: flex;">
  <h3 style="margin-top: 0px;">Signature</h3>
  <div style="flex-grow: 1;"></div>
  <div class="d-flex flex-column align-items-center gap-1">

</div>
</div>

<pre><code class="language-meson"># `program_name` here is a string that can be an executable or script
[[#@program]] find_program(
  [[#@str]] | [[#@file]]                                                                   <a href="#find_program_program_name"><b>program_name</b></a>,     # The name of the program to search, or a [[@file]] object to be used
  [[#@str]] | [[#@file]]                                                                   <b>fallback</b>...,      # These parameters are used as fallback names to search for

  # Keyword arguments:
  <a href="#find_program_default_options"><b>default_options</b></a>  : [[#@array]][[[#@str]]] | [[#@dict]][[[#@str]] | [[#@bool]] | [[#@int]] | [[#@array]][[[#@str]]]]                                                                                                                                                                       # An array of default option values
  <a href="#find_program_dirs"><b>dirs</b></a>             : [[#@array]][[[#@str]]]                                                                                                                                                                                                           # extra array of absolute paths where to look for program names
  <a href="#find_program_disabler"><b>disabler</b></a>         : [[#@bool]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                 # If `true` and the program couldn't be found, return a [[@disabler]] object
  <a href="#find_program_native"><b>native</b></a>           : [[#@bool]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                 # Defines how this executable should be searched
  <a href="#find_program_required"><b>required</b></a>         : [[#@bool]] | [[#@feature]]                                                                                                                                                                                                       # When `true`, Meson will abort if no program can be found
  <a href="#find_program_version"><b>version</b></a>          : [[#@str]] | [[#@array]][[[#@str]]]                                                                                                                                                                                                     # Specifies the required version, see
  <a href="#find_program_version_argument"><b>version_argument</b></a> : [[#@str]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                  # Specifies the argument to pass when trying to find the version of the program
)</code></pre>




  <p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- A bit more space -->

  ### Arguments


  The function `find_program()` accepts the following positional arguments:

  <table class="table table-bordered table-sm">
    <thead>
      <tr>
        <th class="text-center text-nowrap">Name</th>
        <th class="text-center text-nowrap">Type</th>
        <th>Description</th>
      </tr>
    </thead>
    <tbody>
      <tr id="find_program_program_name">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">program_name</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@file]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  The name of the program to search, or a [[@file]] object to be used
without searching.

  </td>
      </tr>
    </tbody>
  </table>

  <p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- Extra space -->

  Additionally, the
  function accepts between `0` and `infinity` variadic
  arguments (`fallback...`) of type <code><span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@file]]</span></code>.

  These parameters are used as fallback names to search for.
This is meant to be used for cases where the
program may have many alternative names, such as `foo` and
`foo.py`. The function will check for the arguments one by one and the
first one that is found is returned.


  {meson:since}`0.37.0`

  <p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- Extra space -->

  Finally, `find_program()`
  accepts the following keyword arguments:

  <table class="table table-bordered table-sm">
    <thead>

      <tr id="find_program_default_options">

  <br>{meson:since}`1.3.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">

        <td>

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="find_program_native">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">native</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`0.43.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@bool]]</span>
  <br><code class="language-meson text-nowrap">default = false</code>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Defines how this executable should be searched. By default
it is set to `false`, which causes Meson to first look for the
executable in the cross file (when cross building) and if it is not
defined there, then from the system. If set to `true`, the cross
file is ignored and the program is only searched from the system.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="find_program_required">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">required</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@bool]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@feature]]</span>
  <br><code class="language-meson text-nowrap">default = true</code>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  When `true`, Meson will abort if no program can be found.
If `required` is set to `false`,
Meson continue even if none of the programs can be found. You can
then use the `.found()` method on the returned [[@program]] to check
whether it was found or not. *(since 0.47.0)* The value of a
[`feature`](Build-options.md#features) option can also be passed to the
`required` keyword argument.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="find_program_version">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">version</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`0.52.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Specifies the required version, see
[[dependency]] for argument format. By default, the version of the program
is determined by running `program_name --version` command. If stdout is empty
it fallbacks to stderr. If the output contains more text than simply a version
number, only the first occurrence of numbers separated by dots is kept.
If the output is more complicated than that, the version checking will have to
be done manually using [[run_command]].

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="find_program_version_argument">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">version_argument</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`1.5.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Specifies the argument to pass when trying to find the version of the program.
If this is unspecified, `program_name --version` will be used.

  </td>
      </tr>
    </tbody>
  </table>
