## generator()

See also: [[custom_target]]

This function creates a [[@generator]] object that can
be used to run custom compilation commands. The only positional
argument is the executable to use. It can either be a self-built
executable or one returned by find_program.

The template strings passed to all the keyword arguments accept
the following special substitutions:

- `@PLAINNAME@`: the complete input file name, e.g: `foo.c` becomes `foo.c` (unchanged)
- `@BASENAME@`: the base of the input filename, e.g.: `foo.c.y` becomes `foo.c` (extension is removed)

Each string passed to the `output` keyword argument *must* be
constructed using one or both of these two substitutions.

In addition to the above substitutions, the `arguments` keyword
argument also accepts the following:

- `@OUTPUT@`: the full path to the output file
- `@INPUT@`: the full path to the input file
- `@DEPFILE@`: the full path to the depfile
- `@SOURCE_DIR@`: the full path to the root of the source tree
- `@CURRENT_SOURCE_DIR@`: this is the directory where the currently processed meson.build is located in
- `@BUILD_DIR@`: the full path to the root of the build dir where the output will be placed

NOTE: Generators should only be used for outputs that will ***only***
be used as inputs for a [[build_target]] or a [[custom_target]].
When you use the processed output of a
generator in multiple targets, the generator will be run multiple
times to create outputs for each target. Each output will be created
in a target-private directory `@BUILD_DIR@`.

If you want to generate files for general purposes such as for
generating headers to be used by several sources, or data that will be
installed, and so on, use a [[custom_target]] instead.

<p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- A bit of space because we remove the top margin below -->
<div style="display: flex;">
  <h3 style="margin-top: 0px;">Signature</h3>
  <div style="flex-grow: 1;"></div>
  <div class="d-flex flex-column align-items-center gap-1">

</div>
</div>

<pre><code class="language-meson"># See also: [[custom_target]]
[[#@generator]] generator(
  [[#@exe]] | [[#@program]]                                                                   <a href="#generator_exe"><b>exe</b></a>,     # Executable for the command to run

  # Keyword arguments:
  <a href="#generator_arguments"><b>arguments</b></a> : [[#@array]][[[#@str]]]                                                                                                                                                                                # An array of template strings that will be the command line arguments passed to the executable
  <a href="#generator_capture"><b>capture</b></a>   : [[#@bool]]                                                                                                                                                                                      # When this argument is set to true, Meson captures `stdout`
  <a href="#generator_depends"><b>depends</b></a>   : [[#@array]][[[#@build_tgt]] | [[#@custom_tgt]] | [[#@custom_idx]] | [[#@program]]]                                                                                                                                      # An array of build targets that must be built before
  <a href="#generator_depfile"><b>depfile</b></a>   : [[#@str]]                                                                                                                                                                                       # A template string pointing to a dependency file that a
  <a href="#generator_output"><b>output</b></a>    : [[#@array]][[[#@str]]]                                                                                                                                                                                # Template string (or array of template strings) defining
)</code></pre>

## get_option()

Obtains the value of the [project build option](Build-options.md)
specified in the positional argument.

Note that the value returned for built-in options that end in `dir`
such as `bindir` and `libdir` is usually a path relative to (and
inside) the `prefix` but you should not rely on that, as it can also
be an absolute path [in some cases](Builtin-options.md#universal-options).
[`install_dir` arguments](Installing.md) handle that as expected
but if you need an absolute path, e.g. to use in a define etc.,
you should use the path concatenation operator like this:
`get_option('prefix') / get_option('localstatedir')`.
Never manually join paths as if they were strings.

For options of type `feature` a
[[@feature]] option object
is returned instead of a string.
See [`feature` options](Build-options.md#features)
documentation for more details.

For options that are [specified
per-machine](Builtin-options.md#specifying-options-per-machine)
`get_option()` retrieves the value of the option for the
build machine if the argument starts with `build.`.

<p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- A bit of space because we remove the top margin below -->
<div style="display: flex;">
  <h3 style="margin-top: 0px;">Signature</h3>
  <div style="flex-grow: 1;"></div>
  <div class="d-flex flex-column align-items-center gap-1">

</div>
</div>

<pre><code class="language-meson"># Obtains the value of the [project build option](Build-options
[[#@str]] | [[#@int]] | [[#@bool]] | [[#@feature]] | [[#@array]][[[#@str]] | [[#@int]] | [[#@bool]]] get_option(
  [[#@str]]                                  <a href="#get_option_option_name"><b>option_name</b></a>,     # Name of the option to query
)</code></pre>




  <p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- A bit more space -->

  ### Arguments

## get_variable()

This function can be used to dynamically obtain a variable. `res =
get_variable(varname, fallback)` takes the value of `varname` (which
must be a string) and stores the variable of that name into `res`. If
the variable does not exist, the variable `fallback` is stored to
`res`instead. If a fallback is not specified, then attempting to read
a non-existing variable will cause a fatal error.

<p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- A bit of space because we remove the top margin below -->
<div style="display: flex;">
  <h3 style="margin-top: 0px;">Signature</h3>
  <div style="flex-grow: 1;"></div>
  <div class="d-flex flex-column align-items-center gap-1">

</div>
</div>

<pre><code class="language-meson"># This function can be used to dynamically obtain a variable
[[#@any]] get_variable(
  [[#@str]]                                  <a href="#get_variable_variable_name"><b>variable_name</b></a>,     # Name of the variable to get
  [[#@any]]                                  [<a href="#get_variable_default"><b>default</b></a>],         # Fallback value to return when the variable does not exist
)</code></pre>




  <p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- A bit more space -->

  ### Arguments

  [Argument flattening](Syntax.md#argument-flattening) is **NOT SUPPORTED** by this function.

  The function `get_variable()` accepts the following positional arguments:

  <br>{meson:optional}` `

## import()

Imports the given extension module. Returns an object that can be used to call
the methods of the module. Here's an example for a hypothetical `testmod`
module.

<p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- A bit of space because we remove the top margin below -->

</div>
</div>

<pre><code class="language-meson"># Imports the given extension module
[[#@module]] import(
  [[#@str]]                                  <a href="#import_module_name"><b>module_name</b></a>,     # Name of the module to import

  # Keyword arguments:
  <a href="#import_disabler"><b>disabler</b></a> : [[#@bool]]                                                                              # Returns a [[@disabler]] object when not found.
  <a href="#import_required"><b>required</b></a> : [[#@bool]] | [[#@feature]]                                                                    # When set to `false`, Meson will proceed with the build even if the module is not found
)</code></pre>

  <p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- A bit more space -->

## include_directories()

Returns an opaque object which contains the directories (relative to the
current directory) given in the positional arguments. The result can then be
passed to the `include_directories:` and `d_import_dirs:` keyword arguments
when building executables or libraries. You can use the returned object in any
subdirectory you want, Meson will make the paths work automatically.

Note that this function call itself does not add the directories into
the search path, since there is no global search path. For something
like that, see [`add_project_arguments()`](#add_project_arguments).

See also `implicit_include_directories` parameter of
[[executable]], which adds current source and build
directories to include path.

Each directory given is converted to two include paths: one that is
relative to the source root and one relative to the build root.

<p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- A bit of space because we remove the top margin below -->
<div style="display: flex;">
  <h3 style="margin-top: 0px;">Signature</h3>
  <div style="flex-grow: 1;"></div>
  <div class="d-flex flex-column align-items-center gap-1">

</div>
</div>

<pre><code class="language-meson"># Returns an opaque object which contains the directories (relative to the
[[#@inc]] include_directories(
  [[#@str]]                                  <b>includes</b>...,  # Include paths to add

  # Keyword arguments:
  <a href="#include_directories_is_system"><b>is_system</b></a> : [[#@bool]]                                   # If set to `true`, flags the specified directories as system directories
)</code></pre>

  <p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- A bit more space -->

  ### Example

For example, with the following source tree layout in
`/home/user/project.git`:

`meson.build`:
```meson
project(...)

subdir('include')
subdir('src')

...
```

`include/meson.build`:
```meson
inc = include_directories('.')

...
```

`src/meson.build`:
```meson
sources = [...]

executable('some-tool', sources,
  include_directories : inc,
  ...)

...
```

If the build tree is `/tmp/build-tree`, the following include paths
will be added to the `executable()` call: `-I/tmp/build-tree/include
-I/home/user/project.git/include`.



```{warning}
When `is_system : true` is used, the include directory order on the
command line is **reversed** compared to the order given in the
arguments: the *last* directory listed has the highest priority
(it appears first among the `-isystem` flags and is searched first
by the compiler).  This is the opposite of the default (`is_system :
false`), where the *first* directory listed has the highest priority.
This may be changed in future releases, but behavior will not change
as long as `meson_version` is not changed in `project()`.

```

  <p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- A bit more space -->

  ### Arguments



  The
  function accepts between `0` and `infinity` variadic
  arguments (`includes...`) of type <code><span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span></code>.

  Include paths to add.

  
  <p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- Extra space -->

  The function `include_directories()`
  accepts the following keyword arguments:

  <table class="table table-bordered table-sm">
    <thead>
      <tr>
        <th class="text-center text-nowrap">Name</th>
        <th class="text-center text-nowrap">Type</th>
        <th>Description</th>
      </tr>
    </thead>
    <tbody>
      <tr id="include_directories_is_system">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">is_system</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@bool]]</span>
  <br><code class="language-meson text-nowrap">default = false</code>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  If set to `true`, flags the specified directories as system directories.
This means that
they will be used with the `-isystem` compiler argument rather than
`-I` on compilers that support this flag (in practice everything
except Visual Studio).

  </td>
      </tr>
    </tbody>
  </table>

<p style="padding: 7.5px; margin: 0px;"></p>

---
<p style="padding: 7.5px; margin: 0px;"></p>

## install_data()

Installs files from the source tree that are listed as positional arguments.
Please note that this can only install static files from the source tree.
Generated files are installed via the `install_dir:` kwarg on the respective
generators, such as `custom_target()` or `configure_file()`.

See [Installing](Installing.md) for more examples.

<p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- A bit of space because we remove the top margin below -->
<div style="display: flex;">
  <h3 style="margin-top: 0px;">Signature</h3>
  <div style="flex-grow: 1;"></div>
  <div class="d-flex flex-column align-items-center gap-1">

</div>
</div>

<pre><code class="language-meson"># Installs files from the source tree that are listed as positional arguments
[[#@void]] install_data(
  [[#@file]] | [[#@str]]                                                                   <b>file</b>...,  # Files to install

  # Keyword arguments:
  <a href="#install_data_follow_symlinks"><b>follow_symlinks</b></a> : [[#@bool]]                                                                                 # If true, dereferences links and copies their target instead
  <a href="#install_data_install_dir"><b>install_dir</b></a>     : [[#@str]]                                                                                  # The absolute or relative path to the installation directory
  <a href="#install_data_install_mode"><b>install_mode</b></a>    : [[#@array]][[[#@str]] | [[#@int]]]                                                                     # specify the file mode in symbolic format and
  <a href="#install_data_install_tag"><b>install_tag</b></a>     : [[#@str]]                                                                                  # A string used by the `meson install --tags` command
  <a href="#install_data_preserve_path"><b>preserve_path</b></a>   : [[#@bool]]                                                                                 # Disable stripping child-directories from data files when installing
  <a href="#install_data_rename"><b>rename</b></a>          : [[#@array]][[[#@str]]]                                                                           # If specified renames each source file into corresponding file from `rename` array
  <a href="#install_data_sources"><b>sources</b></a>         : [[#@array]][[[#@file]] | [[#@str]]]                                                                    # Additional files to install
)</code></pre>



```{warning}
the `install_mode` kwarg ignored integer values between 0.59.0 -- 1.1.0.
```
```{warning}
an omitted `install_dir` kwarg did not work correctly inside of a subproject until 1.3.0.
```
```{warning}
an omitted `install_dir` kwarg did not work correctly when combined with the `preserve_path` kwarg until 1.3.0.
```

  <p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- A bit more space -->

  ### Arguments



  The
  function accepts between `0` and `infinity` variadic
  arguments (`file...`) of type <code><span class="text-nowrap">[[@file]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span></code>.

  Files to install.

  
  <p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- Extra space -->

  The function `install_data()`
  accepts the following keyword arguments:

  <table class="table table-bordered table-sm">

  </td>

  </td>

  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  The absolute or relative path to the installation directory.
If this is a relative path, it is assumed to be relative to the prefix.

If omitted, the directory defaults to `{datadir}/{projectname}` *(since 0.45.0)*.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="install_data_install_mode">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">install_mode</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`0.38.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">

  </td>
        <td>

optionally the owner/uid and group/gid for the installed files. For
example:

`install_mode: 'rw-r--r--'` for just the file mode

`install_mode: ['rw-r--r--', 'nobody', 'nogroup']` for the file mode and the user/group

`install_mode: ['rw-r-----', 0, 0]` for the file mode and uid/gid

To leave any of these three as the default, specify `false`.

      </tr>
      <tr id="install_data_install_tag">

  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  A string used by the `meson install --tags` command
to install only a subset of the files. By default these files have no install
tag which means they are not being installed when `--tags` argument is specified.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="install_data_preserve_path">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">preserve_path</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`0.64.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@bool]]</span>
  <br><code class="language-meson text-nowrap">default = false</code>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Disable stripping child-directories from data files when installing.

This is equivalent to GNU Automake's `nobase` option.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="install_data_rename">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">rename</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`0.46.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>]

  If specified renames each source file into corresponding file from `rename` array.

argument. If the directory exists and is not empty, the contents are left in

## install_headers()

Installs the specified header files from the source tree into the
system header directory (usually `/{prefix}/include`) during the
install step. This directory can be overridden by specifying it with
the `install_dir` keyword argument. If you just want to install into a
subdirectory of the system header directory, then use the `subdir`
argument. As an example if this has the value `myproj` then the
headers would be installed to `/{prefix}/include/myproj`.

Please note that this can only install static files from the source tree.
Generated files are installed via the `install_dir:` kwarg on the respective
generators, such as `custom_target()` or `configure_file()`.

<p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- A bit of space because we remove the top margin below -->
<div style="display: flex;">
  <h3 style="margin-top: 0px;">Signature</h3>
  <div style="flex-grow: 1;"></div>
  <div class="d-flex flex-column align-items-center gap-1">

</div>
</div>

<pre><code class="language-meson"># Installs the specified header files from the source tree into the
[[#@void]] install_headers(
  [[#@file]] | [[#@str]]                                                                   <b>file</b>...,  # Header files to install

  # Keyword arguments:
  <a href="#install_headers_follow_symlinks"><b>follow_symlinks</b></a> : [[#@bool]]                                                                                # If true, dereferences links and copies their target instead
  <a href="#install_headers_install_dir"><b>install_dir</b></a>     : [[#@str]]                                                                                 # Where to install to
  <a href="#install_headers_install_mode"><b>install_mode</b></a>    : [[#@array]][[[#@str]] | [[#@int]]]                                                                    # Specify the file mode in symbolic format
  <a href="#install_headers_install_tag"><b>install_tag</b></a>     : [[#@str]]                                                                                 # A string used by the `meson install --tags` command
  <a href="#install_headers_preserve_path"><b>preserve_path</b></a>   : [[#@bool]]                                                                                # Disable stripping child-directories from header files when installing
  <a href="#install_headers_subdir"><b>subdir</b></a>          : [[#@str]]                                                                                 # Install to the `subdir` subdirectory of the default includedir
)</code></pre>

  <p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- A bit more space -->

  ### Example

For example, this will install `common.h` and `kola.h` into
`/{prefix}/include`:

```meson
install_headers('common.h', 'proj/kola.h')
```

This will install `common.h` and `kola.h` into `/{prefix}/include/myproj`:

```meson
install_headers('common.h', 'proj/kola.h', subdir : 'myproj')
```

This will install `common.h` and `kola.h` into `/{prefix}/cust/myproj`:

```meson
install_headers('common.h', 'proj/kola.h', install_dir : 'cust', subdir : 'myproj')
```

This will install `common.h` into `/{prefix}/include` and `kola.h`
into `/{prefix}/include/proj/`:

```meson
install_headers('common.h', 'proj/kola.h', preserve_path : true)
```



```{warning}
the `install_mode` kwarg ignored integer values between 0.59.0 -- 1.1.0.
```

  <p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- A bit more space -->

  ### Arguments



  The
  function accepts between `0` and `infinity` variadic
  arguments (`file...`) of type <code><span class="text-nowrap">[[@file]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span></code>.

  Header files to install.

  
  <p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- Extra space -->

  The function `install_headers()`
  accepts the following keyword arguments:

  <table class="table table-bordered table-sm">
    <thead>
      <tr>
        <th class="text-center text-nowrap">Name</th>
        <th class="text-center text-nowrap">Type</th>
        <th>Description</th>
      </tr>
    </thead>
    <tbody>
      <tr id="install_headers_follow_symlinks">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">follow_symlinks</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`1.3.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@bool]]</span>
  <br><code class="language-meson text-nowrap">default = true</code>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  If true, dereferences links and copies their target instead.  The default
value will become false in the future.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="install_headers_install_dir">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">install_dir</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Where to install to.
  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="install_headers_install_mode">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">install_mode</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`0.47.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@int]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Specify the file mode in symbolic format
and optionally the owner/uid and group/gid for the installed files.

See the `install_mode` kwarg of [[install_data]] for more information.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="install_headers_install_tag">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">install_tag</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`1.11.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  A string used by the `meson install --tags` command
to install only a subset of the files. By default these files have the `devel`
install tag which means they are installed explicitly or when no tags given at all.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="install_headers_preserve_path">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">preserve_path</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`0.63.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@bool]]</span>
  <br><code class="language-meson text-nowrap">default = false</code>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Disable stripping child-directories from header files when installing.

This is equivalent to GNU Automake's `nobase` option.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="install_headers_subdir">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">subdir</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Install to the `subdir` subdirectory of the default includedir.

Incompatible with the `install_dir` kwarg.

  </td>
      </tr>
    </tbody>
  </table>

## install_man()

Installs the specified man files from the source tree into system's man directory
during the install step. This directory can be
overridden by specifying it with the `install_dir` keyword argument.

*(since 0.49.0)* [manpages are no longer compressed implicitly][install_man_49].
*(since 1.11.0)* install_man supports `install_tag` kwarg

[install_man_49]:
https://mesonbuild.com/Release-notes-for-0-49-0.html#manpages-are-no-longer-compressed-implicitly

<p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- A bit of space because we remove the top margin below -->

</div>
</div>

  [[#@file]] | [[#@str]]                                                                   <b>file</b>...,  # Man pages to install

  <a href="#install_man_install_dir"><b>install_dir</b></a>  : [[#@str]]                                                                                 # Where to install to
  <a href="#install_man_install_mode"><b>install_mode</b></a> : [[#@array]][[[#@str]] | [[#@int]]]                                                                    # Specify the file mode in symbolic format

)</code></pre>

<p style="padding: 7.5px; margin: 0px;"></p>

## install_subdir()

Installs the entire given subdirectory and its contents from the
source tree to the location specified by the keyword argument
`install_dir`.

*(since 0.45.0, deprecated since 0.60.0)* If the subdirectory does not exist
in the source tree, an empty directory is created in the specified location.
A newly created subdirectory may only be created in the keyword argument
`install_dir`. There are a number of flaws with this method, and it was never
intentionally designed to work this way, please use [[install_emptydir]]
instead.

<p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- A bit of space because we remove the top margin below -->
<div style="display: flex;">
  <h3 style="margin-top: 0px;">Signature</h3>
  <div style="flex-grow: 1;"></div>
  <div class="d-flex flex-column align-items-center gap-1">

</div>
</div>

<pre><code class="language-meson"># Installs the entire given subdirectory and its contents from the
[[#@void]] install_subdir(
  [[#@str]]                                  <a href="#install_subdir_subdir_name"><b>subdir_name</b></a>,     # The sub-directory to install

  # Keyword arguments:
  <a href="#install_subdir_exclude_directories"><b>exclude_directories</b></a> : [[#@array]][[[#@str]]]                                                                          # An array of directory names that should not be installed
  <a href="#install_subdir_exclude_files"><b>exclude_files</b></a>       : [[#@array]][[[#@str]]]                                                                          # An array of file names that should not be installed
  <a href="#install_subdir_follow_symlinks"><b>follow_symlinks</b></a>     : [[#@bool]]                                                                                # If true, dereferences links and copies their target instead
  <a href="#install_subdir_install_dir"><b>install_dir</b></a>         : [[#@str]]                                                                                 # Where to install to
  <a href="#install_subdir_install_mode"><b>install_mode</b></a>        : [[#@array]][[[#@str]] | [[#@int]]]                                                                    # Specify the file mode in symbolic format
  <a href="#install_subdir_install_tag"><b>install_tag</b></a>         : [[#@str]]                                                                                 # A string used by the `meson install --tags` command
  <a href="#install_subdir_strip_directory"><b>strip_directory</b></a>     : [[#@bool]]                                                                                # Install directory contents
)</code></pre>

  <p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- A bit more space -->

  ### Example

For a given directory `foo`:
```text
foo/
  bar/
    file1
  file2
```
`install_subdir('foo', install_dir : 'share', strip_directory : false)` creates
```text
share/
  foo/
    bar/
      file1
    file2
```

`install_subdir('foo', install_dir : 'share', strip_directory : true)` creates
```text
share/
  bar/
    file1
  file2
```

`install_subdir('foo/bar', install_dir : 'share', strip_directory : false)` creates
```text
share/
  bar/
    file1
```

`install_subdir('foo/bar', install_dir : 'share', strip_directory : true)` creates
```text
share/
  file1
```

`install_subdir('new_directory', install_dir : 'share')` creates
```text
share/
  new_directory/
```



```{warning}
the `install_mode` kwarg ignored integer values between 0.59.0 -- 1.1.0.
```

  <p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- A bit more space -->

  ### Arguments


  The function `install_subdir()` accepts the following positional arguments:

  <table class="table table-bordered table-sm">
    <thead>
      <tr>
        <th class="text-center text-nowrap">Name</th>
        <th class="text-center text-nowrap">Type</th>
        <th>Description</th>
      </tr>
    </thead>
    <tbody>
      <tr id="install_subdir_subdir_name">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">subdir_name</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  The sub-directory to install
  </td>
      </tr>
    </tbody>
  </table>

  <p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- Extra space -->


  Finally, `install_subdir()`
  accepts the following keyword arguments:

  <table class="table table-bordered table-sm">
    <thead>
      <tr>
        <th class="text-center text-nowrap">Name</th>
        <th class="text-center text-nowrap">Type</th>
        <th>Description</th>
      </tr>
    </thead>
    <tbody>
      <tr id="install_subdir_exclude_directories">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">exclude_directories</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`0.47.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  An array of directory names that should not be installed.
Names are interpreted as paths relative to the `subdir_name` location.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="install_subdir_exclude_files">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">exclude_files</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  An array of file names that should not be installed.
Names are interpreted as paths relative to the `subdir_name` location.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="install_subdir_follow_symlinks">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">follow_symlinks</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`1.3.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@bool]]</span>
  <br><code class="language-meson text-nowrap">default = true</code>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  If true, dereferences links and copies their target instead.  The default
value will become false in the future.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="install_subdir_install_dir">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">install_dir</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Where to install to.
  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="install_subdir_install_mode">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">install_mode</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`0.47.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@int]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Specify the file mode in symbolic format
and optionally the owner/uid and group/gid for the installed files.

See the `install_mode` kwarg of [[install_data]] for more information.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="install_subdir_install_tag">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">install_tag</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`0.60.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  A string used by the `meson install --tags` command
to install only a subset of the files. By default these files have no install
tag which means they are not being installed when `--tags` argument is specified.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="install_subdir_strip_directory">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">strip_directory</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`0.45.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@bool]]</span>
  <br><code class="language-meson text-nowrap">default = false</code>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Install directory contents.
If `strip_directory=true` only the last component of the source path is used.

  </td>
      </tr>
    </tbody>
  </table>

<p style="padding: 7.5px; margin: 0px;"></p>

## install_symlink()

Installs a symbolic link to `pointing_to` target under install_dir.

<p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- A bit of space because we remove the top margin below -->
<div style="display: flex;">
  <h3 style="margin-top: 0px;">Signature</h3>
  <div style="flex-grow: 1;"></div>
  <div class="d-flex flex-column align-items-center gap-1">

{meson:since}`0.61.0`
</div>
</div>

<pre><code class="language-meson"># Installs a symbolic link to `pointing_to` target under install_dir
[[#@void]] install_symlink(
  [[#@str]]                                  <a href="#install_symlink_link_name"><b>link_name</b></a>,     # Name of the created link under `install_dir`

  # Keyword arguments:
  <a href="#install_symlink_install_dir"><b>install_dir</b></a> : [[#@str]]                                   <i>[required]</i>  # The absolute or relative path to the installation directory for the links
  <a href="#install_symlink_install_tag"><b>install_tag</b></a> : [[#@str]]                                               # A string used by the `meson install --tags` command
  <a href="#install_symlink_pointing_to"><b>pointing_to</b></a> : [[#@str]]                                   <i>[required]</i>  # Target to point the link to
)</code></pre>




  <p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- A bit more space -->

  ### Arguments


  The function `install_symlink()` accepts the following positional arguments:

  <table class="table table-bordered table-sm">
    <thead>
      <tr>
        <th class="text-center text-nowrap">Name</th>
        <th class="text-center text-nowrap">Type</th>
        <th>Description</th>
      </tr>
    </thead>
    <tbody>
      <tr id="install_symlink_link_name">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">link_name</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Name of the created link under `install_dir`.
It cannot contain path separators. Those should go in `install_dir`.

  </td>
      </tr>
    </tbody>
  </table>

  <p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- Extra space -->


  Finally, `install_symlink()`
  accepts the following keyword arguments:

  <table class="table table-bordered table-sm">
    <thead>
      <tr>
        <th class="text-center text-nowrap">Name</th>
        <th class="text-center text-nowrap">Type</th>
        <th>Description</th>
      </tr>
    </thead>
    <tbody>
      <tr id="install_symlink_install_dir">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">install_dir</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  The absolute or relative path to the installation directory for the links.
If this is a relative path, it is assumed to be relative to the prefix.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="install_symlink_install_tag">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">install_tag</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  A string used by the `meson install --tags` command
to install only a subset of the files. By default these files have no install
tag which means they are not being installed when `--tags` argument is specified.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="install_symlink_pointing_to">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">pointing_to</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Target to point the link to.
Can be absolute or relative and that will be respected when creating the link.

  </td>
      </tr>
    </tbody>
  </table>

## jar()

Build a jar from the specified Java source files. Keyword arguments
are the same as [[executable]]'s, with the addition of
`main_class` which specifies the main class to execute when running
the jar with `java -jar file.jar`.

<p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- A bit of space because we remove the top margin below -->
<div style="display: flex;">
  <h3 style="margin-top: 0px;">Signature</h3>
  <div style="flex-grow: 1;"></div>
  <div class="d-flex flex-column align-items-center gap-1">

</div>
</div>

<pre><code class="language-meson"># Build a jar from the specified Java source files
[[#@jar]] jar(
  [[#@str]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                        <a href="#jar_target_name"><b>target_name</b></a>,     # The *unique* name of the build target
  [[#@str]] | [[#@file]] | [[#@custom_tgt]] | [[#@custom_idx]] | [[#@generated_list]]                                                                                                                                                                      <b>source</b>...,       # Input source to compile

  # Keyword arguments:
  <a href="#jar__lang__args"><b>&lt;lang&gt;_args</b></a>                  : [[#@array]][[[#@str]] | [[#@file]]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             # compiler flags to use for the given language; eg: `cpp_args` for C++
  <a href="#jar__lang__pch"><b>&lt;lang&gt;_pch</b></a>                   : [[#@str]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           # precompiled header file to use for the given language
  <a href="#jar_build_by_default"><b>build_by_default</b></a>             : [[#@bool]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          # Causes, when set to `true`, to have this target be built by default
  <a href="#jar_build_rpath"><b>build_rpath</b></a>                  : [[#@str]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           # A string to add to target's rpath definition in the build dir,
  <a href="#jar_build_subdir"><b>build_subdir</b></a>                 : [[#@str]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           # Places the build results in a subdirectory of the given name rather than directly into the build directory
  <a href="#jar_d_debug"><b>d_debug</b></a>                      : [[#@array]][[[#@str]]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    # The [D version identifiers](https://dlang
  <a href="#jar_d_import_dirs"><b>d_import_dirs</b></a>                : [[#@array]][[[#@inc]] | [[#@str]]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              # the directories to add to the string search path (i
  <a href="#jar_d_module_versions"><b>d_module_versions</b></a>            : [[#@array]][[[#@str]] | [[#@int]]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              # List of module version identifiers set when compiling D sources
  <a href="#jar_d_unittest"><b>d_unittest</b></a>                   : [[#@bool]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          # When set to true, the D modules are compiled in debug mode
  <a href="#jar_dependencies"><b>dependencies</b></a>                 : [[#@array]][[[#@dep]]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    # one or more dependency objects
  <a href="#jar_extra_files"><b>extra_files</b></a>                  : [[#@str]] | [[#@file]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    # Not used for the build itself but are shown as source files in IDEs
  <a href="#jar_gnu_symbol_visibility"><b>gnu_symbol_visibility</b></a>        : [[#@str]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           # Specifies how symbols should be exported, see
  <a href="#jar_gui_app"><b>gui_app</b></a>                      : [[#@bool]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          # When set to true flags this target as a GUI application
  <a href="#jar_implicit_include_directories"><b>implicit_include_directories</b></a> : [[#@bool]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          # Controls whether Meson adds the current source and build directories to the include path
  <a href="#jar_include_directories"><b>include_directories</b></a>          : [[#@array]][[[#@inc]] | [[#@str]]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              # one or more objects created with the [[include_directories]] function, or
  <a href="#jar_install"><b>install</b></a>                      : [[#@bool]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          # When set to true, all outputs of this build_target will be installed
  <a href="#jar_install_dir"><b>install_dir</b></a>                  : [[#@array]][[[#@str]] | [[#@bool]]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             # override install directory for outputs of this build target
  <a href="#jar_install_mode"><b>install_mode</b></a>                 : [[#@array]][[[#@str]] | [[#@int]]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              # Specify the file mode in symbolic format
  <a href="#jar_install_rpath"><b>install_rpath</b></a>                : [[#@str]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           # A string to set the target's rpath to after install
  <a href="#jar_install_tag"><b>install_tag</b></a>                  : [[#@str]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           # A string used by the `meson install --tags` command
  <a href="#jar_install_vala_gir"><b>install_vala_gir</b></a>             : [[#@str]] | [[#@bool]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    # If set to a string value, the vala generated gir library will be
  <a href="#jar_install_vala_header"><b>install_vala_header</b></a>          : [[#@str]] | [[#@bool]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    # If set to a string value, the vala generated header will be installed to
  <a href="#jar_install_vala_vapi"><b>install_vala_vapi</b></a>            : [[#@str]] | [[#@bool]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    # If set to a string value, the vala generated vapi library will be
  <a href="#jar_java_resources"><b>java_resources</b></a>               : [[#@structured_src]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                # Resources to be added to the jar
  <a href="#jar_link_args"><b>link_args</b></a>                    : [[#@array]][[[#@str]]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    # Flags to use during linking
  <a href="#jar_link_depends"><b>link_depends</b></a>                 : [[#@str]] | [[#@file]] | [[#@custom_tgt]] | [[#@custom_idx]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          # Strings, files, or custom targets the link step depends on
  <a href="#jar_link_early_args"><b>link_early_args</b></a>              : [[#@array]][[[#@str]]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    # Flags to use during linking
  <a href="#jar_link_language"><b>link_language</b></a>                : [[#@str]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           # Makes the linker for this target be for the specified language
  <a href="#jar_link_whole"><b>link_whole</b></a>                   : [[#@array]][[[#@lib]] | [[#@custom_tgt]] | [[#@custom_idx]]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          # Links all contents of the given static libraries whether they are used or
  <a href="#jar_link_with"><b>link_with</b></a>                    : [[#@array]][[[#@lib]] | [[#@custom_tgt]] | [[#@custom_idx]]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          # One or more shared or static libraries
  <a href="#jar_main_class"><b>main_class</b></a>                   : [[#@str]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           # Main class for running the built jar
  <a href="#jar_name_prefix"><b>name_prefix</b></a>                  : [[#@str]] | [[#@array]][[[#@void]]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             # The string that will be used as the prefix for the
  <a href="#jar_name_suffix"><b>name_suffix</b></a>                  : [[#@str]] | [[#@array]][[[#@void]]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             # The string that will be used as the extension for the
  <a href="#jar_native"><b>native</b></a>                       : [[#@bool]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          # Controls whether the target is compiled for the build or host machines
  <a href="#jar_objects"><b>objects</b></a>                      : [[#@array]][[[#@extracted_obj]] | [[#@file]] | [[#@str]]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             # List of object files that should be linked in this target
  <a href="#jar_override_options"><b>override_options</b></a>             : [[#@array]][[[#@str]]] | [[#@dict]][[[#@str]] | [[#@bool]] | [[#@int]] | [[#@array]][[[#@str]]]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                # takes an array of strings in the same format as `project`'s `default_options`
  <a href="#jar_rust_crate_type"><b>rust_crate_type</b></a>              : [[#@str]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           # Set the specific type of rust crate to compile (when compiling rust)
  <a href="#jar_rust_dependency_map"><b>rust_dependency_map</b></a>          : [[#@dict]][[[#@str]]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     # On rust targets this allows giving custom names to the crates that are
  <a href="#jar_sources"><b>sources</b></a>                      : [[#@str]] | [[#@file]] | [[#@custom_tgt]] | [[#@custom_idx]] | [[#@generated_list]] | [[#@structured_src]]                                                                                                                                                                                                        # Additional source files
  <a href="#jar_vala_gir"><b>vala_gir</b></a>                     : [[#@str]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           # If set, generates a GIR file with the given name
  <a href="#jar_vala_header"><b>vala_header</b></a>                  : [[#@str]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           # On Vala targets, this provides a way to override the name of the generated
  <a href="#jar_vala_vapi"><b>vala_vapi</b></a>                    : [[#@str]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           # On Vala targets, this provides a way to override the name of the generated
  <a href="#jar_win_subsystem"><b>win_subsystem</b></a>                : [[#@str]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           # Specifies the subsystem type to use
)</code></pre>




  <p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- A bit more space -->

  ### Arguments


  The function `jar()` accepts the following positional arguments:

  <table class="table table-bordered table-sm">
    <thead>
      <tr>
        <th class="text-center text-nowrap">Name</th>
        <th class="text-center text-nowrap">Type</th>
        <th>Description</th>
      </tr>
    </thead>
    <tbody>
      <tr id="jar_target_name">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">target_name</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  The *unique* name of the build target
  </td>
      </tr>
    </tbody>
  </table>

  <p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- Extra space -->

  Additionally, the
  function accepts between `0` and `infinity` variadic
  arguments (`source...`) of type <code><span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@file]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@custom_tgt]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@custom_idx]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@generated_list]]</span></code>.

  Input source to compile. The following types are supported:

- Strings relative to the current source directory
- [[@file]] objects defined in any preceding build file
- The return value of configure-time generators such as [[configure_file]]
- The return value of build-time generators such as
  [[custom_target]] or [[generator.process]]

These input files can be sources, objects, libraries, or any other
file. Meson will automatically categorize them based on the extension
and use them accordingly. For instance, sources (`.c`, `.cpp`,
`.vala`, `.rs`, etc) will be compiled and objects (`.o`, `.obj`) and
libraries (`.so`, `.dll`, etc) will be linked.

With the Ninja backend, Meson will create a build-time [order-only
dependency](https://ninja-build.org/manual.html#ref_dependencies) on
all generated input files, including unknown files. This is needed to
bootstrap the generation of the real dependencies in the
[depfile](https://ninja-build.org/manual.html#ref_headers) generated
by your compiler to determine when to rebuild sources. Ninja relies on
this dependency file for all input files, generated and non-generated.
The behavior is similar for other backends.


  
  <p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- Extra space -->

  Finally, `jar()`
  accepts the following keyword arguments:

  <table class="table table-bordered table-sm">
    <thead>

        <th class="text-center text-nowrap">Name</th>
        <th class="text-center text-nowrap">Type</th>
        <th>Description</th>
      </tr>
    </thead>
    <tbody>
      <tr id="jar__lang__args">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">&lt;lang&gt;_args</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@file]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  compiler flags to use for the given language; eg: `cpp_args` for C++

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="jar__lang__pch">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">&lt;lang&gt;_pch</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  precompiled header file to use for the given language
  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="jar_build_by_default">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">build_by_default</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`0.38.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@bool]]</span>
  <br><code class="language-meson text-nowrap">default = true</code>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Causes, when set to `true`, to have this target be built by default.
This means it will be built when `meson compile` is called without any
arguments. The default value is `true` for all built target types.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="jar_build_rpath">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">

  <br>{meson:since}`0.42.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>

but which will be removed on install

  </td>

      <tr id="jar_build_subdir">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">build_subdir</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`1.10.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Places the build results in a subdirectory of the given name rather than directly into the build directory. This does not affect the install directory, which uses install_dir.
This allows inserting a directory name into the build path, either when needed to use the build result while building other targets or as a way to support multiple targets with the same basename by using unique build_subdir values for each one.
To prevent collisions within the build directory, build_subdir is not allowed to match a file or directory in the source directory, nor contain '..' to refer to the parent of the build directory.
  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="jar_d_debug">

      <tr id="jar_gnu_symbol_visibility">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">gnu_symbol_visibility</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`0.48.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Specifies how symbols should be exported, see
e.g [the GCC Wiki](https://gcc.gnu.org/wiki/Visibility) for more
information. This value can either be an empty string or one of
`default`, `internal`, `hidden`, `protected` or `inlineshidden`, which
is the same as `hidden` but also includes things like C++ implicit
constructors as specified in the GCC manual. Ignored on compilers that
do not support GNU visibility arguments. See also
[`snippets.symbol_visibility_header()`](Snippets-module.md#symbol_visibility_header)
method to help with defining public API.

  </td>
      </tr>

        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">

      <tr id="jar_install_dir">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">install_dir</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@bool]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  override install directory for outputs of this build target. If the value is a
relative path, it will be considered relative the `prefix` option.
For example, if you want to install plugins into a subdir, you'd use
something like this: `install_dir : get_option('libdir') / 'projectname-1.0'`.

This can be set to an array of values to control the the installation path
of build targets with multiple outputs. Currently, that means Vala. Setting
them to `true` means "use the default", and `false` means, don't install.
By default the first element is true, and all other elements are false.

*Since 1.11.0* Passing an array is deprecated, and install_vala_header,
install_vala_vapi, and install_vala_gir keyword arguments should be used
instead.

  </td>
      </tr>

and optionally the owner/uid and group/gid for the installed files.

  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@file]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@custom_tgt]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@custom_idx]]</span>

  </td>

      <tr id="jar_link_language">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">link_language</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`0.51.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Makes the linker for this target be for the specified language.
It is generally unnecessary to set
this, as Meson will detect the right linker to use in most cases. There are
only two cases where this is needed. One, your main function in an
executable is not in the language Meson picked, or second you want to force
a library to use only one ABI.

*(broken until 0.55.0)*

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="jar_link_whole">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">link_whole</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`0.40.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@lib]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@custom_tgt]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@custom_idx]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Links all contents of the given static libraries whether they are used or
not, equivalent to the `-Wl,--whole-archive` argument flag of GCC, or the
'/WHOLEARCHIVE' MSVC linker option. This allows the linked target to
re-export symbols from all objects in the static libraries.

*(since 0.41.0)* If passed an array that array will be flattened.

*(since 0.51.0)* This argument also accepts outputs produced by
custom targets. The user must ensure that the output is a library in
the correct format.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="jar_link_with">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">link_with</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@lib]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@custom_tgt]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@custom_idx]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  One or more shared or static libraries
(built by this project) that this target should be linked with. *(since 0.41.0)* If passed an
array that array will be flattened. *(since 0.51.0)* The arguments can also be custom targets.
In this case Meson will assume that merely adding the output file in the linker command
line is sufficient to make linking work. If this is not sufficient,
then the build system writer must write all other steps manually.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="jar_main_class">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">main_class</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Main class for running the built jar
  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="jar_name_prefix">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">name_prefix</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@void]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  The string that will be used as the prefix for the
target output filename by overriding the default (only used for
libraries). By default this is `lib` on all platforms and compilers,
except for MSVC shared libraries where it is omitted to follow
convention, and Cygwin shared libraries where it is `cyg`.

Set this to `[]`, or omit the keyword argument for the default behaviour.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="jar_name_suffix">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">name_suffix</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@void]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  The string that will be used as the extension for the
target by overriding the default. By default on Windows this is
`exe` for executables and on other platforms it is omitted.

For shared libraries, the default value
is `dylib` on macOS, `dll` on Windows, and `so` everywhere else.
For static libraries, it is `a` everywhere. By convention MSVC
static libraries use the `lib` suffix, but we use `a` to avoid a
potential name clash with shared libraries which also generate
import libraries with a `lib` suffix.

Set this to `[]`, or omit the keyword argument for the default behaviour.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="jar_native">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">native</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@bool]]</span>
  <br><code class="language-meson text-nowrap">default = false</code>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Controls whether the target is compiled for the build or host machines.
  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="jar_objects">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">objects</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@extracted_obj]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@file]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  List of object files that should be linked in this target.

**Since 1.1.0** this can include generated files in addition to
object files that you don't have source to or that object files
produced by other build targets.  In earlier release, generated
object files had to be placed in `sources`.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="jar_override_options">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">override_options</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`0.40.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>] | [[@dict]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@bool]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@int]]</span> | [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>]]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  takes an array of strings in the same format as `project`'s `default_options`
overriding the values of these options
for this target only.
*(since 1.2.0)*: A dictionary may now be passed.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="jar_rust_crate_type">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">rust_crate_type</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`0.42.0`
  <br>{meson:deprecated}`1.3.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Set the specific type of rust crate to compile (when compiling rust).

If the target is an [[executable]] this defaults to "bin", the only
allowed value.

If it is a [[static_library]] it defaults to "lib", and may be "lib",
"staticlib", or "rlib". If "lib" then Rustc will pick a default, "staticlib"
means a C ABI library, "rlib" means a Rust ABI.

If it is a [[shared_library]] it defaults to "lib", and may be "lib",
"dylib", "cdylib", or "proc-macro". If "lib" then Rustc will pick a
default, "cdylib" means a C ABI library, "dylib" means a Rust ABI, and
"proc-macro" is a special rust procedural macro crate.

"proc-macro" is new in 0.62.0.

*Since 1.3.0* this is deprecated and replaced by "rust_abi" keyword argument.
`proc_macro` crates are now handled by the [`rust.proc_macro()`](Rust-module.md#proc_macro)
method.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="jar_rust_dependency_map">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">rust_dependency_map</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`1.2.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  [[@dict]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  On rust targets this allows giving custom names to the crates that are
linked into the target.  For example, passing a dependency map of
`{ 'gtk4': 'gtk' }` has the same effect of writing `gtk.package = "gtk4"`
in the `dependencies` section of a Cargo.toml file, or
`extern crate gtk4 as gtk` inside Rust code.

*Since 1.10.0*, the keys can be either crate names or target names.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="jar_sources">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">sources</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@file]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@custom_tgt]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@custom_idx]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@generated_list]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@structured_src]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Additional source files. Same as the source varargs.
  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="jar_vala_gir">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">vala_gir</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  If set, generates a GIR file with the given name. If this is unset then
no GIR file will be generated.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="jar_vala_header">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">vala_header</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  On Vala targets, this provides a way to override the name of the generated
C compatible header for targets that generate them.

If it is not set the default will be calculated from the target's name.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="jar_vala_vapi">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">vala_vapi</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  On Vala targets, this provides a way to override the name of the generated
VAPI file.

If it is not set the default will be calculated from the target's name.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="jar_win_subsystem">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">win_subsystem</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`0.56.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  <br><code class="language-meson text-nowrap">default = 'console'</code>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Specifies the subsystem type to use
on the Windows platform. Typical values include `console` for text
mode programs and `windows` for gui apps. The value can also contain
version specification such as `windows,6.0`. See [MSDN
documentation](https://docs.microsoft.com/en-us/cpp/build/reference/subsystem-specify-subsystem)
for the full list.

  </td>
      </tr>
    </tbody>
  </table>

<p style="padding: 7.5px; margin: 0px;"></p>

## join_paths()

Joins the given strings into a file system path segment. For example
`join_paths('foo', 'bar')` results in `foo/bar`. If any one of the
individual segments is an absolute path, all segments before it are
dropped. That means that `join_paths('foo', '/bar')` returns `/bar`.

*(since 0.49.0)* Using the `/` operator on strings is equivalent to calling
[[join_paths]].

```meson
# res1 and res2 will have identical values
res1 = join_paths(foo, bar)
res2 = foo / bar
```

<p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- A bit of space because we remove the top margin below -->
<div style="display: flex;">
  <h3 style="margin-top: 0px;">Signature</h3>
  <div style="flex-grow: 1;"></div>
  <div class="d-flex flex-column align-items-center gap-1">

{meson:since}`0.36.0`
</div>
</div>

<pre><code class="language-meson"># Joins the given strings into a file system path segment
[[#@str]] join_paths(
  [[#@str]]                                  <b>part</b>...,  # The path parts to join
)</code></pre>



```{warning}
Don't use [[join_paths]] for sources in [[library]] and [[executable]]. You should use [[files]] instead.
```

  <p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- A bit more space -->

  ### Arguments



  The
  function accepts between `1` and `infinity` variadic
  arguments (`part...`) of type <code><span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span></code>.

  The path parts to join.

  
  <p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- Extra space -->

## library()

Builds a library that is either static, shared or both depending on
the value of `default_library`
user [option](https://mesonbuild.com/Builtin-options.html).
You should use this instead of [[shared_library]],
[[static_library]] or
[[both_libraries]] most of the time. This allows you
to toggle your entire project (including subprojects) from shared to
static with only one option. This option applies to libraries being
built internal to the entire project. For external dependencies, the
default library type preferred is shared. This can be adapted on a per
library basis using the [[dependency]] `static` keyword.

The keyword arguments for this are the same as for
[[build_target]]

  <h3 style="margin-top: 0px;">Signature</h3>

  <div class="d-flex flex-column align-items-center gap-1">

</div>
</div>

  ### Arguments


  The function `library()` accepts the following positional arguments:

  <table class="table table-bordered table-sm">
    <thead>
      <tr>
        <th class="text-center text-nowrap">Name</th>
        <th class="text-center text-nowrap">Type</th>
        <th>Description</th>
      </tr>
    </thead>
    <tbody>
      <tr id="library_target_name">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">target_name</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  The *unique* name of the build target
  </td>
      </tr>
    </tbody>
  </table>

  <p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- Extra space -->

  Additionally, the
  function accepts between `0` and `infinity` variadic
  arguments (`source...`) of type <code><span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@file]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@custom_tgt]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@custom_idx]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@generated_list]]</span></code>.

  Input source to compile. The following types are supported:

- Strings relative to the current source directory
- [[@file]] objects defined in any preceding build file
- The return value of configure-time generators such as [[configure_file]]
- The return value of build-time generators such as
  [[custom_target]] or [[generator.process]]

These input files can be sources, objects, libraries, or any other
file. Meson will automatically categorize them based on the extension
and use them accordingly. For instance, sources (`.c`, `.cpp`,
`.vala`, `.rs`, etc) will be compiled and objects (`.o`, `.obj`) and
libraries (`.so`, `.dll`, etc) will be linked.

With the Ninja backend, Meson will create a build-time [order-only
dependency](https://ninja-build.org/manual.html#ref_dependencies) on
all generated input files, including unknown files. This is needed to
bootstrap the generation of the real dependencies in the
[depfile](https://ninja-build.org/manual.html#ref_headers) generated
by your compiler to determine when to rebuild sources. Ninja relies on
this dependency file for all input files, generated and non-generated.
The behavior is similar for other backends.


  
  <p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- Extra space -->

  Finally, `library()`
  accepts the following keyword arguments:
