  </td>
        <td>
  
  Places the build results in a subdirectory of the given name rather than directly into the build directory. This does not affect the install directory, which uses install_dir.
This allows inserting a directory name into the build path, either when needed to use the build result while building other targets or as a way to support multiple targets with the same basename by using unique build_subdir values for each one.
To prevent collisions within the build directory, build_subdir is not allowed to match a file or directory in the source directory, nor contain '..' to refer to the parent of the build directory.
  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="library_d_debug">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">

      <tr id="library_install_dir">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">install_dir</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@bool]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  override install directory for outputs of this build target. If the value is a
relative path, it will be considered relative the `prefix` option.
For example, if you want to install plugins into a subdir, you'd use
something like this: `install_dir : get_option('libdir') / 'projectname-1.0'`.

This can be set to an array of values to control the the installation path
of build targets with multiple outputs. Currently, that means Vala. Setting
them to `true` means "use the default", and `false` means, don't install.
By default the first element is true, and all other elements are false.

*Since 1.11.0* Passing an array is deprecated, and install_vala_header,
install_vala_vapi, and install_vala_gir keyword arguments should be used
instead.

  </td>
      </tr>

      <tr id="library_link_language">

  </td>
        <td class="text-center">

  </td>

      <tr id="library_link_whole">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">link_whole</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`0.40.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@lib]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@custom_tgt]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@custom_idx]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Links all contents of the given static libraries whether they are used or
not, equivalent to the `-Wl,--whole-archive` argument flag of GCC, or the
'/WHOLEARCHIVE' MSVC linker option. This allows the linked target to
re-export symbols from all objects in the static libraries.

*(since 0.41.0)* If passed an array that array will be flattened.

*(since 0.51.0)* This argument also accepts outputs produced by
custom targets. The user must ensure that the output is a library in
the correct format.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="library_link_with">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">link_with</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@lib]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@custom_tgt]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@custom_idx]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  One or more shared or static libraries
(built by this project) that this target should be linked with. *(since 0.41.0)* If passed an
array that array will be flattened. *(since 0.51.0)* The arguments can also be custom targets.

  </td>

      <tr id="library_name_prefix">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">name_prefix</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@void]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  The string that will be used as the prefix for the
target output filename by overriding the default (only used for
libraries). By default this is `lib` on all platforms and compilers,
except for MSVC shared libraries where it is omitted to follow
convention, and Cygwin shared libraries where it is `cyg`.

Set this to `[]`, or omit the keyword argument for the default behaviour.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="library_name_suffix">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">name_suffix</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@void]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  The string that will be used as the extension for the
target by overriding the default. By default on Windows this is
`exe` for executables and on other platforms it is omitted.

For shared libraries, the default value
is `dylib` on macOS, `dll` on Windows, and `so` everywhere else.
For static libraries, it is `a` everywhere. By convention MSVC
static libraries use the `lib` suffix, but we use `a` to avoid a
potential name clash with shared libraries which also generate
import libraries with a `lib` suffix.

Set this to `[]`, or omit the keyword argument for the default behaviour.

  </td>
      </tr>

      <tr id="library_rust_abi">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">rust_abi</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`1.3.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Set the specific ABI to compile (when compiling rust).
- 'rust' (default): Create a "rlib" or "dylib" crate depending on the library
  type being build.
- 'c': Create a "cdylib" or "staticlib" crate depending on the library
  type being build.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="library_rust_crate_type">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">rust_crate_type</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`0.42.0`
  <br>{meson:deprecated}`1.3.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Set the specific type of rust crate to compile (when compiling rust).

If the target is an [[executable]] this defaults to "bin", the only
allowed value.

If it is a [[static_library]] it defaults to "lib", and may be "lib",
"staticlib", or "rlib". If "lib" then Rustc will pick a default, "staticlib"
means a C ABI library, "rlib" means a Rust ABI.

If it is a [[shared_library]] it defaults to "lib", and may be "lib",
"dylib", "cdylib", or "proc-macro". If "lib" then Rustc will pick a
default, "cdylib" means a C ABI library, "dylib" means a Rust ABI, and
"proc-macro" is a special rust procedural macro crate.

"proc-macro" is new in 0.62.0.

*Since 1.3.0* this is deprecated and replaced by "rust_abi" keyword argument.
`proc_macro` crates are now handled by the [`rust.proc_macro()`](Rust-module.md#proc_macro)
method.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="library_rust_dependency_map">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">rust_dependency_map</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`1.2.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  [[@dict]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  On rust targets this allows giving custom names to the crates that are
linked into the target.  For example, passing a dependency map of
`{ 'gtk4': 'gtk' }` has the same effect of writing `gtk.package = "gtk4"`
in the `dependencies` section of a Cargo.toml file, or
`extern crate gtk4 as gtk` inside Rust code.

*Since 1.10.0*, the keys can be either crate names or target names.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="library_shortname">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">shortname</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`1.10.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  A string specifying a DLL name fitting to 8.3 limit on OS/2 of this shared library.

  </td>
      </tr>

      <tr id="library_soversion">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">soversion</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@int]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  A string or integer specifying the soversion of this shared library,
such as `0`. On Linux and Windows this is used to set the
soversion (or equivalent) in the filename. For example, if
`soversion` is `4`, a Windows DLL will be called `foo-4.dll` and one
of the aliases of the Linux shared library would be
`libfoo.so.4`. If this is not specified, the first part of `version`
is used instead (see below). For example, if `version` is `3.6.0` and
`soversion` is not defined, it is set to `3`.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="library_vala_gir">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">vala_gir</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  If set, generates a GIR file with the given name. If this is unset then
no GIR file will be generated.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="library_vala_header">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">vala_header</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  On Vala targets, this provides a way to override the name of the generated
C compatible header for targets that generate them.

If it is not set the default will be calculated from the target's name.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="library_vala_shared_args">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">vala_shared_args</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`1.3.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@file]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Arguments that are only passed to a shared library
Like `vala_args`, [[files]] is allowed in addition to string
  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="library_vala_static_args">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">vala_static_args</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`1.3.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@file]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Arguments that are only passed to a static library
Like `vala_args`, [[files]] is allowed in addition to string
  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="library_vala_vapi">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">vala_vapi</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  On Vala targets, this provides a way to override the name of the generated
VAPI file.

If it is not set the default will be calculated from the target's name.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="library_version">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">version</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  A string specifying the version of this shared library,
such as `1.1.0`. On Linux and OS X, this is used to set the shared
library version in the filename, such as `libfoo.so.1.1.0` and
`libfoo.1.1.0.dylib`. If this is not specified, `soversion` is used
instead (see above).

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="library_vs_module_defs">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">vs_module_defs</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@file]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@custom_tgt]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@custom_idx]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Specify a Microsoft module definition file for controlling symbol exports,
etc., on platforms where that is possible (e.g. Windows).

*(Since 1.3.0)* [[@custom_idx]] are supported

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="library_win_subsystem">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">win_subsystem</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`0.56.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  <br><code class="language-meson text-nowrap">default = 'console'</code>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Specifies the subsystem type to use
on the Windows platform. Typical values include `console` for text
mode programs and `windows` for gui apps. The value can also contain
version specification such as `windows,6.0`. See [MSDN
documentation](https://docs.microsoft.com/en-us/cpp/build/reference/subsystem-specify-subsystem)
for the full list.

  </td>
      </tr>
    </tbody>
  </table>

## project()

The first function called in each project, to initialize Meson.

The first argument to this function must be a string defining the name
of this project.

The project name can be any string you want, it's not used for
anything except descriptive purposes. However since it is written to
e.g. the dependency manifest it usually makes sense to have it be the
same as the project tarball or pkg-config name. So for example you
would probably want to use the name _libfoobar_ instead of _The Foobar
Library_.

It may be followed by the array of programming languages that the project uses.

*(since 0.40.0)* The array of languages is optional.

These languages may be used both for `native: false` (the default)
(host machine) targets and for `native: true` (build machine) targets.
*(since 0.56.0)* The build machine compilers for the specified
languages are not required.

Supported values for languages are `c`, `cpp` (for `C++`), `cuda`,
`cython`, `d`, `objc`, `objcpp`, `fortran`, `java`, `cs` (for `C#`),
`swift`, `nasm`, `masm`, `linearasm`, `vala` and `rust`.

<p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- A bit of space because we remove the top margin below -->
<div style="display: flex;">
  <h3 style="margin-top: 0px;">Signature</h3>
  <div style="flex-grow: 1;"></div>
  <div class="d-flex flex-column align-items-center gap-1">

</div>
</div>

<pre><code class="language-meson"># The first function called in each project, to initialize Meson
[[#@void]] project(
  [[#@str]]                                  <a href="#project_project_name"><b>project_name</b></a>,     # The name of the project
  [[#@str]]                                  <b>language</b>...,      # The languages that Meson should initialize

  # Keyword arguments:
  <a href="#project_default_options"><b>default_options</b></a> : [[#@array]][[[#@str]]] | [[#@dict]][[[#@str]] | [[#@bool]] | [[#@int]] | [[#@array]][[[#@str]]]]                                                                                                                                                                       # Accepts strings in the form `key=value`
  <a href="#project_license"><b>license</b></a>         : [[#@str]] | [[#@array]][[[#@str]]]                                                                                                                                                                                                     # Takes a string or array of strings describing the license(s) the code is under
  <a href="#project_license_files"><b>license_files</b></a>   : [[#@str]] | [[#@array]][[[#@str]]]                                                                                                                                                                                                     # Takes a string or array of strings with the paths to the license file(s)
  <a href="#project_meson_version"><b>meson_version</b></a>   : [[#@str]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                  # Takes a string describing which Meson version the project requires
  <a href="#project_subproject_dir"><b>subproject_dir</b></a>  : [[#@str]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                  # Specifies the top level directory name that holds Meson subprojects
  <a href="#project_version"><b>version</b></a>         : [[#@str]] | [[#@file]]                                                                                                                                                                                                           # A free form string describing the version of this project
)</code></pre>




  <p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- A bit more space -->

  ### Arguments


  The function `project()` accepts the following positional arguments:

  <table class="table table-bordered table-sm">
    <thead>
      <tr>
        <th class="text-center text-nowrap">Name</th>
        <th class="text-center text-nowrap">Type</th>
        <th>Description</th>
      </tr>
    </thead>
    <tbody>
      <tr id="project_project_name">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">

  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  The name of the project.
  </td>
      </tr>
    </tbody>
  </table>

  <p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- Extra space -->

  Additionally, the
  function accepts between `0` and `infinity` variadic
  arguments (`language...`) of type <code><span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span></code>.

  The languages that Meson should initialize.

  
  <p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- Extra space -->

  Finally, `project()`
  accepts the following keyword arguments:

  <table class="table table-bordered table-sm">
    <thead>
      <tr>
        <th class="text-center text-nowrap">Name</th>
        <th class="text-center text-nowrap">Type</th>
        <th>Description</th>
      </tr>
    </thead>
    <tbody>
      <tr id="project_default_options">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">default_options</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>] | [[@dict]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@bool]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@int]]</span> | [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>]]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Accepts strings in the form `key=value`
which have the same format as options to `meson configure`.
For example to set the default project type you would
set this: `default_options : ['buildtype=debugoptimized']`. Note
that these settings are only used when running Meson for the first
time.

Note that some options can override the default behavior;
for example, using `c_args` here means that the `CFLAGS`
environment variable is not used. Consider using
[[add_project_arguments()]] instead.

Also note that not all options are taken into account when
building as a subproject, and the exact set of options
that are per-subproject has increased over time; for more
information, see [core options](Builtin-options.md#core-options)
and [compiler options](Builtin-options.md#compiler-options).

*(since 1.2.0)*: A dictionary may now be passed.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="project_license">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">license</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Takes a string or array of strings describing the license(s) the code is under.

This *should* be an [SPDX license expression](https://spdx.dev/ids/),
using the standardized license identifier from the [SPDX license
list](https://spdx.org/licenses/).  Usually this would be something like
`license : 'GPL-2.0-or-later'`. If there are multiple licenses you can use the
`AND` and `OR` operators to join them: `license : 'Apache-2.0 OR GPL-2.0'`.

For backwards compatibility reasons you can also pass an array of
licenses here. This is not recommended, as it is ambiguous: `license :
['Apache-2.0', 'GPL-2.0-only']` instead use an SPDX expression: `license
: 'Apache-2.0 OR GPL-2.0-only'`, which makes it clear that the license
mean OR, not AND.

Note that the text is informal and is only written to the dependency
manifest. Meson does not do any license validation, you are responsible
for verifying that you abide by all licensing terms. You can access the
value in your Meson build files with `meson.project_license()`.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="project_license_files">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">license_files</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`1.1.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Takes a string or array of strings with the paths to the license file(s)
the code is under.

This enhances the value of the `license` kwarg by allowing to specify both
the short license name and the full license text. Usually this would be
something like `license_files: ['COPYING']`.

Note that the files are informal and are only installed with the dependency
manifest. Meson does not do any license validation, you are responsible
for verifying that you abide by all licensing terms. You can access the
value in your Meson build files with [[meson.project_license_files]].

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="project_meson_version">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">meson_version</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Takes a string describing which Meson version the project requires.
Usually something like `>=0.28.0`.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="project_subproject_dir">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">subproject_dir</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  <br><code class="language-meson text-nowrap">default = 'subprojects'</code>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Specifies the top level directory name that holds Meson subprojects.
This is only meant as a compatibility option
for existing code bases that house their embedded source code in a
custom directory. All new projects should not set this but instead
use the default value. It should be noted that this keyword
argument is ignored inside subprojects. There can be only one
subproject dir and it is set in the top level Meson file.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="project_version">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">version</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@file]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  A free form string describing the version of this project.
You can access the value in your Meson build files
with [[meson.project_version]]. *(Since 0.57.0)* this can also be a
[[@file]] object pointing to a file that contains exactly one line of
text.

  </td>
      </tr>
    </tbody>
  </table>

<p style="padding: 7.5px; margin: 0px;"></p>

## range()

Return an opaque object that can be only be used in `foreach` statements.

<pre><code class="language-meson">[[@range]] range([[@int]] <b>stop</b>)
[[@range]] range([[@int]] <b>start</b>, [[@int]] <b>stop</b>[, [[@int]] <b>step</b>])</code></pre>

- `start` must be integer greater or equal to 0. Defaults to 0.
- `stop` must be integer greater or equal to `start`.
- `step` must be integer greater or equal to 1. Defaults to 1.

It cause the `foreach` loop to be called with the value from `start` included
to `stop` excluded with an increment of `step` after each loop.

<p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- A bit of space because we remove the top margin below -->
<div style="display: flex;">
  <h3 style="margin-top: 0px;">Signature</h3>
  <div style="flex-grow: 1;"></div>
  <div class="d-flex flex-column align-items-center gap-1">

{meson:since}`0.58.0`
</div>
</div>

<pre><code class="language-meson"># Return an opaque object that can be only be used in `foreach` statements
[[#@range]] range(
  [[#@int]]                                  [<a href="#range_start"><b>start</b></a>],   # The start of the range
  [[#@int]]                                  [<a href="#range_stop"><b>stop</b></a>],    # The end of the range
  [[#@int]]                                  [<a href="#range_step"><b>step</b></a>],    # The loop increment
)</code></pre>

  <p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- A bit more space -->

  ### Example

```meson
# Loop 15 times with i from 0 to 14 included.
foreach i : range(15)
  ...
endforeach
```

The range object can also be assigned to a variable and indexed.
```meson
r = range(5, 10, 2)
assert(r[2] == 9)
```




  <p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- A bit more space -->

  ### Arguments


  The function `range()` accepts the following positional arguments:

  <table class="table table-bordered table-sm">
    <thead>
      <tr>
        <th class="text-center text-nowrap">Name</th>
        <th class="text-center text-nowrap">Type</th>
        <th>Description</th>
      </tr>
    </thead>
    <tbody>
      <tr id="range_start">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">start</code>
  <br>{meson:optional}` `
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@int]]</span>
  <br><code class="language-meson text-nowrap">default = 0</code>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  The start of the range
  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="range_stop">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">stop</code>
  <br>{meson:optional}` `
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@int]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  The end of the range
  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="range_step">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">step</code>
  <br>{meson:optional}` `
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@int]]</span>
  <br><code class="language-meson text-nowrap">default = 1</code>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  The loop increment
  </td>
      </tr>
    </tbody>
  </table>

  <p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- Extra space -->



<p style="padding: 7.5px; margin: 0px;"></p>

<p style="padding: 7.5px; margin: 0px;"></p>

## run_command()

Runs the command specified in positional arguments. `command` can be a
string, or the output of [[find_program]],
[[files]] or [[configure_file]], or [a
compiler object](#compiler-object).

Returns a [[@runresult]] object containing the result
of the invocation. The command is run from an *unspecified* directory,
and Meson will set three environment variables `MESON_SOURCE_ROOT`,
`MESON_BUILD_ROOT` and `MESON_SUBDIR` that specify the source
directory, build directory and subdirectory the target was defined in,
respectively.

See also [External commands](External-commands.md).

<p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- A bit of space because we remove the top margin below -->
<div style="display: flex;">
  <h3 style="margin-top: 0px;">Signature</h3>
  <div style="flex-grow: 1;"></div>
  <div class="d-flex flex-column align-items-center gap-1">

</div>
</div>

<pre><code class="language-meson"># Runs the command specified in positional arguments
[[#@runresult]] run_command(
  [[#@str]] | [[#@file]] | [[#@program]]                                                                                                    <b>command</b>...,  # The command to execute during the setup process

  # Keyword arguments:
  <a href="#run_command_capture"><b>capture</b></a> : [[#@bool]]                                                                                                                             # If `true`, any output generated on stdout will be captured and returned by
  <a href="#run_command_check"><b>check</b></a>   : [[#@bool]]                                                                                                                             # If `true`, the exit status code of the command will be checked,
  <a href="#run_command_console"><b>console</b></a> : [[#@bool]]                                                                                                                             # stdout and stderr are written to console as it is generated by the command
  <a href="#run_command_env"><b>env</b></a>     : [[#@env]] | [[#@array]][[[#@str]]] | [[#@dict]][[[#@str]]]                                                                                                     # environment variables to set,
)</code></pre>




  <p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- A bit more space -->

  ### Arguments



  The
  function accepts between `0` and `infinity` variadic
  arguments (`command...`) of type <code><span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@file]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@program]]</span></code>.

  The command to execute during the setup process.

  
  <p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- Extra space -->

  The function `run_command()`
  accepts the following keyword arguments:

  <table class="table table-bordered table-sm">
    <thead>
      <tr>
        <th class="text-center text-nowrap">Name</th>
        <th class="text-center text-nowrap">Type</th>
        <th>Description</th>
      </tr>
    </thead>
    <tbody>
      <tr id="run_command_capture">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">capture</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`0.47.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@bool]]</span>
  <br><code class="language-meson text-nowrap">default = true</code>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  If `true`, any output generated on stdout will be captured and returned by
the `.stdout()` method. If it is false, then `.stdout()` will return an
empty string.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="run_command_check">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">check</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`0.47.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@bool]]</span>
  <br><code class="language-meson text-nowrap">default = false</code>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  If `true`, the exit status code of the command will be checked,
and the configuration will fail if it is non-zero. Note that
the default value will be `true` in future releases.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="run_command_console">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">console</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`1.11.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@bool]]</span>
  <br><code class="language-meson text-nowrap">default = false</code>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  stdout and stderr are written to console as it is generated by the command.
Meant for commands that are resource-intensive and take a long time to finish.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="run_command_env">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">env</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`0.50.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@env]]</span> | [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>] | [[@dict]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  environment variables to set,
such as `['NAME1=value1', 'NAME2=value2']`,
or an [[@env]] object which allows more sophisticated
environment juggling. *(Since 0.52.0)* A dictionary is also accepted.

  </td>
      </tr>
    </tbody>
  </table>

## run_target()

This function creates a new top-level target that runs a specified
command with the specified arguments. Like all top-level targets, this
integrates with the selected backend. For instance, you can run it as
`meson compile target_name`. Note that a run target produces no output
as far as Meson is concerned. It is only meant for tasks such as
running a code formatter or flashing an external device's firmware
with a built file.

The command is run from an *unspecified* directory, and Meson will set
three environment variables `MESON_SOURCE_ROOT`, `MESON_BUILD_ROOT`
and `MESON_SUBDIR` that specify the source directory, build directory
and subdirectory the target was defined in, respectively.

*Since 0.57.0* The template strings passed to `command` keyword arguments accept
the following special substitutions:
- `@SOURCE_ROOT@`: the path to the root of the source tree. Depending on the backend,
  this may be an absolute or a relative to current workdir path.
- `@BUILD_ROOT@`: the path to the root of the build tree. Depending on the backend,
  this may be an absolute or a relative to current workdir path.
- `@CURRENT_SOURCE_DIR@` *Since 0.57.1*: this is the directory where the currently
  processed meson.build is located in. Depending on the backend,
  this may be an absolute or a relative to current workdir path.

<p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- A bit of space because we remove the top margin below -->
<div style="display: flex;">
  <h3 style="margin-top: 0px;">Signature</h3>
  <div style="flex-grow: 1;"></div>
  <div class="d-flex flex-column align-items-center gap-1">

</div>
</div>

## set_variable()

Assigns a value to the given variable name. Calling
`set_variable('foo', bar)` is equivalent to `foo = bar`.

*(since 0.46.1)* The `value` parameter can be an array type.

<p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- A bit of space because we remove the top margin below -->

  [[#@any]]                                  <a href="#set_variable_value"><b>value</b></a>,             # The value to set the variable to
)</code></pre>




  <p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- A bit more space -->

  <a href="#shared_library_win_subsystem"><b>win_subsystem</b></a>                : [[#@str]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           # Specifies the subsystem type to use
)</code></pre>




  <p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- A bit more space -->

  ### Arguments


  The function `shared_library()` accepts the following positional arguments:

  <table class="table table-bordered table-sm">
    <thead>
      <tr>
        <th class="text-center text-nowrap">Name</th>
        <th class="text-center text-nowrap">Type</th>
        <th>Description</th>
      </tr>
    </thead>
    <tbody>
      <tr id="shared_library_target_name">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">target_name</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  The *unique* name of the build target
  </td>
      </tr>
    </tbody>
  </table>

  <p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- Extra space -->

  Additionally, the
  function accepts between `0` and `infinity` variadic
  arguments (`source...`) of type <code><span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@file]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@custom_tgt]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@custom_idx]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@generated_list]]</span></code>.

  Input source to compile. The following types are supported:

- Strings relative to the current source directory
- [[@file]] objects defined in any preceding build file
- The return value of configure-time generators such as [[configure_file]]
- The return value of build-time generators such as
  [[custom_target]] or [[generator.process]]

These input files can be sources, objects, libraries, or any other
file. Meson will automatically categorize them based on the extension
and use them accordingly. For instance, sources (`.c`, `.cpp`,
`.vala`, `.rs`, etc) will be compiled and objects (`.o`, `.obj`) and
libraries (`.so`, `.dll`, etc) will be linked.

With the Ninja backend, Meson will create a build-time [order-only
dependency](https://ninja-build.org/manual.html#ref_dependencies) on
all generated input files, including unknown files. This is needed to
bootstrap the generation of the real dependencies in the
[depfile](https://ninja-build.org/manual.html#ref_headers) generated
by your compiler to determine when to rebuild sources. Ninja relies on
this dependency file for all input files, generated and non-generated.
The behavior is similar for other backends.


  
  <p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- Extra space -->

  Finally, `shared_library()`
  accepts the following keyword arguments:

  <table class="table table-bordered table-sm">
    <thead>
      <tr>
        <th class="text-center text-nowrap">Name</th>
        <th class="text-center text-nowrap">Type</th>
        <th>Description</th>
      </tr>
    </thead>
    <tbody>
      <tr id="shared_library__lang__args">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">&lt;lang&gt;_args</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@file]]</span>]

  <br>{meson:since}`1.10.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Places the build results in a subdirectory of the given name rather than directly into the build directory. This does not affect the install directory, which uses install_dir.
This allows inserting a directory name into the build path, either when needed to use the build result while building other targets or as a way to support multiple targets with the same basename by using unique build_subdir values for each one.
To prevent collisions within the build directory, build_subdir is not allowed to match a file or directory in the source directory, nor contain '..' to refer to the parent of the build directory.
  </td>

      <tr id="shared_library_install_dir">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">install_dir</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@bool]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  override install directory for outputs of this build target. If the value is a
relative path, it will be considered relative the `prefix` option.
For example, if you want to install plugins into a subdir, you'd use
something like this: `install_dir : get_option('libdir') / 'projectname-1.0'`.

This can be set to an array of values to control the the installation path
of build targets with multiple outputs. Currently, that means Vala. Setting
them to `true` means "use the default", and `false` means, don't install.
By default the first element is true, and all other elements are false.

*Since 1.11.0* Passing an array is deprecated, and install_vala_header,
install_vala_vapi, and install_vala_gir keyword arguments should be used
instead.

  </td>
      </tr>

      <tr id="shared_library_link_language">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">

  <br>{meson:since}`0.51.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  </td>

      <tr id="shared_library_link_whole">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">link_whole</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`0.40.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@lib]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@custom_tgt]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@custom_idx]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Links all contents of the given static libraries whether they are used or
not, equivalent to the `-Wl,--whole-archive` argument flag of GCC, or the
'/WHOLEARCHIVE' MSVC linker option. This allows the linked target to
re-export symbols from all objects in the static libraries.

*(since 0.41.0)* If passed an array that array will be flattened.

*(since 0.51.0)* This argument also accepts outputs produced by
custom targets. The user must ensure that the output is a library in
the correct format.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="shared_library_link_with">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">link_with</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@lib]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@custom_tgt]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@custom_idx]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  One or more shared or static libraries
(built by this project) that this target should be linked with. *(since 0.41.0)* If passed an
array that array will be flattened. *(since 0.51.0)* The arguments can also be custom targets.
In this case Meson will assume that merely adding the output file in the linker command
line is sufficient to make linking work. If this is not sufficient,

  </td>

      <tr id="shared_library_name_prefix">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">name_prefix</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@void]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  The string that will be used as the prefix for the
target output filename by overriding the default (only used for
libraries). By default this is `lib` on all platforms and compilers,
except for MSVC shared libraries where it is omitted to follow
convention, and Cygwin shared libraries where it is `cyg`.

Set this to `[]`, or omit the keyword argument for the default behaviour.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="shared_library_name_suffix">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">name_suffix</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@void]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  The string that will be used as the extension for the
target by overriding the default. By default on Windows this is
`exe` for executables and on other platforms it is omitted.

For shared libraries, the default value
is `dylib` on macOS, `dll` on Windows, and `so` everywhere else.
For static libraries, it is `a` everywhere. By convention MSVC
static libraries use the `lib` suffix, but we use `a` to avoid a
potential name clash with shared libraries which also generate
import libraries with a `lib` suffix.

Set this to `[]`, or omit the keyword argument for the default behaviour.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="shared_library_native">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">

  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>] | [[@dict]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@bool]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@int]]</span> | [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>]]

      <tr id="shared_library_rust_abi">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">rust_abi</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`1.3.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Set the specific ABI to compile (when compiling rust).
- 'rust' (default): Create a "dylib" crate.
- 'c': Create a "cdylib" crate.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="shared_library_rust_crate_type">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">rust_crate_type</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`0.42.0`
  <br>{meson:deprecated}`1.3.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Set the specific type of rust crate to compile (when compiling rust).

If the target is an [[executable]] this defaults to "bin", the only
allowed value.

If it is a [[static_library]] it defaults to "lib", and may be "lib",
"staticlib", or "rlib". If "lib" then Rustc will pick a default, "staticlib"
means a C ABI library, "rlib" means a Rust ABI.

If it is a [[shared_library]] it defaults to "lib", and may be "lib",
"dylib", "cdylib", or "proc-macro". If "lib" then Rustc will pick a
default, "cdylib" means a C ABI library, "dylib" means a Rust ABI, and
"proc-macro" is a special rust procedural macro crate.

"proc-macro" is new in 0.62.0.

*Since 1.3.0* this is deprecated and replaced by "rust_abi" keyword argument.
`proc_macro` crates are now handled by the [`rust.proc_macro()`](Rust-module.md#proc_macro)
method.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="shared_library_rust_dependency_map">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">rust_dependency_map</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`1.2.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  [[@dict]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  On rust targets this allows giving custom names to the crates that are
linked into the target.  For example, passing a dependency map of
`{ 'gtk4': 'gtk' }` has the same effect of writing `gtk.package = "gtk4"`
in the `dependencies` section of a Cargo.toml file, or
`extern crate gtk4 as gtk` inside Rust code.

*Since 1.10.0*, the keys can be either crate names or target names.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="shared_library_shortname">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">shortname</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`1.10.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  A string specifying a DLL name fitting to 8.3 limit on OS/2 of this shared library.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="shared_library_sources">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">sources</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@file]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@custom_tgt]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@custom_idx]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@generated_list]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@structured_src]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Additional source files. Same as the source varargs.
  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="shared_library_soversion">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">soversion</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@int]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  A string or integer specifying the soversion of this shared library,
such as `0`. On Linux and Windows this is used to set the
soversion (or equivalent) in the filename. For example, if
`soversion` is `4`, a Windows DLL will be called `foo-4.dll` and one
of the aliases of the Linux shared library would be
`libfoo.so.4`. If this is not specified, the first part of `version`
is used instead (see below). For example, if `version` is `3.6.0` and
`soversion` is not defined, it is set to `3`.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="shared_library_vala_gir">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">vala_gir</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  If set, generates a GIR file with the given name. If this is unset then
no GIR file will be generated.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="shared_library_vala_header">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">vala_header</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  On Vala targets, this provides a way to override the name of the generated
C compatible header for targets that generate them.

If it is not set the default will be calculated from the target's name.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="shared_library_vala_vapi">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">vala_vapi</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  On Vala targets, this provides a way to override the name of the generated
VAPI file.

If it is not set the default will be calculated from the target's name.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="shared_library_version">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">version</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  A string specifying the version of this shared library,
such as `1.1.0`. On Linux and OS X, this is used to set the shared
library version in the filename, such as `libfoo.so.1.1.0` and
`libfoo.1.1.0.dylib`. If this is not specified, `soversion` is used
instead (see above).

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="shared_library_vs_module_defs">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">vs_module_defs</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@file]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@custom_tgt]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@custom_idx]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Specify a Microsoft module definition file for controlling symbol exports,
etc., on platforms where that is possible (e.g. Windows).

*(Since 1.3.0)* [[@custom_idx]] are supported

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="shared_library_win_subsystem">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">win_subsystem</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`0.56.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  <br><code class="language-meson text-nowrap">default = 'console'</code>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Specifies the subsystem type to use
on the Windows platform. Typical values include `console` for text
mode programs and `windows` for gui apps. The value can also contain
version specification such as `windows,6.0`. See [MSDN
documentation](https://docs.microsoft.com/en-us/cpp/build/reference/subsystem-specify-subsystem)
for the full list.

  </td>
      </tr>
    </tbody>
  </table>

<p style="padding: 7.5px; margin: 0px;"></p>

---
<p style="padding: 7.5px; margin: 0px;"></p>

## shared_module()

Builds a shared module with the given sources.

This is useful for building modules that will be `dlopen()`ed and
hence may contain undefined symbols that will be provided by the
library that is loading it.

If you want the shared module to be able to refer to functions and
variables defined in the [[executable]] it is loaded by,
you will need to set the `export_dynamic` argument of the executable to
`true`.

<p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- A bit of space because we remove the top margin below -->
<div style="display: flex;">
  <h3 style="margin-top: 0px;">Signature</h3>
  <div style="flex-grow: 1;"></div>
  <div class="d-flex flex-column align-items-center gap-1">

{meson:since}`0.37.0`
</div>
</div>

  <a href="#shared_module_vala_vapi"><b>vala_vapi</b></a>                    : [[#@str]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           # On Vala targets, this provides a way to override the name of the generated
  <a href="#shared_module_vs_module_defs"><b>vs_module_defs</b></a>               : [[#@str]] | [[#@file]] | [[#@custom_tgt]] | [[#@custom_idx]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          # Specify a Microsoft module definition file for controlling symbol exports,
  <a href="#shared_module_win_subsystem"><b>win_subsystem</b></a>                : [[#@str]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           # Specifies the subsystem type to use
)</code></pre>


```{note}
*Linking to a shared module on platforms other than Android is deprecated, and will be an error
in the future*.
It was previously allowed because it was the only way to have a shared-library-like target that
contained references to undefined symbols. However, since 0.40.0, the `override_options:`
[[build_target]] keyword argument can be used to create such a [[shared_library]] by passing
`override_options: 'b_lundef=false'`. Shared modules have other characteristics that make
them incompatible with linking, such as a lack of SONAME.
On macOS and iOS, linking to shared modules is disallowed by the linker, so we disallow it at
configure time.
On Android, if a shared module `foo` uses symbols from another shared module `bar`, `foo` must
also be linked to `bar`. Hence, linking one shared module to another will always be allowed when
building for Android.

```


  <p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- A bit more space -->

  ### Arguments


  The function `shared_module()` accepts the following positional arguments:

  <table class="table table-bordered table-sm">
    <thead>
      <tr>
        <th class="text-center text-nowrap">Name</th>
        <th class="text-center text-nowrap">Type</th>
        <th>Description</th>
      </tr>
    </thead>
    <tbody>
      <tr id="shared_module_target_name">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">target_name</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  The *unique* name of the build target
  </td>
      </tr>
    </tbody>
  </table>

  <p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- Extra space -->

  Additionally, the
  function accepts between `0` and `infinity` variadic
  arguments (`source...`) of type <code><span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@file]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@custom_tgt]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@custom_idx]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@generated_list]]</span></code>.

  Input source to compile. The following types are supported:

- Strings relative to the current source directory
- [[@file]] objects defined in any preceding build file
- The return value of configure-time generators such as [[configure_file]]
- The return value of build-time generators such as
  [[custom_target]] or [[generator.process]]

These input files can be sources, objects, libraries, or any other
file. Meson will automatically categorize them based on the extension
and use them accordingly. For instance, sources (`.c`, `.cpp`,
`.vala`, `.rs`, etc) will be compiled and objects (`.o`, `.obj`) and
libraries (`.so`, `.dll`, etc) will be linked.

With the Ninja backend, Meson will create a build-time [order-only
dependency](https://ninja-build.org/manual.html#ref_dependencies) on
all generated input files, including unknown files. This is needed to
bootstrap the generation of the real dependencies in the
[depfile](https://ninja-build.org/manual.html#ref_headers) generated
by your compiler to determine when to rebuild sources. Ninja relies on
this dependency file for all input files, generated and non-generated.
The behavior is similar for other backends.


  
  <p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- Extra space -->

  Finally, `shared_module()`
  accepts the following keyword arguments:

  <table class="table table-bordered table-sm">
    <thead>
      <tr>
        <th class="text-center text-nowrap">Name</th>
        <th class="text-center text-nowrap">Type</th>
        <th>Description</th>
      </tr>
    </thead>
    <tbody>
      <tr id="shared_module__lang__args">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">&lt;lang&gt;_args</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@file]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  compiler flags to use for the given language; eg: `cpp_args` for C++

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="shared_module__lang__pch">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">&lt;lang&gt;_pch</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  precompiled header file to use for the given language
  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="shared_module_build_by_default">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">build_by_default</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`0.38.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@bool]]</span>
  <br><code class="language-meson text-nowrap">default = true</code>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Causes, when set to `true`, to have this target be built by default.
This means it will be built when `meson compile` is called without any
arguments. The default value is `true` for all built target types.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="shared_module_build_rpath">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">build_rpath</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`0.42.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  A string to add to target's rpath definition in the build dir,
but which will be removed on install

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="shared_module_build_subdir">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">build_subdir</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`1.10.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Places the build results in a subdirectory of the given name rather than directly into the build directory. This does not affect the install directory, which uses install_dir.
This allows inserting a directory name into the build path, either when needed to use the build result while building other targets or as a way to support multiple targets with the same basename by using unique build_subdir values for each one.
To prevent collisions within the build directory, build_subdir is not allowed to match a file or directory in the source directory, nor contain '..' to refer to the parent of the build directory.
  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="shared_module_d_debug">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">d_debug</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  The [D version identifiers](https://dlang.org/spec/version.html#version) to add
during the compilation of D source files.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="shared_module_d_import_dirs">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">d_import_dirs</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`0.62.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@inc]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  the directories to add to the string search path (i.e. `-J` switch for DMD).
Must be objects created with the [[include_directories]] function or plain
strings, which will be implicitly converted into [[@inc]] objects.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="shared_module_d_module_versions">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">d_module_versions</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@int]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  List of module version identifiers set when compiling D sources.
  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="shared_module_d_unittest">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">d_unittest</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@bool]]</span>
  <br><code class="language-meson text-nowrap">default = false</code>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  When set to true, the D modules are compiled in debug mode.
  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="shared_module_dependencies">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">dependencies</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@dep]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  one or more dependency objects
created with
[[dependency]] or [[compiler.find_library]]
(for external deps) or [[declare_dependency]]
(for deps built by the project)

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="shared_module_extra_files">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">extra_files</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@file]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Not used for the build itself but are shown as source files in IDEs
that group files by targets (such as Visual Studio).

These may only be static sources.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="shared_module_gnu_symbol_visibility">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">gnu_symbol_visibility</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`0.48.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Specifies how symbols should be exported, see
e.g [the GCC Wiki](https://gcc.gnu.org/wiki/Visibility) for more
information. This value can either be an empty string or one of
`default`, `internal`, `hidden`, `protected` or `inlineshidden`, which
is the same as `hidden` but also includes things like C++ implicit
constructors as specified in the GCC manual. Ignored on compilers that
do not support GNU visibility arguments. See also
[`snippets.symbol_visibility_header()`](Snippets-module.md#symbol_visibility_header)
method to help with defining public API.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="shared_module_gui_app">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">gui_app</code>
  <br>{meson:deprecated}`0.56.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@bool]]</span>
  <br><code class="language-meson text-nowrap">default = false</code>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  When set to true flags this target as a GUI application
on platforms where this makes a difference, **deprecated** since
0.56.0, use `win_subsystem` instead.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="shared_module_implicit_include_directories">

  </td>

  </td>

      <tr id="shared_module_include_directories">

      <tr id="shared_module_install">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">install</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@bool]]</span>
  <br><code class="language-meson text-nowrap">default = false</code>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  When set to true, all outputs of this build_target will be installed.
  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="shared_module_install_dir">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">install_dir</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@bool]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  override install directory for outputs of this build target. If the value is a
relative path, it will be considered relative the `prefix` option.
For example, if you want to install plugins into a subdir, you'd use
something like this: `install_dir : get_option('libdir') / 'projectname-1.0'`.

This can be set to an array of values to control the the installation path
of build targets with multiple outputs. Currently, that means Vala. Setting
them to `true` means "use the default", and `false` means, don't install.
By default the first element is true, and all other elements are false.

*Since 1.11.0* Passing an array is deprecated, and install_vala_header,
install_vala_vapi, and install_vala_gir keyword arguments should be used
instead.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="shared_module_install_mode">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">install_mode</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`0.47.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@int]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Specify the file mode in symbolic format
and optionally the owner/uid and group/gid for the installed files.

See the `install_mode` kwarg of [[install_data]] for more information.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="shared_module_install_rpath">

  </td>

such as a symbol visibility map. The purpose is to
automatically trigger a re-link (but not a re-compile) of the target
when this file changes.

  </td>

      <tr id="shared_module_link_early_args">

  </td>
        <td>

except that they are inserted into the linker command line
before any object files or libraries. You can use UNIX-style

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="shared_module_link_language">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">

  <br>{meson:since}`0.51.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Makes the linker for this target be for the specified language.
It is generally unnecessary to set
this, as Meson will detect the right linker to use in most cases. There are
only two cases where this is needed. One, your main function in an
executable is not in the language Meson picked, or second you want to force
a library to use only one ABI.

*(broken until 0.55.0)*

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="shared_module_link_whole">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">

  <br>{meson:since}`0.40.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">

  </td>
        <td>

*(since 0.41.0)* If passed an array that array will be flattened.

*(since 0.51.0)* This argument also accepts outputs produced by
custom targets. The user must ensure that the output is a library in
the correct format.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="shared_module_link_with">

  <code class="language-meson">link_with</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@lib]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@custom_tgt]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@custom_idx]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  One or more shared or static libraries
(built by this project) that this target should be linked with. *(since 0.41.0)* If passed an
array that array will be flattened. *(since 0.51.0)* The arguments can also be custom targets.
In this case Meson will assume that merely adding the output file in the linker command
line is sufficient to make linking work. If this is not sufficient,
then the build system writer must write all other steps manually.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="shared_module_name_prefix">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">name_prefix</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@void]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  The string that will be used as the prefix for the
target output filename by overriding the default (only used for
libraries). By default this is `lib` on all platforms and compilers,
except for MSVC shared libraries where it is omitted to follow
convention, and Cygwin shared libraries where it is `cyg`.

Set this to `[]`, or omit the keyword argument for the default behaviour.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="shared_module_name_suffix">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">name_suffix</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@void]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  The string that will be used as the extension for the
target by overriding the default. By default on Windows this is
`exe` for executables and on other platforms it is omitted.

For shared libraries, the default value
is `dylib` on macOS, `dll` on Windows, and `so` everywhere else.
For static libraries, it is `a` everywhere. By convention MSVC
static libraries use the `lib` suffix, but we use `a` to avoid a
potential name clash with shared libraries which also generate
import libraries with a `lib` suffix.

Set this to `[]`, or omit the keyword argument for the default behaviour.

  </td>
      </tr>

  </td>

  </td>

      <tr id="shared_module_rust_crate_type">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">rust_crate_type</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`0.42.0`
  <br>{meson:deprecated}`1.3.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Set the specific type of rust crate to compile (when compiling rust).

If the target is an [[executable]] this defaults to "bin", the only
allowed value.

If it is a [[static_library]] it defaults to "lib", and may be "lib",
"staticlib", or "rlib". If "lib" then Rustc will pick a default, "staticlib"
means a C ABI library, "rlib" means a Rust ABI.

If it is a [[shared_library]] it defaults to "lib", and may be "lib",
"dylib", "cdylib", or "proc-macro". If "lib" then Rustc will pick a
default, "cdylib" means a C ABI library, "dylib" means a Rust ABI, and
"proc-macro" is a special rust procedural macro crate.

"proc-macro" is new in 0.62.0.

*Since 1.3.0* this is deprecated and replaced by "rust_abi" keyword argument.
`proc_macro` crates are now handled by the [`rust.proc_macro()`](Rust-module.md#proc_macro)
method.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="shared_module_rust_dependency_map">

  </td>

  </td>
        <td>
  
  On rust targets this allows giving custom names to the crates that are
linked into the target.  For example, passing a dependency map of
`{ 'gtk4': 'gtk' }` has the same effect of writing `gtk.package = "gtk4"`
in the `dependencies` section of a Cargo.toml file, or
`extern crate gtk4 as gtk` inside Rust code.

*Since 1.10.0*, the keys can be either crate names or target names.

  </td>
      </tr>
