<p style="padding: 7.5px; margin: 0px;"></p>

## static_library()

Builds a static library with the given sources.

<p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- A bit of space because we remove the top margin below -->
<div style="display: flex;">
  <h3 style="margin-top: 0px;">Signature</h3>
  <div style="flex-grow: 1;"></div>
  <div class="d-flex flex-column align-items-center gap-1">

</div>
</div>

<pre><code class="language-meson"># Builds a static library with the given sources
[[#@lib]] static_library(
  [[#@str]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                        <a href="#static_library_target_name"><b>target_name</b></a>,     # The *unique* name of the build target
  [[#@str]] | [[#@file]] | [[#@custom_tgt]] | [[#@custom_idx]] | [[#@generated_list]]                                                                                                                                                                      <b>source</b>...,       # Input source to compile

  # Keyword arguments:
  <a href="#static_library__lang__args"><b>&lt;lang&gt;_args</b></a>                  : [[#@array]][[[#@str]] | [[#@file]]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             # compiler flags to use for the given language; eg: `cpp_args` for C++
  <a href="#static_library__lang__pch"><b>&lt;lang&gt;_pch</b></a>                   : [[#@str]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           # precompiled header file to use for the given language
  <a href="#static_library_build_by_default"><b>build_by_default</b></a>             : [[#@bool]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          # Causes, when set to `true`, to have this target be built by default
  <a href="#static_library_build_rpath"><b>build_rpath</b></a>                  : [[#@str]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           # A string to add to target's rpath definition in the build dir,
  <a href="#static_library_build_subdir"><b>build_subdir</b></a>                 : [[#@str]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           # Places the build results in a subdirectory of the given name rather than directly into the build directory
  <a href="#static_library_d_debug"><b>d_debug</b></a>                      : [[#@array]][[[#@str]]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    # The [D version identifiers](https://dlang
  <a href="#static_library_d_import_dirs"><b>d_import_dirs</b></a>                : [[#@array]][[[#@inc]] | [[#@str]]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              # the directories to add to the string search path (i
  <a href="#static_library_d_module_versions"><b>d_module_versions</b></a>            : [[#@array]][[[#@str]] | [[#@int]]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              # List of module version identifiers set when compiling D sources
  <a href="#static_library_d_unittest"><b>d_unittest</b></a>                   : [[#@bool]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          # When set to true, the D modules are compiled in debug mode
  <a href="#static_library_dependencies"><b>dependencies</b></a>                 : [[#@array]][[[#@dep]]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    # one or more dependency objects
  <a href="#static_library_extra_files"><b>extra_files</b></a>                  : [[#@str]] | [[#@file]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    # Not used for the build itself but are shown as source files in IDEs
  <a href="#static_library_gnu_symbol_visibility"><b>gnu_symbol_visibility</b></a>        : [[#@str]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           # Specifies how symbols should be exported, see
  <a href="#static_library_gui_app"><b>gui_app</b></a>                      : [[#@bool]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          # When set to true flags this target as a GUI application
  <a href="#static_library_implicit_include_directories"><b>implicit_include_directories</b></a> : [[#@bool]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          # Controls whether Meson adds the current source and build directories to the include path
  <a href="#static_library_include_directories"><b>include_directories</b></a>          : [[#@array]][[[#@inc]] | [[#@str]]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              # one or more objects created with the [[include_directories]] function, or
  <a href="#static_library_install"><b>install</b></a>                      : [[#@bool]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          # When set to true, all outputs of this build_target will be installed
  <a href="#static_library_install_dir"><b>install_dir</b></a>                  : [[#@array]][[[#@str]] | [[#@bool]]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             # override install directory for outputs of this build target
  <a href="#static_library_install_mode"><b>install_mode</b></a>                 : [[#@array]][[[#@str]] | [[#@int]]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              # Specify the file mode in symbolic format
  <a href="#static_library_install_rpath"><b>install_rpath</b></a>                : [[#@str]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           # A string to set the target's rpath to after install
  <a href="#static_library_install_tag"><b>install_tag</b></a>                  : [[#@str]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           # A string used by the `meson install --tags` command
  <a href="#static_library_install_vala_gir"><b>install_vala_gir</b></a>             : [[#@str]] | [[#@bool]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    # If set to a string value, the vala generated gir library will be
  <a href="#static_library_install_vala_header"><b>install_vala_header</b></a>          : [[#@str]] | [[#@bool]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    # If set to a string value, the vala generated header will be installed to
  <a href="#static_library_install_vala_vapi"><b>install_vala_vapi</b></a>            : [[#@str]] | [[#@bool]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    # If set to a string value, the vala generated vapi library will be
  <a href="#static_library_link_args"><b>link_args</b></a>                    : [[#@array]][[[#@str]]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    # Flags to use during linking
  <a href="#static_library_link_depends"><b>link_depends</b></a>                 : [[#@str]] | [[#@file]] | [[#@custom_tgt]] | [[#@custom_idx]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          # Strings, files, or custom targets the link step depends on
  <a href="#static_library_link_early_args"><b>link_early_args</b></a>              : [[#@array]][[[#@str]]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    # Flags to use during linking
  <a href="#static_library_link_language"><b>link_language</b></a>                : [[#@str]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           # Makes the linker for this target be for the specified language
  <a href="#static_library_link_whole"><b>link_whole</b></a>                   : [[#@array]][[[#@lib]] | [[#@custom_tgt]] | [[#@custom_idx]]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          # Links all contents of the given static libraries whether they are used or
  <a href="#static_library_link_with"><b>link_with</b></a>                    : [[#@array]][[[#@lib]] | [[#@custom_tgt]] | [[#@custom_idx]]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          # One or more shared or static libraries
  <a href="#static_library_name_prefix"><b>name_prefix</b></a>                  : [[#@str]] | [[#@array]][[[#@void]]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             # The string that will be used as the prefix for the
  <a href="#static_library_name_suffix"><b>name_suffix</b></a>                  : [[#@str]] | [[#@array]][[[#@void]]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             # The string that will be used as the extension for the
  <a href="#static_library_native"><b>native</b></a>                       : [[#@bool]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          # Controls whether the target is compiled for the build or host machines
  <a href="#static_library_objects"><b>objects</b></a>                      : [[#@array]][[[#@extracted_obj]] | [[#@file]] | [[#@str]]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             # List of object files that should be linked in this target
  <a href="#static_library_override_options"><b>override_options</b></a>             : [[#@array]][[[#@str]]] | [[#@dict]][[[#@str]] | [[#@bool]] | [[#@int]] | [[#@array]][[[#@str]]]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                # takes an array of strings in the same format as `project`'s `default_options`
  <a href="#static_library_pic"><b>pic</b></a>                          : [[#@bool]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          # Builds the library as positional independent code
  <a href="#static_library_prelink"><b>prelink</b></a>                      : [[#@bool]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          # If `true` the object files in the target will be prelinked,
  <a href="#static_library_rust_abi"><b>rust_abi</b></a>                     : [[#@str]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           # Set the specific ABI to compile (when compiling rust)
  <a href="#static_library_rust_crate_type"><b>rust_crate_type</b></a>              : [[#@str]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           # Set the specific type of rust crate to compile (when compiling rust)
  <a href="#static_library_rust_dependency_map"><b>rust_dependency_map</b></a>          : [[#@dict]][[[#@str]]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     # On rust targets this allows giving custom names to the crates that are
  <a href="#static_library_sources"><b>sources</b></a>                      : [[#@str]] | [[#@file]] | [[#@custom_tgt]] | [[#@custom_idx]] | [[#@generated_list]] | [[#@structured_src]]                                                                                                                                                                                                        # Additional source files
  <a href="#static_library_vala_gir"><b>vala_gir</b></a>                     : [[#@str]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           # If set, generates a GIR file with the given name
  <a href="#static_library_vala_header"><b>vala_header</b></a>                  : [[#@str]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           # On Vala targets, this provides a way to override the name of the generated
  <a href="#static_library_vala_vapi"><b>vala_vapi</b></a>                    : [[#@str]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           # On Vala targets, this provides a way to override the name of the generated
  <a href="#static_library_win_subsystem"><b>win_subsystem</b></a>                : [[#@str]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           # Specifies the subsystem type to use
)</code></pre>




  <p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- A bit more space -->

  ### Arguments


  The function `static_library()` accepts the following positional arguments:

  <table class="table table-bordered table-sm">
    <thead>
      <tr>
        <th class="text-center text-nowrap">Name</th>
        <th class="text-center text-nowrap">Type</th>
        <th>Description</th>
      </tr>
    </thead>
    <tbody>
      <tr id="static_library_target_name">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">target_name</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  The *unique* name of the build target
  </td>
      </tr>
    </tbody>
  </table>

  <p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- Extra space -->

  Additionally, the
  function accepts between `0` and `infinity` variadic
  arguments (`source...`) of type <code><span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@file]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@custom_tgt]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@custom_idx]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@generated_list]]</span></code>.

  Input source to compile. The following types are supported:

- Strings relative to the current source directory
- [[@file]] objects defined in any preceding build file
- The return value of configure-time generators such as [[configure_file]]
- The return value of build-time generators such as
  [[custom_target]] or [[generator.process]]

These input files can be sources, objects, libraries, or any other
file. Meson will automatically categorize them based on the extension
and use them accordingly. For instance, sources (`.c`, `.cpp`,
`.vala`, `.rs`, etc) will be compiled and objects (`.o`, `.obj`) and
libraries (`.so`, `.dll`, etc) will be linked.

With the Ninja backend, Meson will create a build-time [order-only
dependency](https://ninja-build.org/manual.html#ref_dependencies) on
all generated input files, including unknown files. This is needed to
bootstrap the generation of the real dependencies in the
[depfile](https://ninja-build.org/manual.html#ref_headers) generated
by your compiler to determine when to rebuild sources. Ninja relies on
this dependency file for all input files, generated and non-generated.
The behavior is similar for other backends.


  
  <p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- Extra space -->

  Finally, `static_library()`
  accepts the following keyword arguments:

  <table class="table table-bordered table-sm">
    <thead>
      <tr>
        <th class="text-center text-nowrap">Name</th>
        <th class="text-center text-nowrap">Type</th>
        <th>Description</th>
      </tr>
    </thead>
    <tbody>
      <tr id="static_library__lang__args">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">&lt;lang&gt;_args</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@file]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  compiler flags to use for the given language; eg: `cpp_args` for C++

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="static_library__lang__pch">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">&lt;lang&gt;_pch</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  precompiled header file to use for the given language
  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="static_library_build_by_default">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">build_by_default</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`0.38.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@bool]]</span>
  <br><code class="language-meson text-nowrap">default = true</code>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Causes, when set to `true`, to have this target be built by default.
This means it will be built when `meson compile` is called without any
arguments. The default value is `true` for all built target types.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="static_library_build_rpath">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">build_rpath</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`0.42.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  A string to add to target's rpath definition in the build dir,
but which will be removed on install

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="static_library_build_subdir">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">build_subdir</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`1.10.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Places the build results in a subdirectory of the given name rather than directly into the build directory. This does not affect the install directory, which uses install_dir.
This allows inserting a directory name into the build path, either when needed to use the build result while building other targets or as a way to support multiple targets with the same basename by using unique build_subdir values for each one.
To prevent collisions within the build directory, build_subdir is not allowed to match a file or directory in the source directory, nor contain '..' to refer to the parent of the build directory.
  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="static_library_d_debug">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">d_debug</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  The [D version identifiers](https://dlang.org/spec/version.html#version) to add
during the compilation of D source files.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="static_library_d_import_dirs">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">d_import_dirs</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`0.62.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@inc]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  the directories to add to the string search path (i.e. `-J` switch for DMD).
Must be objects created with the [[include_directories]] function or plain
strings, which will be implicitly converted into [[@inc]] objects.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="static_library_d_module_versions">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">d_module_versions</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@int]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  List of module version identifiers set when compiling D sources.
  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="static_library_d_unittest">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">d_unittest</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@bool]]</span>
  <br><code class="language-meson text-nowrap">default = false</code>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  When set to true, the D modules are compiled in debug mode.
  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="static_library_dependencies">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">dependencies</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@dep]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  one or more dependency objects
created with
[[dependency]] or [[compiler.find_library]]
(for external deps) or [[declare_dependency]]
(for deps built by the project)

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="static_library_extra_files">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">extra_files</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@file]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Not used for the build itself but are shown as source files in IDEs
that group files by targets (such as Visual Studio).

These may only be static sources.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="static_library_gnu_symbol_visibility">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">gnu_symbol_visibility</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`0.48.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Specifies how symbols should be exported, see
e.g [the GCC Wiki](https://gcc.gnu.org/wiki/Visibility) for more
information. This value can either be an empty string or one of
`default`, `internal`, `hidden`, `protected` or `inlineshidden`, which
is the same as `hidden` but also includes things like C++ implicit
constructors as specified in the GCC manual. Ignored on compilers that
do not support GNU visibility arguments. See also
[`snippets.symbol_visibility_header()`](Snippets-module.md#symbol_visibility_header)
method to help with defining public API.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="static_library_gui_app">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">gui_app</code>
  <br>{meson:deprecated}`0.56.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@bool]]</span>
  <br><code class="language-meson text-nowrap">default = false</code>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  When set to true flags this target as a GUI application
on platforms where this makes a difference, **deprecated** since
0.56.0, use `win_subsystem` instead.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="static_library_implicit_include_directories">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">implicit_include_directories</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`0.42.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@bool]]</span>
  <br><code class="language-meson text-nowrap">default = true</code>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Controls whether Meson adds the current source and build directories to the include path
  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="static_library_include_directories">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">include_directories</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@inc]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  one or more objects created with the [[include_directories]] function, or
*(since 0.50.0)* strings, which will be implicitly convert to [[@inc]]
objects

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="static_library_install">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">install</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@bool]]</span>
  <br><code class="language-meson text-nowrap">default = false</code>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  When set to true, all outputs of this build_target will be installed.
  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="static_library_install_dir">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">install_dir</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@bool]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  override install directory for outputs of this build target. If the value is a
relative path, it will be considered relative the `prefix` option.
For example, if you want to install plugins into a subdir, you'd use
something like this: `install_dir : get_option('libdir') / 'projectname-1.0'`.

This can be set to an array of values to control the the installation path
of build targets with multiple outputs. Currently, that means Vala. Setting
them to `true` means "use the default", and `false` means, don't install.
By default the first element is true, and all other elements are false.

*Since 1.11.0* Passing an array is deprecated, and install_vala_header,
install_vala_vapi, and install_vala_gir keyword arguments should be used
instead.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="static_library_install_mode">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">install_mode</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`0.47.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@int]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Specify the file mode in symbolic format
and optionally the owner/uid and group/gid for the installed files.

See the `install_mode` kwarg of [[install_data]] for more information.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="static_library_install_rpath">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">install_rpath</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  A string to set the target's rpath to after install
(but *not* before that). On Windows, this argument has no effect.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="static_library_install_tag">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">install_tag</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`0.60.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  A string used by the `meson install --tags` command
to install only a subset of the files. By default all build targets have the
tag `runtime` except for static libraries that have the `devel` tag.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="static_library_install_vala_gir">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">install_vala_gir</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`1.11.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@bool]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  If set to a string value, the vala generated gir library will be
installed to that path, if set to `true`, the default gir path will be
used, if `false` the gir library will not be installed.

It is an error for this to be either `true` or a string and `vala_gir` to
be unset.

The default value is `false`.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="static_library_install_vala_header">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">install_vala_header</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`1.11.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@bool]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  If set to a string value, the vala generated header will be installed to
that path, if set to `true`, the default include path will be used, if
`false` the header will not be installed.

The default value is `false`.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="static_library_install_vala_vapi">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">install_vala_vapi</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`1.11.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@bool]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  If set to a string value, the vala generated vapi library will be
installed to that path, if set to `true`, the default vapi path will be
used, if `false` the vapi library will not be installed.

The default value is `false`.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="static_library_link_args">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">link_args</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Flags to use during linking. You can use UNIX-style
flags here for all platforms.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="static_library_link_depends">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">link_depends</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@file]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@custom_tgt]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@custom_idx]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Strings, files, or custom targets the link step depends on
such as a symbol visibility map. The purpose is to
automatically trigger a re-link (but not a re-compile) of the target
when this file changes.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="static_library_link_early_args">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">link_early_args</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Flags to use during linking. These are just like link_args,
except that they are inserted into the linker command line
before any object files or libraries. You can use UNIX-style
flags here for all platforms. This is only supported when using
the Ninja backend.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="static_library_link_language">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">link_language</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`0.51.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Makes the linker for this target be for the specified language.
It is generally unnecessary to set
this, as Meson will detect the right linker to use in most cases. There are
only two cases where this is needed. One, your main function in an
executable is not in the language Meson picked, or second you want to force
a library to use only one ABI.

*(broken until 0.55.0)*

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="static_library_link_whole">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">link_whole</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`0.40.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@lib]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@custom_tgt]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@custom_idx]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Links all contents of the given static libraries whether they are used or
not, equivalent to the `-Wl,--whole-archive` argument flag of GCC, or the
'/WHOLEARCHIVE' MSVC linker option. This allows the linked target to
re-export symbols from all objects in the static libraries.

*(since 0.41.0)* If passed an array that array will be flattened.

*(since 0.51.0)* This argument also accepts outputs produced by
custom targets. The user must ensure that the output is a library in
the correct format.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="static_library_link_with">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">link_with</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@lib]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@custom_tgt]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@custom_idx]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  One or more shared or static libraries
(built by this project) that this target should be linked with. *(since 0.41.0)* If passed an
array that array will be flattened. *(since 0.51.0)* The arguments can also be custom targets.
In this case Meson will assume that merely adding the output file in the linker command
line is sufficient to make linking work. If this is not sufficient,
then the build system writer must write all other steps manually.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="static_library_name_prefix">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">name_prefix</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@void]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  The string that will be used as the prefix for the
target output filename by overriding the default (only used for
libraries). By default this is `lib` on all platforms and compilers,
except for MSVC shared libraries where it is omitted to follow
convention, and Cygwin shared libraries where it is `cyg`.

Set this to `[]`, or omit the keyword argument for the default behaviour.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="static_library_name_suffix">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">name_suffix</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@void]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  The string that will be used as the extension for the
target by overriding the default. By default on Windows this is
`exe` for executables and on other platforms it is omitted.

For shared libraries, the default value
is `dylib` on macOS, `dll` on Windows, and `so` everywhere else.
For static libraries, it is `a` everywhere. By convention MSVC
static libraries use the `lib` suffix, but we use `a` to avoid a
potential name clash with shared libraries which also generate
import libraries with a `lib` suffix.

Set this to `[]`, or omit the keyword argument for the default behaviour.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="static_library_native">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">native</code>
  </td>

        <td>

      <tr id="static_library_objects">

  </td>

- 'rust' (default): Create a "rlib" crate.
- 'c': Create a "staticlib" crate.

  </td>

      <tr id="static_library_rust_crate_type">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">rust_crate_type</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`0.42.0`
  <br>{meson:deprecated}`1.3.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Set the specific type of rust crate to compile (when compiling rust).

If the target is an [[executable]] this defaults to "bin", the only
allowed value.

If it is a [[static_library]] it defaults to "lib", and may be "lib",
"staticlib", or "rlib". If "lib" then Rustc will pick a default, "staticlib"
means a C ABI library, "rlib" means a Rust ABI.

If it is a [[shared_library]] it defaults to "lib", and may be "lib",
"dylib", "cdylib", or "proc-macro". If "lib" then Rustc will pick a
default, "cdylib" means a C ABI library, "dylib" means a Rust ABI, and
"proc-macro" is a special rust procedural macro crate.

"proc-macro" is new in 0.62.0.

*Since 1.3.0* this is deprecated and replaced by "rust_abi" keyword argument.
`proc_macro` crates are now handled by the [`rust.proc_macro()`](Rust-module.md#proc_macro)
method.

  </td>

in the `dependencies` section of a Cargo.toml file, or
`extern crate gtk4 as gtk` inside Rust code.

*Since 1.10.0*, the keys can be either crate names or target names.

## structured_sources()

Create a StructuredSource object, which is opaque and may be passed as a source
to any build_target (including static_library, shared_library, executable,
etc.). This is useful for languages like Rust, which use the filesystem layout
to determine import names. This is only allowed in Rust targets, and cannot be
mixed with non structured inputs.

<p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- A bit of space because we remove the top margin below -->

  <h3 style="margin-top: 0px;">Signature</h3>

  <div class="d-flex flex-column align-items-center gap-1">

{meson:since}`0.62.0`
</div>
</div>

<pre><code class="language-meson"># Create a StructuredSource object, which is opaque and may be passed as a source
[[#@structured_src]] structured_sources(
  [[#@array]][[[#@str]] | [[#@file]] | [[#@custom_tgt]] | [[#@custom_idx]] | [[#@generated_list]]]                                                                                                                                                                      <a href="#structured_sources_root"><b>root</b></a>,           # Sources to put at the root of the generated structure
  [[#@dict]][[[#@str]] | [[#@file]] | [[#@custom_tgt]] | [[#@custom_idx]] | [[#@generated_list]]]                                                                                                                                                                       [<a href="#structured_sources_additional"><b>additional</b></a>],   # Additional sources, where the key is the directory under the root to place
)</code></pre>




  <p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- A bit more space -->

## subdir()

Enters the specified subdirectory and executes the `meson.build` file in it.
Once that is done, it returns and execution continues on the
line following this `subdir()` command. Variables defined in that
`meson.build` file are then available for use in later parts of the
current build file and in all subsequent build files executed with
`subdir()`.

Note that this means that each `meson.build` file in a source tree can
and must only be executed once.

<p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- A bit of space because we remove the top margin below -->
<div style="display: flex;">
  <h3 style="margin-top: 0px;">Signature</h3>
  <div style="flex-grow: 1;"></div>
  <div class="d-flex flex-column align-items-center gap-1">

</div>
</div>

<pre><code class="language-meson"># Enters the specified subdirectory and executes the `meson
[[#@void]] subdir(
  [[#@str]]                                  <a href="#subdir_dir_name"><b>dir_name</b></a>,     # Directory relative to the current `meson

  # Keyword arguments:
  <a href="#subdir_if_found"><b>if_found</b></a> : [[#@array]][[[#@dep]]]                                   # Only enter the subdir if all [[dep.found]] methods return `true`.
)</code></pre>




  <p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- A bit more space -->

  ### Arguments


  The function `subdir()` accepts the following positional arguments:

  <table class="table table-bordered table-sm">
    <thead>
      <tr>
        <th class="text-center text-nowrap">Name</th>
        <th class="text-center text-nowrap">Type</th>
        <th>Description</th>
      </tr>
    </thead>
    <tbody>
      <tr id="subdir_dir_name">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">dir_name</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Directory relative to the current `meson.build` to enter.

Cannot contain `..`

  </td>
      </tr>
    </tbody>
  </table>

  <p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- Extra space -->


  Finally, `subdir()`
  accepts the following keyword arguments:

  <table class="table table-bordered table-sm">
    <thead>

<p style="padding: 7.5px; margin: 0px;"></p>

## subdir_done()

Stops further interpretation of the Meson script file from the point
of the invocation. All steps executed up to this point are valid and
will be executed by Meson. This means that all targets defined before
the call of [[subdir_done]] will be built.

If the current script was called by `subdir`, the execution returns to
the calling directory and continues as if the script had reached the
end. If the current script is the top level script, Meson configures
the project as defined up to this point.

<p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- A bit of space because we remove the top margin below -->
<div style="display: flex;">
  <h3 style="margin-top: 0px;">Signature</h3>
  <div style="flex-grow: 1;"></div>
  <div class="d-flex flex-column align-items-center gap-1">

{meson:since}`0.46.0`
</div>
</div>

<pre><code class="language-meson">[[#@void]] subdir_done()</code></pre>

  <p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- A bit more space -->

  ### Example

```meson
project('example exit', 'cpp')
executable('exe1', 'exe1.cpp')
subdir_done()
executable('exe2', 'exe2.cpp')
```

The executable `exe1` will be built, while the executable `exe2` is not
built.








<p style="padding: 7.5px; margin: 0px;"></p>

---
<p style="padding: 7.5px; margin: 0px;"></p>

## subproject()

Takes the project specified in the positional argument and brings that
in the current build specification by returning a [[@subproject]] object.
Subprojects must always be placed inside
the `subprojects` directory at the top source directory. So for
example a subproject called `foo` must be located in
`${MESON_SOURCE_ROOT}/subprojects/foo`.

- `default_options` *(since 0.37.0)*: an array of default option values
  that override those set in the subproject's `meson.options`
  (like `default_options` in `project`, they only have effect when
  Meson is run for the first time, and command line arguments override
  any default options in build files).
  *(since 0.54.0)*: `default_library` built-in option can also be overridden.
  *(since 1.2.0)*: A dictionary may be passed instead of array.
- `version`: works just like the same as in `dependency`.
  It specifies what version the subproject should be, as an example `>=1.0.1`
- `required` *(since 0.48.0)*: By default, `required` is `true` and
  Meson will abort if the subproject could not be setup. You can set
  this to `false` and then use the `.found()` method on the [[@subproject]]
  object. You may also pass the value of a
  [`feature`](Build-options.md#features) option, same as
  [[dependency]].

Note that you can use the returned [[@subproject]] object
to access any variable in the
subproject. However, if you want to use a dependency object from
inside a subproject, an easier way is to use the `fallback:` keyword
argument to [[dependency]].

[See additional documentation](Subprojects.md).

<p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- A bit of space because we remove the top margin below -->
<div style="display: flex;">
  <h3 style="margin-top: 0px;">Signature</h3>
  <div style="flex-grow: 1;"></div>
  <div class="d-flex flex-column align-items-center gap-1">

</div>
</div>

<pre><code class="language-meson"># Takes the project specified in the positional argument and brings that
[[#@subproject]] subproject(
  [[#@str]]                                  <a href="#subproject_subproject_name"><b>subproject_name</b></a>,     # Name of the subproject

  # Keyword arguments:
  <a href="#subproject_default_options"><b>default_options</b></a> : [[#@array]][[[#@str]]] | [[#@dict]][[[#@str]] | [[#@bool]] | [[#@int]] | [[#@array]][[[#@str]]]]                                                                                                                                                                       # An array of default option values
  <a href="#subproject_required"><b>required</b></a>        : [[#@bool]] | [[#@feature]]                                                                                                                                                                                                       # Works just the same as in [[dependency]].
  <a href="#subproject_version"><b>version</b></a>         : [[#@str]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                  # Works just like the same as in [[dependency]].
)</code></pre>




  <p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- A bit more space -->

  ### Arguments


  The function `subproject()` accepts the following positional arguments:

  <table class="table table-bordered table-sm">
    <thead>
      <tr>
        <th class="text-center text-nowrap">Name</th>
        <th class="text-center text-nowrap">Type</th>
        <th>Description</th>
      </tr>
    </thead>
    <tbody>
      <tr id="subproject_subproject_name">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">subproject_name</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Name of the subproject. The subproject must exist in the `subprojects`
directory (or the directory specified in the `subproject_dir` of
[[project]]) as a directory or wrap file.

  </td>
      </tr>
    </tbody>
  </table>

  <p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- Extra space -->


  Finally, `subproject()`
  accepts the following keyword arguments:

  <table class="table table-bordered table-sm">
    <thead>
      <tr>
        <th class="text-center text-nowrap">Name</th>
        <th class="text-center text-nowrap">Type</th>
        <th>Description</th>
      </tr>
    </thead>
    <tbody>
      <tr id="subproject_default_options">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">default_options</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`0.37.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>] | [[@dict]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@bool]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@int]]</span> | [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>]]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  An array of default option values
that override those set in the subproject's `meson.options`
(like `default_options` in [[project]], they only have effect when

*(since 0.54.0)*: `default_library` built-in option can also be overridden.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="subproject_required">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">

  </td>
        <td class="text-center">

  <br><code class="language-meson text-nowrap">default = true</code>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  Works just the same as in [[dependency]].

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="subproject_version">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">version</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>

It specifies what version the subproject should be, as an example `>=1.0.1`.

  </td>
      </tr>
    </tbody>
  </table>

<p style="padding: 7.5px; margin: 0px;"></p>

## summary()

This function is used to summarize build configuration at the end of the build
process. This function provides a way for projects (and subprojects) to report
this information in a clear way.

The content is a series of key/value pairs grouped into sections. If
the section keyword argument is omitted, those key/value pairs are
implicitly grouped into a section with no title. key/value pairs can
optionally be grouped into a dictionary, but keep in mind that
dictionaries do not guarantee ordering. `key` must be string,
`value` can be:

- an integer, boolean or string
- *since 0.57.0* an external program or a dependency
- *since 0.58.0* a feature option
- an array of those.

Instead of calling summary as `summary(key, value)`, it is also possible to
directly pass a dictionary to the [[summary]] function, as seen in the example
below.

`summary()` can be called multiple times as long as the same
section/key pair doesn't appear twice. All sections will be collected
and printed at the end of the configuration in the same order as they
have been called.

<p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- A bit of space because we remove the top margin below -->
<div style="display: flex;">
  <h3 style="margin-top: 0px;">Signature</h3>
  <div style="flex-grow: 1;"></div>
  <div class="d-flex flex-column align-items-center gap-1">

{meson:since}`0.53.0`
</div>
</div>

<pre><code class="language-meson"># This function is used to summarize build configuration at the end of the build
[[#@void]] summary(
  [[#@str]] | [[#@dict]][[[#@str]] | [[#@bool]] | [[#@int]] | [[#@dep]] | [[#@program]] | [[#@array]][[[#@str]] | [[#@bool]] | [[#@int]] | [[#@dep]] | [[#@program]]]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            <a href="#summary_key_or_dict"><b>key_or_dict</b></a>,     # The name of the new entry, or a dict containing multiple entries
  [[#@str]] | [[#@bool]] | [[#@int]] | [[#@dep]] | [[#@program]] | [[#@array]][[[#@str]] | [[#@bool]] | [[#@int]] | [[#@dep]] | [[#@program]]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      [<a href="#summary_value"><b>value</b></a>],         # The value to print for the `key`

  # Keyword arguments:
  <a href="#summary_bool_yn"><b>bool_yn</b></a>  : [[#@bool]]                                   # Convert bool values to yes and no
  <a href="#summary_list_sep"><b>list_sep</b></a> : [[#@str]]                                    # The separator to use when printing array values in this summary
  <a href="#summary_section"><b>section</b></a>  : [[#@str]]                                    # The section to put this summary information under
)</code></pre>

  <p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- A bit more space -->

  ### Example

Example `meson.build`:
```meson
project('My Project', version : '1.0')
summary({'bindir': get_option('bindir'),
        'libdir': get_option('libdir'),
        'datadir': get_option('datadir'),
        }, section: 'Directories')
summary({'Some boolean': false,
        'Another boolean': true,
        'Some string': 'Hello World',
        'An array': ['string', 1, true],
        }, section: 'Configuration')
```

Output:
```
My Project 1.0

  Directories
    bindir         : bin
    libdir         : lib/x86_64-linux-gnu
    datadir        : share

  Configuration
    Some boolean   : false
    Another boolean: true
    Some string    : Hello World
    An array       : string
                     1
                     true
```




  <p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- A bit more space -->

  ### Arguments

  [Argument flattening](Syntax.md#argument-flattening) is **NOT SUPPORTED** by this function.

  The function `summary()` accepts the following positional arguments:

  <table class="table table-bordered table-sm">
    <thead>
      <tr>
        <th class="text-center text-nowrap">Name</th>
        <th class="text-center text-nowrap">Type</th>
        <th>Description</th>
      </tr>
    </thead>
    <tbody>
      <tr id="summary_key_or_dict">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">key_or_dict</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | [[@dict]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@bool]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@int]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@dep]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@program]]</span> | [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@bool]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@int]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@dep]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@program]]</span>]]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  The name of the new entry, or a dict containing multiple entries.  If a
dict is passed it is equivalent to calling summary() once for each
key-value pair.  Keep in mind that dictionaries do not guarantee
ordering.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="summary_value">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">value</code>
  <br>{meson:optional}` `
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@bool]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@int]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@dep]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@program]]</span> | [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@bool]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@int]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@dep]]</span> | <span class="text-nowrap">[[@program]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  The value to print for the `key`.  Only valid if `key_or_dict` is a str.

  </td>
      </tr>
    </tbody>
  </table>

  <p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- Extra space -->


  Finally, `summary()`
  accepts the following keyword arguments:

  <table class="table table-bordered table-sm">
    <thead>
      <tr>
        <th class="text-center text-nowrap">Name</th>

## test()

Defines a test to run with the test harness. Takes two positional
arguments, the first is the name of the test and the second is the
executable to run. The executable can be an [[@exe]] object returned by
[[executable]] or an [[@program]] object] returned by
[[find_program]].

*(since 0.55.0)* When cross compiling, if an exe_wrapper is needed and
defined the environment variable `MESON_EXE_WRAPPER` will be set to
the string value of that wrapper (implementation detail: using
`mesonlib.join_args`). Test scripts may use this to run cross built
binaries. If your test needs `MESON_EXE_WRAPPER` in cross build
situations it is your responsibility to return code 77 to tell the
harness to report "skip".

By default, environment variable
[`MALLOC_PERTURB_`](http://man7.org/linux/man-pages/man3/mallopt.3.html)
is automatically set by `meson test` to a random value between 1..255.
This can help find memory leaks on configurations using glibc,
including with non-GCC compilers. However, this can have a performance
impact, and may fail a test due to external libraries whose internals
are out of the user's control. To check if this feature is causing an
expected runtime crash, disable the feature by temporarily setting
environment variable `MALLOC_PERTURB_=0`. While it's preferable to
only temporarily disable this check, if a project requires permanent
disabling of this check in meson.build do like:

```meson
nomalloc = environment({'MALLOC_PERTURB_': '0'})

test(..., env: nomalloc, ...)
```

By default, the environment variables `ASAN_OPTIONS`, `UBSAN_OPTIONS`,
and `MSAN_OPTIONS` are set to enable aborting on detected violations and to
give a backtrace. To suppress this, `ASAN_OPTIONS`, `UBSAN_OPTIONS`, or
`MSAN_OPTIONS` can be set in the environment.

In addition to running individual executables as test cases, `test()`
can also be used to invoke an external test harness.  In this case,
it is best to use `verbose: true` *(since 0.62.0)* and, if supported
by the external harness, `protocol: 'tap'` *(since 0.50.0)*.  This will
ensure that Meson logs each subtest as it runs, instead of including
the whole log at the end of the run.

Defined tests can be run in a backend-agnostic way by calling
`meson test` inside the build dir, or by using backend-specific
commands, such as `ninja test` or `msbuild RUN_TESTS.vcxproj`.

<p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- A bit of space because we remove the top margin below -->
<div style="display: flex;">
  <h3 style="margin-top: 0px;">Signature</h3>
  <div style="flex-grow: 1;"></div>
  <div class="d-flex flex-column align-items-center gap-1">

</div>
</div>

<pre><code class="language-meson"># Defines a test to run with the test harness
[[#@void]] test(
  [[#@str]]                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        <a href="#test_name"><b>name</b></a>,           # The *unique* test id
  [[#@exe]] | [[#@jar]] | [[#@program]] | [[#@file]] | [[#@custom_tgt]] | [[#@custom_idx]]                                                                                                                                                                                                       <a href="#test_executable"><b>executable</b></a>,     # The program to execute

  # Keyword arguments:
  <a href="#test_args"><b>args</b></a>              : [[#@array]][[[#@str]] | [[#@file]] | [[#@tgt]] | [[#@program]]]                                                                                                                                                         # Arguments to pass to the executable
  <a href="#test_depends"><b>depends</b></a>           : [[#@array]][[[#@build_tgt]] | [[#@custom_tgt]] | [[#@custom_idx]] | [[#@program]]]                                                                                                                                      # specifies that this test depends on the specified
  <a href="#test_env"><b>env</b></a>               : [[#@env]] | [[#@array]][[[#@str]]] | [[#@dict]][[[#@str]]]                                                                                                                                                              # environment variables to set, such as `['NAME1=value1',
  <a href="#test_expected_exitcode"><b>expected_exitcode</b></a> : [[#@int]]                                                                                                                                                                                       # the test is considered passed if the
  <a href="#test_expected_fail"><b>expected_fail</b></a>     : [[#@bool]]                                                                                                                                                                                      # when true the test is considered passed if the
  <a href="#test_is_parallel"><b>is_parallel</b></a>       : [[#@bool]]                                                                                                                                                                                      # when false, specifies that no other test must be
  <a href="#test_priority"><b>priority</b></a>          : [[#@int]]                                                                                                                                                                                       # specifies the priority of a test
  <a href="#test_protocol"><b>protocol</b></a>          : [[#@str]]                                                                                                                                                                                       # specifies how the test results are parsed and can
  <a href="#test_should_fail"><b>should_fail</b></a>       : [[#@bool]]                                                                                                                                                                                      # when true the test is considered passed if the
  <a href="#test_suite"><b>suite</b></a>             : [[#@str]] | [[#@array]][[[#@str]]]                                                                                                                                                                          # `'label'` (or array of labels `['label1', 'label2']`)
  <a href="#test_timeout"><b>timeout</b></a>           : [[#@int]]                                                                                                                                                                                       # the amount of seconds the test is allowed to run, a test
  <a href="#test_verbose"><b>verbose</b></a>           : [[#@bool]]                                                                                                                                                                                      # if true, forces the test results to be logged as if `--verbose` was passed
  <a href="#test_workdir"><b>workdir</b></a>           : [[#@str]]                                                                                                                                                                                       # absolute path that will be used as the working directory
)</code></pre>




  <p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- A bit more space -->

  ### Arguments

  Finally, `test()`
  accepts the following keyword arguments:

  <table class="table table-bordered table-sm">

      </tr>

    <tbody>
      <tr id="test_args">

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="test_depends">

  </td>
        <td class="text-center">

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="test_expected_exitcode">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">

  </td>
      </tr>

  </td>
        <td class="text-center">

  <br><code class="language-meson text-nowrap">default = false</code>
  </td>
        <td>

  </td>

      <tr id="test_priority">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">

  <br>{meson:since}`0.52.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">

  <br><code class="language-meson text-nowrap">default = 0</code>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  specifies the priority of a test. Tests with a
higher priority are *started* before tests with a lower priority.
The starting order of tests with identical priorities is
implementation-defined. The default priority is 0, negative numbers are
permitted.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="test_protocol">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">protocol</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`0.50.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  <br><code class="language-meson text-nowrap">default = 'exitcode'</code>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  specifies how the test results are parsed and can
be one of `exitcode`, `tap`, or `gtest`. For more information about test
harness protocol read [Unit Tests](Unit-tests.md). The following values are
accepted:

- `exitcode`: the executable's exit code is used by the test harness
  to record the outcome of the test).
- `tap`: [Test Anything Protocol](https://www.testanything.org/).
- `gtest` *(since 0.55.0)*: for Google Tests.
- `rust` *(since 0.56.0)*: for native rust tests

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="test_should_fail">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">should_fail</code>
  <br>{meson:deprecated}`1.11.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@bool]]</span>
  <br><code class="language-meson text-nowrap">default = false</code>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  when true the test is considered passed if the
executable returns a non-zero return value (i.e. reports an error)

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="test_suite">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">suite</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span> | [[@array]][<span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>]
  </td>
        <td>
  
  `'label'` (or array of labels `['label1', 'label2']`)
attached to this test. The suite name is qualified by a (sub)project
name resulting in `(sub)project_name:label`. In the case of an array
of strings, the suite names will be `(sub)project_name:label1`,
`(sub)project_name:label2`, etc.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="test_timeout">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">timeout</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@int]]</span>
  <br><code class="language-meson text-nowrap">default = 30</code>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  the amount of seconds the test is allowed to run, a test
that exceeds its time limit is always considered failed, defaults to
30 seconds. *Since 0.57* if timeout is `<= 0` the test has infinite duration,
in previous versions of Meson the test would fail with a timeout immediately.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="test_verbose">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">verbose</code>
  <br>{meson:since}`0.62.0`
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@bool]]</span>
  <br><code class="language-meson text-nowrap">default = false</code>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  if true, forces the test results to be logged as if `--verbose` was passed
to `meson test`.

  </td>
      </tr>
      <tr id="test_workdir">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">workdir</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  absolute path that will be used as the working directory
for the test

  </td>
      </tr>
    </tbody>
  </table>

<p style="padding: 7.5px; margin: 0px;"></p>

## unset_variable()

Unsets a variable.
Referencing a variable which has been unset is an error until it has been set again.

<p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- A bit of space because we remove the top margin below -->
<div style="display: flex;">
  <h3 style="margin-top: 0px;">Signature</h3>
  <div style="flex-grow: 1;"></div>
  <div class="d-flex flex-column align-items-center gap-1">

{meson:since}`0.60.0`
</div>
</div>

<pre><code class="language-meson"># Unsets a variable
[[#@void]] unset_variable(
  [[#@str]]                                  <a href="#unset_variable_varname"><b>varname</b></a>,     # The variable to unset
)</code></pre>




  <p style="padding: 5px; margin: 0px;"></p> <!-- A bit more space -->

  ### Arguments


  The function `unset_variable()` accepts the following positional arguments:

  <table class="table table-bordered table-sm">
    <thead>
      <tr>
        <th class="text-center text-nowrap">Name</th>
        <th class="text-center text-nowrap">Type</th>
        <th>Description</th>
      </tr>
    </thead>
    <tbody>
      <tr id="unset_variable_varname">
        <td class="text-center text-nowrap">
  
  <code class="language-meson">varname</code>
  </td>
        <td class="text-center">
  
  <span class="text-nowrap">[[@str]]</span>
  </td>
        <td>
  
  The variable to unset.
  </td>
      </tr>
